(3) Share the FIR design with the Farfisa stage (`gain_fir.rs`). (4) Flip no default.
- **Why:** The rule is bit exact over 297 replayed writes of 9 sequences; the chord 60 64 67 is +1.1 to +2.5 dB per oracle line (not within 1 dB); the key tables are the `888888888` key-down and the real default program writes smaller window steps; the B3 clip of the presence gap plays the B3 model, so no hardware clip of a B3 Bass program exists to compare.
- **Alternatives:** Find a closed form of `T` first (rejected: five fit families leave 0.3 to 1.3 % residual, the table is exact); flip the Bass default on the chord check (rejected: 2.5 dB at slot 48 and no clip); wait for the bit-exact `0x122296` model (rejected: the opt-in stage is useful now and does not touch defaults).
- **Impact:** `render_hash` unchanged (flag default off, no code path changes when off). The Farfisa FIR design moves to `gain_fir.rs` with identical numerics. Open: drawbar and percussion dependence of the key tables, bit-exact processor, closed form of `T`, `G` resync after key-up, a B3 Bass clip.

### D71. The B3 Bass stage runs the decoded firmware routines at the DSP rate; opt-in and offline

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/organ-bass-recursion`; evidence [organ-farfisa-bass](research/organ-farfisa-bass.md) s15)
- **Context:** D41 wired a B3 Bass stage with a magnitude-only 511 tap FIR per window (EMPIRICAL). The processor is linear but not time invariant (the scanner taps move every sample), so no FIR can match it: the chord 60 64 67 read +1.1 to +2.5 dB. The generator `0x121BBE` and processor `0x122296` run bit exact in the Python VM over thousands of calls (s13, s14).
- **Decision:** (1) Decode both listings once (`tools/sharc_listing_rust.py`, same rules as the Python VM) into integer rows (`organ/b3_bass_fw/prog_*.rs`, GENERATED by `tools/organ_bass_fw_gen.py`, `--check`) and run them in a small Rust machine with a 40 bit float executor. (2) The stage feeds the machine the key-down state of the held keys (windows, generator weights, `G` from the verified tables; voice records from a per key capture table) and resamples the two output samples per call from the DSP frame rate to the host rate. (3) The FIR path of the Bass stage is removed; `--b3-bass-processor on` stays opt-in and is for offline rendering.
- **Why:** The machine is bit exact against `sharc-chain` over 48 recorded calls and the stage reads 0.004 dB worst line error on all 46 lines of the six oracle chords (1, 3 and 10 keys, three press orders) against +1.1 to +2.5 dB for the FIR (D22 line bar: <= 1 dB). Single precision was not used because the 40 bit rules are needed for bit exactness (1 ulp drift after about 70 instructions, s13).
- **Alternatives:** Hand written recursion per section (rejected: the part after the lane fold is a software pipelined sequence with PEy helpers, a copy of the listing is the safe form); a time varying FIR bank (rejected: needs the scanner phase anyway); flip the Bass default (rejected: no hardware clip of a B3 Bass program, drawbar and percussion dependent key tables are not captured, the key-up `G` resync is replayed, D22).
- **Impact:** `render_hash` unchanged (flag default off). The stage costs about 3.6 s of CPU per second of audio in a release build, so it is not real time (TECH_DEBT TD-15). The old FIR design (`gain_fir.rs`) stays for the Farfisa stage. Open: drawbar and percussion key tables, key-up capture, a faster path, the Farfisa processor.

### D74. The Farfisa stage runs the decoded processor `0x122B85`; the firmware stages are not real time yet

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/organ-farfisa-recursion`; evidence [organ-farfisa-bass](research/organ-farfisa-bass.md) s16 / s17)
- **Context:** The Farfisa key stage (s9) used two measured gain FIRs, EMPIRICAL within 2.9 dB. D71 ran the Bass routines on a row machine; the Farfisa routine needs a conditional jump, a jump and the ALU flags.
- **Decision:** (1) The listing model and the row machine get forward jumps (`if ne` / `if eq` on the PEx `AZ`) and AZ / AN from the ALU (not from the multiplier or shifter). (2) `0x122B85` runs on the machine (`organ/farfisa_fw/`) with the key-down words of the ARM and the divider ring of `FarfisaWheels` at the DSP rate; `farfisa_keys.rs` is that stage (`OrganLayer::set_farfisa_processor`), the FIR code and `gain_fir.rs` are removed (`farfisa_proc_gain` stays as public data). (3) The machine DM is one flat window (`0x95000 + 0x30000`) and the 40 bit add / multiply have a 64 bit path for normal operands. Opt-in stays (D22).
- **Why:** Bit exact against `sharc-chain` over 24 + 12 calls (both lattice tables); the stage reads at most 0.0039 dB off the 48 strongest lines of four oracle chords (FIR: 2.9 dB).
- **Alternatives:** Keep the FIRs (rejected: 2.9 dB); run the Farfisa generator listing too (not done: needs `float` clip, `max`, `fix`, register loop counts; the ring of `FarfisaWheels` is within 2e-4); straight line code from the rows (tried, `rustc` did not finish, s17).
- **Impact:** `render_hash` unchanged (flag off). The stage costs about 2 s of CPU per second of audio (Bass 3.3 to 3.6), so offline only (TECH_DEBT TD-15). No hardware clip of Farfisa or B3 Bass exists, so no default flips.

### D48. The master level word and the layer word targets are found; the organ chain word is 1.6; the level defaults stay

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/master-level-chain`; evidence [piano-receiver-scale](research/piano-receiver-scale.md) s5, `tools/arm_master_level.py`)
- **Context:** D39 left the writer of the FX master level word `DM(0xB6518)` (0 in the image), the chip behind the `0xC023195C` messages and the 17.5 dB gap between the organ `K = 2.8` and the piano peaks open (OQ20, OQ24).
- **Decision:** Record that `DM(0xB6518)` is channel-table record 2 of `0xC0549E28` (FX core 1, byte address `0x282D9460`), written by two ARM senders that share the mixer object `[0xC055D720]`: `0xC01897A4` stores `(v / 127)^2` and `0xC010BE18` a 128-entry knob taper (1.0 at 127); the word is `(volume / 127)^2 * taper` = 1.0 at the defaults (`piano::downstream::master_level_word`). Record that the layer level sender `0xC023195C` writes `base + 1` / `base + 2` of the rows of table `0xC0547C58`: PNO core 1 organ chain gains (`0xB2ECF`, `0xB2EDE`, ...) and FX core 1 / 2 bus gains (`DM(0xC2780 + 15 i)`), and that the organ chain word is **1.6** at layer level 127 (scale cell `[mixer + 0x150]`), not the image default 1.0 of the oracle states. No engine default changes.
- **Why:** The writer and the chip are verified by running the firmware senders in the settled emulator; the organ `K` is still not derivable (13.4 dB remain after the 1.6 word, the FX bus scale cells depend on a layer mode flag that is 0 in the settled state), and no hardware clip was re-run, so D22 gives no basis to flip `firmware_bus_scale`, `firmware_layer_law`, `piano_firmware_level` or the organ `K`.
- **Alternatives:** Apply the 1.6 word to the engine organ gain and lower `K` to 1.75 (rejected: the two numbers are not independent of the unresolved FX bus gains, and the clips were not re-run); flip the piano scale to the receiver reading 0.7464 (rejected for the same reason, the organ side disagrees by 13.4 dB).
- **Impact:** None on rendering. OQ20 and OQ24 narrow (writer and chips resolved; open: the layer mode flag and the FX bus scale cells, the source of sender A, stream edges, damper strokes). The ASSUMED master level 1.0 of D39 is the firmware default; the ASSUMED organ chain word 1.0 of the oracle states 1.6.

### D49. The layer mode flag is Reverb GLOBAL; the verified chain is exposed opt-in; the level defaults stay

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/organ-k-derivation`; evidence [piano-receiver-scale](research/piano-receiver-scale.md) s6, `tools/arm_master_level.py modes`)
- **Context:** D48 left the layer mode flag `[mixer + scene + 0x5B8]` and the FX bus scale cells (2.5, 3.25, 0.25) open as candidates for the 13.4 dB between the organ `K = 2.8` and the piano reading, plus the source of the volume sender.
- **Decision:** (1) Record the flag as the Reverb GLOBAL state (`0xC010E36C`, edit id `0x1B9`) and the sender's slot map: with Reverb GLOBAL off organ 1.6 (PNO chain words), piano 1.0 (SMP stream rows), synth 1.0; with it on piano 2.5 and synth 3.25; cells 1.3, 1.14, 0.25 are not reachable from layer slots (`layer_scale_cell`). (2) Add the verified chain as opt-in: `--organ-level chain` (`OutputLevel::Chain`, `1.6 * 0.3732 = 0.597`) and `--firmware-piano-chain` (`PianoLayer::receiver_chain_scale`, `4 G = 0.7464`). (3) Keep every default, `K = 2.8` and `render_hash`.
- **Why:** The cells cannot close the gap (they apply only with a global reverb). The clips under the chain are 14 to 15 dB under (B3, Vox) and 10 to 12 dB over (Velvet Grand) on the same video, so the chain is not verified end to end (D22).
- **Alternatives:** Lower `K` to 1.75 and apply the 1.6 word (rejected: the same 25 dB piano / organ spread remains and the clips decide the sum, not the words); flip the piano to 0.7464 (rejected: +10 dB on the Velvet Grand clip).
- **Impact:** Two new opt-in switches (CLI, `FirmwareOptions`, GUI Firmware bar); defaults, GUI defaults and `render_hash` unchanged. OQ20 and OQ24 narrow: the flag and cells are resolved; open are the 25 dB engine spread, the rows `0..4` and `0xB2FF0` family writers, and White Grand / DSoundman clips under the chain.

### D50. FX Mod 1 RM, A-WAH, WAH and PUMP use the firmware routines behind `FirmwareFx::mod1`; boot words complete PHAS 1 and SPIN; CHOR delays are rescaled

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/fx-mod1-rest`; evidence [fx-mod-types](research/fx-mod-types.md) s7, [fx-mod2-types](research/fx-mod2-types.md) s8.3)
- **Context:** After D26 only A-PAN and TREM (70 of 89 Mod 1 programs) ran the firmware; RM, A-WAH, WAH and PUMP had routines named but no model, and PHAS 1, ENS and SPIN were ported without a stage because instance words were missing. CHOR kept its delays in DSP frames.
- **Decision:** (1) Model RM `0x1239D0`, A-WAH `0x123E9B`, WAH `0x123DD2` and PUMP `0x122D2C` with the committed listing to model to Rust pipeline (500/500 twice and per-instruction traces for each; new ops: `ashift`, `(uui)` multiply, integer `comp`, `gt` / `lt` / `le`), and wire them through `FwMod1Rest` (`fx/fw_mod1_rest.rs`) behind `FirmwareFx::mod1`: row, type words and the 11 sample laws as derived data (`tools/fx_mod1_laws.py`, `fw_mod1_data.rs`, `fx-mod1-rows.csv`), 2-frame latency, phase increments by `F / fs`. (2) Take the instance rows of PHAS 1, ENS and SPIN from the image under the **boot words** of the ARM (`tools/arm_bulk_tables.py capture`), wire PHAS 1 and SPIN, leave ENS on the engine (right output only). (3) Rescale the six CHOR delay and swing words by `fs / F`, capped at 250 frames.
- **Why:** The routines reproduce the oracle word for word; the boot log is the ARM's own word list, so the pointers, block counts and gains are not guessed. ENS would be wrong on the left channel.
- **Alternatives:** Hand-written ports of the four routines (rejected: D37 chose generated ports); wiring ENS with an assumed second voice (rejected); rescaling the ring size as well (rejected: the ring is a context word set by the ARM).
- **Impact:** `FirmwareFx::ALL` changes the 20 Mod 1 programs of RM, A-WAH, WAH, PUMP and the PHAS 1 and SPIN programs (2 to 4 frames of latency); `DEFAULT` and `render_hash` are unchanged. OQ21 keeps: ENS left voice, hardware clips, the 2-frame versus 4-frame call of the A-PAN / TREM stages on the piano unit, the PUMP Master Clock counter.
Ladder24 filter modelled wired opt-in strip chain

`feat/synth-stages-rest-9`; [synth-stage-models-9](research/synth-stage-models-9.md))
`kFltType = 2` (`Ladder24`, 68 442 filter layers) maps `0x12549D`, last filter model; approximated it four-pole low pass `Lp4` (ASSUMED).
Port (`synth/fw_ladder.rs`, `FwFilterType::Ladder`) select it `Ladder24` strip chain when `strip_stages` (`--synth-stages`) on; `FilterEngine::Firmware` strip chain keeps `Lp4`. panel cutoff reaches table position whose section pole equals `exp(-2 atan R)`.
equals (1840 1840), verified; strip position law (the reads log-frequency position, other tangent coefficient `R`), hence ASSUMED opt-in per D22.
Keep `Lp4` `Ladder24` different shape resonance); feed `R` straight position table runs 8 Hz 28 kHz over `0..0.95`, 6 kHz play near 400 Hz); make default outside `--synth-stages` (rejected until cutoff 2 traced).
Ladder24 layers change sound strip chain oscillators (the default `SynthLayer::new`, off `legacy_all`); test `strip_filter_matches_the_firmware_engine_when_the_cutoff_is_in_range` now pins `Lp24`. OQ22 narrows.

### D52. The Ladder24 filter stage is modelled and wired opt-in under the strip chain

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/synth-stages-rest-9`; evidence [synth-stage-models-9](research/synth-stage-models-9.md))
- **Context:** `kFltType = 2` (`Ladder24`, 68 of 442 filter layers) maps to `0x12549D`, the last filter stage without a model; the Rust engine approximated it with the four-pole low pass `Lp4` (ASSUMED).
- **Decision:** Port the stage (`synth/fw_ladder.rs`, `FwFilterType::Ladder`) and select it for `Ladder24` in the strip chain when `strip_stages` (`--synth-stages`) is on; `FilterEngine::Firmware` without the strip chain keeps `Lp4`. The panel cutoff reaches the stage as the table position whose section pole equals `exp(-2 atan R)`.
- **Why:** The stage equals the oracle word for word (1840 of 1840), so the DSP is verified; only the strip word to position law is not (the stage reads a log-frequency position, the other stages the tangent coefficient `R`), hence ASSUMED and opt-in per D22.
- **Alternatives:** Keep `Lp4` for `Ladder24` (rejected: different shape and resonance); feed `R` straight to the position word (rejected: the table runs 8 Hz to 28 kHz over `0..0.95`, so 6 kHz would play near 400 Hz); make the stage the default outside `--synth-stages` (rejected until the ARM cutoff word for type 2 is traced).
- **Impact:** Ladder24 layers change sound under the firmware strip chain with stage oscillators (the default of `SynthLayer::new`, off in `legacy_all`); the test `strip_filter_matches_the_firmware_engine_when_the_cutoff_is_in_range` now pins `Lp24`. OQ22 narrows.
D49. layer mode flag Reverb GLOBAL; verified exposed opt-in; level defaults stay

`feat/organ-k-derivation`; [piano-receiver-scale](research/piano-receiver-scale.md) s6, `tools/arm_master_level.py modes`)
D48 left layer mode flag `[mixer + scene + 0x5B8]` FX bus scale cells (2.5, 3.25, 0.25) open candidates 13.4 dB between organ `K 2.8` piano reading, plus source volume sender.
(1) Record flag Reverb GLOBAL state (`0xC010E36C`, edit id `0x1B9`) sender's slot map: Reverb GLOBAL organ 1.6 (PNO words), piano 1.0 (SMP stream rows), synth 1.0; on piano 2.5 synth 3.25; cells 1.3, 1.14, 0.25 are reachable from layer slots (`layer_scale_cell`). (2) Add verified opt-in: `--organ-level chain` (`OutputLevel::Chain`, `1.6 * 0.3732 0.597`) `--firmware-piano-chain` (`PianoLayer::receiver_chain_scale`, `4 G 0.7464`). (3) every default, `K 2.8` `render_hash`.
cells cannot close gap (they apply global reverb). clips are 14 15 dB (B3, Vox) 10 12 dB (Velvet Grand) on same video, verified end end (D22).
Lower `K` 1.75 apply 1.6 same 25 dB piano / organ spread remains clips decide sum, words); flip piano 0.7464 +10 dB on Velvet Grand clip).
Two new switches (CLI, `FirmwareOptions`, GUI Firmware bar); defaults, GUI defaults `render_hash` unchanged. OQ20 OQ24 narrow: flag cells are resolved; open are 25 dB spread, rows `0..4` `0xB2FF0` family writers, White Grand / DSoundman clips chain.

### D53. SHAPE waves 3 and 4 are modelled and wired opt-in; SUPER 2..4 waits for its wavetable

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/synth-stages-rest-11`; evidence [synth-stage-models-11](research/synth-stage-models-11.md))
- **Context:** Ten ANALOG layers had no stage model: SHAPE 3 (`0x120F55`), SHAPE 4 (`0x12145E`) and SUPER 2..4 (`0x124985`, 8 layers).
- **Decision:** Port SHAPE 3 and 4 (`synth/fw_shape34.rs`, `FwStage::Shape3 / Shape4`) behind `--synth-stages`; do not port `0x124985` until its wavetable source is known.
- **Why:** SHAPE 3 and 4 equal the oracle word for word (320 of 320 each). `0x124985` reads a wavetable at `0x08030000` and a PM table at `0x9631F` that the oracle only has from the ARM boot log, and the boot log does not carry the wavetable, so a port would play silence or an invented table.
- **Alternatives:** Port `0x124985` with a synthetic band-limited table (rejected: it would be a sound design guess presented as a firmware stage); commit a poked random table (rejected: no musical content).
- **Impact:** SHAPE waves 3 and 4 change sound under the firmware strip chain with stage oscillators; defaults and `render_hash` of the legacy chain are unchanged. ANALOG coverage 304 of 312; OQ22 narrows.

### D54. The Ladder24 stage position is the strip table value `R`; the host keeps the pole-frequency law

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/synth-stages-rest-10`; evidence [synth-stage-models-10](research/synth-stage-models-10.md) s1)
- **Context:** D52 left the strip word to position law of `0x12549D` ASSUMED because the ARM cutoff word for `kFltType = 2` was not traced.
- **Decision:** Record that the stage reads the same `R` as every filter stage (`LadderCoefs::from_strip_r`) and that the ARM cutoff sender `0xC01C56CC` is type independent with the knob law `i = knob - 52` (Q16.16 values, `tools/arm_synth_freq_law.py`). Keep `LadderCoefs::from_r` (host Hz = pole frequency) in the voice chain.
- **Why:** The coefficient words equal the oracle for 14 of 14 lane sets with the real pass control block; the pole frequency is exponential in the knob (74 Hz to 7.8 kHz), which is what a Hz fed engine expects, while feeding the tangent `R` of a host cutoff would play the ladder two decades low (ten synth tests broke in the experiment).
- **Alternatives:** Feed `R` of the host cutoff into the position (rejected: pole near 20 Hz for 2 kHz); wait for a knob driven strip chain (rejected: the finding is useful now).
- **Impact:** No rendering change (`from_strip_r` is not wired). OQ22 narrows; the `R = tan` reading of the other filter types is flagged for a hardware sweep.

### D55. Edit value cells are Q16.16; the knob laws are captured; the envelope rate index from the knob is opt-in

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/edit-capture-q16`; evidence [synth-knob-laws](research/synth-knob-laws.md))
- **Context:** [synth-stage-models-10](research/synth-stage-models-10.md) s1 found that the `kFltFreq` handler reads a Q16.16 cell, so the first edit capture (plain integers) saw position 0 and called the id value independent. The synth capture, the FX small-range ids and the organ / piano capture had to be audited, and the knob to strip word maps (ASSUMED) re-derived.
- **Decision:** (1) Capture with `tools/arm_edit_knobs.py` (both encodings, every value of ranges up to 128 steps) and commit the derived laws ([data/synth-knob-laws.csv](research/data/synth-knob-laws.csv), [data/fx-small-id-laws.csv](research/data/fx-small-id-laws.csv)). (2) Add `synth/fw_knob_laws.rs` (rate index = knob - 64, cutoff word, resonance / osc control word) with every-knob tests against the CSV. (3) Wire the rate index opt-in as `--synth-knob-laws` (`SynthLayer::knob_laws`, `SynthLayerDecoded::knobs`); defaults stay. (4) Renumber the Ladder24 decisions of the stage branches to D52 / D54 (D50, D51 and D53 were taken by FX Mod 1, the CBIN checksum and SHAPE 3 / 4) and restore their text, which the merge into `main` had garbled.
- **Why:** The knob maps are VERIFIED by run for every knob and they verify the ASSUMED pass length of two SY frames (attack time equals the display at the first knob of each plateau) and show the displayed decay time is `ln 100` time constants; the clip set cannot be re-scored here (the harness examples were removed from the tree), so the D22 bar is not shown for the new path.
- **Alternatives:** Flip the strip chain to knob indices now (rejected: no clip evidence, decay constant 0.217 against the fitted 0.18); wire the cutoff word (rejected: the tangent reading of `R` disagrees with the displayed Hz, needs a hardware sweep); re-capture only the synth ids (rejected: `kCmpResponse`, `kDlyFbkFilt` and `kDlyFbkPingPong` were blind too).
- **Impact:** One new opt-in switch (CLI, `FirmwareOptions`); defaults and `render_hash` unchanged. OQ22 narrows (knob to rate index closed, cutoff reading and P block open), TD-11 notes the opt-in; [fx-contexts](research/fx-contexts.md) s7 corrected for `kCmpResponse` and the delay feedback ids.

### D56. SUPER 2..4 is modelled and wired opt-in; its tables are committed as derived numbers

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/synth-super-wavetable`; evidence [synth-stage-models-12](research/synth-stage-models-12.md); supersedes the SUPER part of D53)
- **Context:** `0x124985` (SUPER 2..4, 8 ANALOG layers) reads a wavetable at `0x08030000`, a PM row table at `0x9631F` and a 4,096-word polyphase FIR table. D53 held the port back because the wavetable looked absent from the boot log.
- **Decision:** Model the stage word for word (`tools/synth_stage_super2.py`, 320 of 320 oracle words) and port it behind `--synth-stages` (`synth/fw_super2.rs`, `FwStage::Super24`). Commit the four tables as numbers in `docs/research/data/synth-super24-tables.txt` (3,072 + 985 + 4,096 + 5 words, SHA-256 and FNV-1a fingerprint tests); the extractor stays local (needs the firmware and the boot capture).
- **Why:** The wavetable is written by the ARM boot copier `0xC01C0B3C` (5,910 words to the L2 window `0x200C0000`, six identical copies of 985 words) from `bank0_CODE` rodata: fixed firmware data, not recorded samples or user data, so it is the same kind of derived table as the other committed firmware tables (parity over purity). Runtime loading from a user file would make the opt-in model silent without the file.
- **Alternatives:** Load the tables at runtime from a user-provided firmware path (rejected for now: needs the firmware at run time and a loader for the boot capture, the 73 kB text file is the smaller risk); a synthetic band-limited table (rejected, D53).
- **Impact:** SUPER waves 2..4 change sound under `--synth-stages`; defaults and `render_hash` unchanged. ANALOG coverage 312 of 312. The sign extension of the short-word samples (the oracle zero-extends) and the P block stay ASSUMED; if the licence review of the 985-word table is negative, the file can be replaced by a runtime loader without touching the model.

### D57. The evaluation harness lives in `crates/nord-tools` as release binaries; the knob-law envelope path is the default

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/restore-harness-tools`; evidence [synth-validation](research/synth-validation.md) "Knob-law re-score", [synth-knob-laws](research/synth-knob-laws.md) s7, [program-coverage](research/program-coverage.md))
- **Context:** `de45f6de` deleted the nine harness examples of `nord-core` (`ag_synth`, `farfisa_ref`, `organ_ref`, `program_sweep`, `render_hash`, `sample_dump`, `sinc_ref`, `smp_voice_ref`, `synth_strip_e2e`; 1,898 lines) to stop `cargo test --workspace` building and running them in debug on Windows (stack overflow in the FX chains). `tools/synth_validate.py`, `tools/program_sweep.py`, the oracle cross-check scripts and about 30 docs depend on them, so the 15-clip validation, the program sweep and the oracle comparisons were broken, and D55 could not show the D22 bar for `--synth-knob-laws`.
- **Decision:** (1) Restore the nine tools unchanged as `[[bin]]` targets (auto-discovered `src/bin/*.rs`) of a new workspace crate `crates/nord-tools` (`publish = false`, depends on `nord-core`); they are not examples. `render_hash` includes `nord-core/tests/support/render_segments.rs` instead of a second copy, so the golden test and the tool cannot drift (whole hash `490e6218...bdd39b`, unchanged); the CSV reader of `smp_voice_ref` moved to the tested library module `csv_cols.rs`. Every file ships unit tests (`tools/check_tests.py` stays at 0 uncovered). All references (`tools/*.py`, `docs/`) now read `cargo build --release -p nord-tools --bins` and `target/release/<name>`. (2) `tools/synth_validate.py summary --baseline <variant>` scores an increment against another variant: mean score better and no clip over `WORSE_DB`; the majority-better rule stays for `legacy` only. (3) Re-scored the 15 clips with and without `--synth-knob-laws` (variant `strip+st+tbl+kl`): mean 4.03 -> 3.96 (hold-outs 4.19 -> 4.09), 3 Part A clips move (`sweep_12db` 3.04 -> 1.97, `filter_bank` 4.37 -> 4.33, `sine_pluck` 6.40 -> 6.52), 12 clips identical, no clip over the bar. The knob-law path is now the default: `FirmwareOptions::default().synth_knob_laws = true` (also `--firmware-all`; `--legacy-all` and `--no-synth-knob-laws` turn it off; GUI: checkbox "Knob envelope laws" in the Synth group, preset copy, tooltip), `SynthLayer::knob_laws` defaults to true. (4) Regenerated [program-coverage](research/program-coverage.md) and [data/program-sweep.json](research/data/program-sweep.json) with the new defaults.
- **Why:** The knob laws are VERIFIED by run for every knob (D55) and the clips now show a net gain without a regression, which is the D22 bar for an increment. A separate crate keeps the tools out of `cargo test --workspace --all-targets` as examples; as `bin` targets they build in debug but only their light unit tests run (no engine render on the test thread), so the Windows stack margin of `nord-core` and `nord-gui` is unchanged.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the tools as `nord-core` examples with `test = false` (rejected: `--all-targets` still builds them in debug and the earlier overflow was found there); move them into `tools/` as Python (rejected: they link the engine); flip with the bar against `legacy` (rejected: the default itself no longer clears that bar on two clips, see the findings below, which is independent of the knob laws).
- **Impact:** The default render of every program with stored knobs changes slightly: sweep of 384 programs, 154 render differently (median level change 0.04 dB, largest +1.84 dB `Daft Lead`, -1.51 dB `ScissorSplit Whl`), no silent or non-finite program, no program newly clipping at 0.1 % or more (one firmware-all program, `Ice Pad`, now clips 2 of 96,000 frames). `render_hash` and the golden hashes are unchanged (their layers are built in code and carry no knobs; no re-baseline). Findings that stay open: against `legacy` the default misses the RMS bar on `sci_fi_80s` (-0.4 -> -10.4 dB) and `sine_pluck` (+2.0 -> +5.3 dB), and `mega_line` (1.87 -> 2.53) and `sci_fi_80s` (0.24 -> 1.39) scores are worse than the table of the D22 flip; the cause was not bisected.

### D62. The WAVE bank is core 2 stage `0x1247B6` with 46 rodata tables read from the user's OS image, opt-in

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/synth-wave-bank`; evidence [synth-stage-models-13](research/synth-stage-models-13.md))
- **Context:** The 14 WAVE layers of 12 factory programs had no data path (OQ22): no SY / SMP message appeared for any `kMode x kOscBank` combination and the engine plays a synthetic 64-table placeholder. D22 forbids committing firmware images, samples and audio; D56 allows derived tables that are fixed firmware data.
- **Decision:** (1) `kOscBank 3` is the WAVE bank (strip type 54, stage `0x1247B6` on PNO / ORG core 2, the two-oscillator sibling of SUPER 2..4), modelled bit exact (`tools/synth_stage_wave.py`, 320 of 320 oracle words, 96 of 96 each for four other tables). (2) The 46 tables are firmware rodata (registry `bank0_CODE@0xC02FD4B0`); the repository commits only the registry census (names, counts, addresses: `docs/research/data/synth-wave-registry.csv`) and the layout rule, never the samples. (3) The Rust port reads the tables from the user's own OS image at run time (`--synth-wave-tables PATH`, `synth/fw_wave_tables.rs`) and is opt-in: `--synth-wave-bank` alone plays the boot table (wave id 0) for every wave. The default stays the 64-table placeholder.
- **Why:** The stage and the table machinery are verified against the oracle, but the P-block ratios and weights, the run-time hand-over of a selected record and the per-layer wave ids are open, so a default flip would rest on ASSUMED constants (D22 bar). Run-time loading keeps the tables out of git (the 46 tables are 605,164 int16 samples, about 1.2 MB).
- **Alternatives:** Commit the 46 tables as derived numbers like D56 (rejected: 1.2 MB of firmware rodata, against the spirit of D22 for anything that large); flip the default with the boot table for all waves (rejected: 45 of 46 waves would play the wrong cycle); treat banks 1 and 2 as WAVE (rejected: they are the FM banks, their stages are different code).
- **Impact:** `SynthLayer::wave_bank` (default off) and two new CLI flags; no audio change by default, `render_hash` and the golden hashes are unchanged. OQ22, TD-08 and the ROADMAP are updated; the FM stages `0x124269` / `0x12453F` stay unmodelled.

D63. The ARM rotor speeds and ramp are shared by the engine rotary and the firmware Leslie; the firmware Leslie stays opt-in

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/leslie-speed-ramp-chain`; evidence [fx-leslie](research/fx-leslie.md) s11)
- **Context:** The engine rotary used textbook speeds (0.80 / 0.67 Hz slow, 6.85 / 5.70 Hz fast) and exponential ramps (tau 0.7 s, 4.2 s), ASSUMED; the firmware Leslie mapped the engine's rates onto the ARM endpoints. The ARM speed endpoints were VERIFIED but the ramp time per update was open (the harness tick was ASSUMED 1 ms), as were `DM(0xC00DC)` and the level hand-over.
- **Decision:** (1) The ARM rotor update is a tick queue entry with period 240 ticks at 24,000 ticks per second (the arp clock law and the timer setup), i.e. **10 ms**, and the ramp is linear (horn +0.06 / -0.06 Hz, rotor +0.01 / -0.0218 Hz per update, horn slow to fast 1.04 s, rotor 5.67 s); the real speeds are the ARM values times `F / 44,100`. `organ/rotor_arm.rs` holds these and both the engine rotary and the firmware processor use them. (2) `DM(0xC00DC)` is loaded with 1 by the PNO and ORG loader streams and never written by the ARM, so the hardware runs the 8-pass counter; it is the restart of a DMA block chain (ASSUMED reading) that the oracle does not model. (3) The element that feeds `0x1256AD` was not found. (4) The firmware Leslie stays opt-in (`--leslie firmware`, ring `advance`), the GUI Firmware bar default and `render_hash` of the firmware path are unchanged.
- **Why:** The speeds and the ramp are VERIFIED by a run of the ARM update and by the code, so they replace ASSUMED constants everywhere. The D22 bar for the flip is not met: B3 presence -5.7 dB (bar 3 dB), RMS 4.2 dB under the engine rotary, one Leslie clip, ring DMA not modelled.
- **Alternatives:** Flip `--leslie firmware` to default because its score (3.42) beats the engine rotary (7.90) (rejected: D22 needs every band within about 3 dB and a second clip); keep the engine's textbook speeds for the non-firmware path (rejected: ASSUMED values against VERIFIED ones, the default render of an organ with the rotary on changes slightly).
- **Impact:** The default render of an organ with the rotary on changes (rotor speeds 0.787 / 0.719 Hz slow, 6.889 / 6.299 Hz fast, linear ramps): the `organ_b3` engine-rotary variant moves 7.55 to 7.90, the default render is unchanged on the clip. `render_hash` re-baselined: segments `engine` (rms 0.211763 to 0.212688, peak 0.995806 to 0.995080) and `organ` (rms 0.196888 to 0.196090) changed, `synth`, `piano` and `fx` are identical; whole value `63923599...50a16` to `b9a1d073...5b846`. OQ21, TD-10, ROADMAP and [organ-engine](specs/organ-engine.md) updated; the CHOR, Mod 1 and Mod 2 decisions are untouched.
### D66. The arpeggiator step is `30 / D` seconds and the Rate dial uses the firmware table

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `research/arp-player`; evidence [synth-arp-patterns](research/synth-arp-patterns.md) section 9, bean ns4-rwhu)
- **Context:** The engine played one arp / gate step per `15 / D` s (a 16th at the displayed tempo `D`) and decoded the stored 7-bit Rate linearly to 30..300 BPM, both `ASSUMED`. The firmware clock moves `2**19` phase units per `60 / R[v]` s with `R = 2 D` and a grid point every `2**18`; the manual (p.36) says a step is a 1/8 note, which is `30 / D` s. The record player that decides between the readings was not found (OQ28).
- **Decision:** (1) A step is `30 / D` s: the player `0xC023B3EC` (the pattern object's clock-listener method) plays one record per grid point and a step is two records (`tools/ns4_arp_player.py`, 0 differences against the model on two grids; booted firmware end to end: 25.0 ms at dial 127, 66.7 ms at dial 105). `SynthLayer::step_seconds` = `arp_rate::step_seconds_at(arp_rate_bpm)`. (2) The stored Rate is the 128-entry firmware table (`D = R / 2`, 16..1200 BPM, `synth/arp_rate.rs`); `SynthLayerDecoded` keeps the raw (`arp_rate_raw`) so every stored value, also the second of a duplicate pair, survives decode + pack. (3) The GUI `ArpRate` edit clamps to 16..1200; the knob and host parameter keep 20..300. (4) The gate fraction of `E` (2/3 of a step on the firmware's boot grid) and the staccato plain arpeggio are not changed here (OQ32, ns4-hwe8).
- **Why:** The player is the only code that turns grid points into notes, and it is verified by replay; the booted firmware reproduces the `30 / D` cadence for Pattern on and off. The table is read from the image and verified by 128 replays of the Glue rate update. Keeping the raw makes the change bit-lossless for stored programs.
- **Alternatives:** Keep `15 / D` and call the manual loose (rejected: the replay contradicts it); store only `arp_bpm` and round-trip through the nearest dial position (rejected: it changes the stored raw of the second value of a duplicate pair, and Master Clock sync reads the subdivision from the same raw, `floor(9 * raw / 128)` differs between 28 and 29); widen the knob to the exponential 16..1200 range now (rejected: not additive for saved host parameters).
- **Impact:** Every Arp, Poly and Gate render at a given tempo is twice as slow as before (the same notes land every `30 / D` s); programs with a stored Rate decode to the hardware tempo instead of 30..300 BPM. `ns4 bench` is unchanged (no scenario runs the arpeggiator). Tests that assumed 3000-sample steps run at 480 BPM; `arpeggiator_steps_every_thirty_over_bpm_seconds` pins 120 BPM = 12000 samples. The research note also records that the ARM tick is 24 kHz, not 1 kHz (OQ22 gets a pointer).

### D60. The CHOR hardware clip keeps `FirmwareFx::mod2` opt-in

> **Update 2026-11-18:** superseded for the default by D163 (owner approval relayed in chat).

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/chorus-hw-clip`; evidence [fx-chorus-hw-clip](research/fx-chorus-hw-clip.md))
- **Context:** The first hardware recording of a Mod 2 effect (Nord Stage 4 chorus against the Kontakt Ultimate Pianos 2 chorus) shows a decaying sawtooth on a DAW oscilloscope with a delayed copy before each edge. D22 asks for modulation metrics with no side worse than about 3 dB before a default flips.
- **Decision:** Measure the edge separation of the clip (`tools/fx_chorus_clip.py scope`) and of the emulator on the same sawtooth (`examples/fx_chorus_probe.rs`, `grid`): LFO 0.336 Hz (type 4 word `v = 30` gives 0.343 Hz), copy delay 0.62 to 1.78 ms (firmware 0.87 to 3.28 ms, engine 4.5 to 13.5 ms), wet over dry 0.86. `FirmwareFx::mod2` stays out of `DEFAULT`; the engine chorus stays the default; `render_hash` and the GUI default are unchanged.
- **Why:** The rate and the one-copy structure agree with type 4, but the delay is off by 1.7 (swing 2.1) if the scope saw is the 87.31 Hz note, the scope signal is not the audio of the clip, the knob positions of the Nord are unknown, and no dry reference or audio comb exists, so the D22 bar cannot be evaluated.
- **Alternatives:** Flip CHOR to default because the firmware is closer than the engine (rejected: the engine is far off, but the firmware miss is not explained and the default would move 93 programs on one clip); rescale the firmware delays by 0.55 (rejected: the scale rests on the unverified scope frequency).
- **Impact:** OQ21 gains the clip result and the questions for the user (Nord knob positions, scope source and frequency); TD-10 records the opt-in; no audio change.

### D59. The default P table gets a separate SUPER wave 1 to 4 row; the 15-clip regressions of PR 292 and PR 319 are refitted

- **Status:** accepted. **Decided by:** the 15-clip bar of D22 (mean score, no clip over 3 dB).
- **Context:** The D57 re-run read the default at mean 4.03 (3.96 with knob laws) where the D22 table of PR 275 said 3.93: `mega_line` 1.87 to 2.53 and `sci_fi_80s` 0.24 to 1.39, and against legacy the default missed the RMS bar on `sci_fi_80s` (-10.4 dB) and `sine_pluck` (+5.3 dB). Bisect over the 55 first-parent merges after PR 275 (build of `ns4` per commit, `tools/synth_validate.py run --only mega_line sci_fi_80s --variants strip+st+tbl`): `mega_line` is exact at 83c6c425 (PR 297, 1.87) and 2.53 at 9d7e4045 (PR 292, `feat/synth-stages-rest-5`: SUPER wave 1 `Super1` became a stage model); `sci_fi_80s` is exact at 54644a52 (PR 321, 0.24) and 1.39 at 3a5143fc (PR 319, `feat/synth-stages-rest-9`: the Ladder24 stage `0x12549D` replaced the older filter of that clip). Neither is a bug: both PRs replaced a path the EMPIRICAL rows had been fitted on (SUPER wave 1 played the legacy saw at the idle row, Ladder24 played a biquad), while the rows (`p_table.rs`) and the type factors stayed.
- **Decision:** (1) New row `PWave::Super1` for the ANALOG SUPER waves 1 to 4 (`Super1`, `Super24` stages), fitted on `mega_line`; the `Super` row keeps wave 0 only. (2) Refit: SUPER cutoff factor 1.07 to 0.45 (the old 1.07 was a compromise between `pad_silk` and the wave 1 clip `mega_line`, which share one row no more), SUPER1 gain 1.0 cutoff 3.2, MULTI gain 0.26 to 0.75, SHAPE SINE row gain 0.5 cutoff 3.0, Ladder24 type factor 1.0 to 0.8 (`type_cutoff_scale` moved to `synth/p_type.rs`, `p_table.rs` back near 400 lines). (3) `render_hash` whole value `490e6218...bdd39b` to `63923599...50a16` (engine and synth segments; organ, piano, fx unchanged).
- **Why:** The grids in [synth-validation](research/synth-validation.md) s9 move the 15-clip mean 3.96 to 3.40 (11 of 15 clips better than legacy, hold-outs 8 of 9, no clip over the bar), `pad_silk` 7.62 to 2.36 (legacy 6.53), `mega_line` 2.53 to 1.69, `sci_fi_80s` 1.39 to 0.86, `sine_pluck` 6.52 to 4.75 (legacy 5.99); RMS bar clean for every Part A clip. The `pad_silk` SUPER conflict of the earlier section 7 was a row sharing problem, not a filter envelope depth (the 2 octave hypothesis is not needed).
- **Alternatives:** Re-derive the Lp12 / Lp24 / BP / HP cutoff from the ARM law `i = knob - 52` instead of fitted type factors (rejected for now: with `R(i) = 0.36083 + 0.0079301 i` the displayed 277 Hz reads 4.8 kHz, [synth-knob-laws](research/synth-knob-laws.md) s6; testing it needs a hardware cutoff sweep, which is still missing; only the Ladder24 type factor of the stage that takes `R` as a table position moved); fit the SUPER and SUPER1 rows jointly on one clip (rejected: the two disagree by 7x in factor, so they stay two rows with two clips each).
- **Impact:** Every program with SUPER 1..4, MULTI, SHAPE SINE or a Ladder24 filter renders differently (the factory sweep rows of `program-sweep.json` document the pre-D59 default and were not regenerated, TD-11). OQ22 and TD-11 updated; `filter_bank` (4.33 against 3.15) stays above legacy.

### D61. The SAMPLES step join is decoded, the zone fields come from the sample map, the voice store is the effective key; `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/samples-pitch-join-loop`; evidence [synth-sample-kernel-rule](research/synth-sample-kernel-rule.md) section 13)
- **Context:** D47 left 2 SAMPLES items open: the DSP routine behind the pitch word, the source of the per-zone key and tune, and the voice store with Octave Shift.
- **Decision:** (1) The join is ISR step 3 of the SY image (`0x1C0679..0x1C06E6`): `step = ratio(e) * 0.05 / word`, modelled by `sinc::pitch_law::dsp_step`. (2) `[zone + 0x60]` is the map zone root key and `[zone + 0x10]` is `trunc(3072 * log2(rate / 43402.34375))` of the stroke rate (`zone_tune_q8`, `zone_word`, bit-exact on nine notes). (3) The voice key is the effective key (Octave Shift and transpose applied), verified for octave +1 and -2. (4) `--sample-pitch fw` and the GUI Firmware bar default (`FirmwareOptions::default`) stay off; golden hashes unchanged.
- **Why:** The step falls as the ARM word rises, so the played key has to reach the DSP through the table entry `e` at `DM(0x9354A + index)`; no ARM message of a note-on or of the boot writes it, so the engine cannot yet be rebuilt on the firmware path and checked end to end (D22 needs that). The key law as it stands (`key_ratio`, at most 0.34 cent from equal temperament) is already below audibility, so flipping now would change renders for no verified gain.
- **Alternatives:** Flip the default with `e = word_law(key) / 0.05` as a hypothesis (rejected: fits nine numbers, but the writer of `e` is unseen); keep `key - root + 60` and ignore the rate (kept as the opt-in, its rate term `sr_zone / sr_engine` differs from the firmware's `rate / 43402.34` only by the output rate, a separate question).
- **Impact:** New constants and functions in `sinc/pitch_law.rs` with tests; a SY oracle scenario `sy_pitch_join.scn`; OQ22, TD-08 and the ROADMAP updated; no render changes.

### D65. The SAMPLES step join is built in the engine on a modelled table entry; `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (PR for `feat/sy-step-entry-writer`; evidence [synth-sample-kernel-rule](research/synth-sample-kernel-rule.md) section 14)
- **Context:** D61 decoded the DSP join `step = ratio(e) * 0.05 / word` and left the writer of the 16-bit table entry `e` at `DM(0x9354A + index)` open; D22 needs the firmware path verified end to end before a default flips.
- **Decision:** (1) `--sample-pitch fw` is rebuilt on `sinc::step_join::output_step` (zone word from `zone_word`, entry from `table_entry_for_key`, `dsp_step`, scaled by the SY frame rate over the engine rate) and checked against the SY oracle for the nine captured notes (`sy_pitch_nine.scn`, within 5e-5). (2) The entry format is a 5-bit exponent, 11-bit mantissa float with denormals at `E = 0` (`unpack_ratio`). (3) The writer of `e` was searched in the DSP listing (no store), by `sharc-ref --watch` over the full note-on replay (no write) and in the ARM port tables and boot messages (no word of `0x93500 .. 0x9359F`, no opcode 1 .. 15 call): not found. (4) The default of `--sample-pitch` and `FirmwareOptions::default` stays off; golden hashes unchanged.
- **Why:** The join arithmetic and the entry format are verified, but the entry value `word_law(key) / 0.05` is a model that reproduces the native step; nothing shows the hardware writes it, so D22 (verified firmware path) is not met. The engine path is still closer to the firmware than `key - root + 60` (zone tune and rate enter as they do on the SY).
- **Alternatives:** Flip the default on the model (rejected: the writer is unseen; the visible change is at most 0.5 %, below the audible threshold, and the renders would change for no verified gain); drop the opt-in rebuild (rejected: it is the test bed for the writer once found).
- **Impact:** New `sinc/step_join.rs` with tests and oracle goldens, `sample_pitch_fw` in `synth/voice_alloc.rs` uses it, `sharc-ref --watch`, scenario `sy_pitch_nine.scn`; the table entry format note in sy-smp-audio; OQ22, TD-08 and the ROADMAP updated; no default render changes.

writes synth oscillator P block; `--synth-p verified` opt-in

- **Status:** accepted. **Decided by:** the evidence of [synth-p-writer](research/synth-p-writer.md) and the 15-clip run of [synth-validation](research/synth-validation.md) s10.
- **Context:** Earlier notes concluded that no path writes the P block (ratios, weights, gain, unison ratios) and that the writer is another core or DMA ([synth-wave-models](research/synth-wave-models.md) s9, [synth-knob-laws](research/synth-knob-laws.md) s5), so every row of `synth/p_table.rs` was fitted to clips (EMPIRICAL).
- **Decision:** (1) Record the ARM writer: the strip apply function `0xC01CE7D8` (type index `[strip + 0x98]`, variants `[strip + 0x9C]`, `[strip + 0xA0]`) calls the table driven writers `0xC01C72CC`, `0xC01C7550`, `0xC01C94CC`, which call 44 one-word senders into `DM(0xC167A + 0x96 slot ..)`. Capture the words of all 74 types with `tools/synth_p_writer.py` into `docs/research/data/synth-p-words.csv` (derived numbers only, fingerprint tests). (2) Add `synth/p_words.rs` and the opt-in `--synth-p verified` (gain column of the single-oscillator rows from the words, one EMPIRICAL level constant `GAIN_PER_P16 = 1.6` calibrated on the saw). (3) Keep the EMPIRICAL table as default.
- **Why:** The words are verified by run and replace three fitted gains (pulse 0.28 against the fitted 0.27, SHAPE and SHAPE SINE 0.4). On the 15 clips the verified table moves two RMS errors from 2.2 / 7.3 dB to 1.2 / 1.3 dB but costs 3.5 dB on `lead_smooth` and 0.75 score on `sweep_12db`, whose cutoff factors were fitted with the old gains: mean 3.40 to 3.45, so it is not an improvement to flip on.
- **Alternatives:** Flip the default to the verified table now (rejected: the D22 bar compares with the current default, which is not beaten, and the cutoff factors need a joint refit); replace the SUPER and MULTI stage ratios and weights at the same time (rejected for this PR: the Rust stage normalisations differ from the firmware lane mix and need per-stage unit conversion and one SUPER clip for the spread scale).
- **Impact:** `--synth-p verified` (CLI, `PSpec::parse`); defaults and `render_hash` unchanged. OQ22 and TD-11 updated; the idle P block (`P[-32] = 0.077`) is an image default, not the playing block.

### D68. The SMP slew loop is modelled and verified; the firmware slew voice is opt-in (`--piano-slew-fw`), no piano level default flips

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-14
- **Decided by:** the D22 bar (no default moves without a hardware clip that improves); evidence [smp-slew](research/smp-slew.md)
- **Context:** OQ20 listed the SMP slew time behaviour, the stream edge handling, damper-down strokes, unison / dynamics / EQ and the level defaults (`firmware_bus_scale`, `firmware_layer_law`, the receiver chain) as open. The audio interrupt runs a SIMD loop (`0x1C06B9`) that turns the float fields `+11..+18`, `-2`, `-1`, `+2`, `+3` of each voice record into the Q31 gains `0 / +1`, and a state machine (`0x1C0A89`) that loops, switches rate and ends a stream.
- **Decision:** (1) Model the loop as `piano/smp_slew.rs` on the 40-bit truncating float of `piano/ext40.rs`, bit exact against the `sharc-ref` oracle (11 of 11 random records, 60 of 60 trajectory updates); it is a library building block and is not wired into the render path (the ARM-side initial values of ten of its fourteen inputs are not decoded). (2) Record the edge rules (loop wrap by `F-7` at `F-8`, rate switch `F+6` / `F+7`, stop at `F-9`, no ring end test, no step clamp) as VERIFIED runs; the engine keeps its edge-sample repeat. (3) Leave `firmware_bus_scale`, `firmware_layer_law`, `receiver_chain_scale`, `piano_firmware_level` and the organ `K` at their current defaults; add the verified-chain variants to `tools/emu_vs_hw.py` for the next clip run.
- **Why:** The slew arithmetic is proven but its inputs are not, so wiring it would add an invented envelope; the level chain evidence is unchanged from D49 (piano +10 to +12 dB, organ -14 to -15 dB under one verified path), so the D22 bar for a default flip is not met and no clip was re-run in this pass.
- **Alternatives:** Wire a slew with the message-list values `F14 = 1`, `F16 = 0.9793` and guessed rates (rejected: ASSUMED parameters, audible change, no clip to judge it); flip `firmware_layer_law` because `(v / 127)^2` is verified (rejected: the word-to-gain path of `0xC023195C` ends in a message to the FX / PNO bus gains, not in these record fields, and the default fader stays linear at 127 either way).
- **Impact:** No audio change, `render_hash` unchanged. OQ20, the ROADMAP and the piano-engine spec point at [smp-slew](research/smp-slew.md); the remaining work is the ARM writers of the slew fields and the clip re-run.
- **Update (PR `feat/smp-slew-wired`):** (4) The slew is wired as the opt-in `PianoLayer::firmware_slew` (`--piano-slew-fw`, GUI Piano group): the ARM note-on / note-off messages replayed through the SMP voice-start handler give the record values (hold gain `0.21875 * level`, 44 ms release, extra voice muted; first 8 updates exact at 4 velocities); the rate word table is EMPIRICAL (Velvet Grand points for every sound). (5) The default stays off (the clip comparison of D72 confirms it): no clip comparison was run in this pass (the D22 bar needs attack, decay and release against White Grand / Velvet Grand); the slew voice would lower the piano level by 13.2 dB against `amp = level`, so the flip decision belongs to the clip run. `render_hash` and all goldens are unchanged. (The number D68 replaces the D62 of the first slew PR, which collided with the D62 entries of the WAVE bank and the synth P block; D66 and D67 are taken on `main`.)

### D72. The piano clips under chain + slew: no piano level default flips; the slew rate follows the key

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15
- **Decided by:** the D22 bar (a default moves only when a verified path improves the hardware clips); evidence [piano-receiver-scale](research/piano-receiver-scale.md) s7, [smp-slew](research/smp-slew.md) s6
- **Context:** D49 and D68 left the piano +10 to +12 dB over the Velvet Grand clip under the verified chain (receiver 0.7464, master word 1.0, layer law, `piano_firmware_level`), and the firmware slew voice (`--piano-slew-fw`, gain `0.21875 * level`, no ramp, hold decay, 44 ms release) was expected to cancel it (-13.2 dB) but was not run against a clip. The default decision (`firmware_slew`, `receiver_chain_scale`, `firmware_layer_law`) and the organ `K` were open.
- **Decision:** (1) Run the clip matrix (default, slew, chain, chain + slew, chain + layer law, bus, legacy; velocity 100 and 88) on the Part A Velvet Grand segment and the DSoundman piano and pad clip; add hold-decay and release-drop metrics to `tools/emu_vs_hw.py` (`tools/emu_env.py`) and the slew variants to `emu_vs_hw_segments.json`. (2) Keep every default: `firmware_slew`, `receiver_chain_scale`, `firmware_layer_law`, `firmware_bus_scale` stay off and the organ `K = 2.8` stays EMPIRICAL. (3) Replace the velocity-only slew rate table by `rate_for(velocity, note)`, a bilinear table over the ARM rate words of the Velvet Grand note-on (velocity 20 / 64 / 88 / 100 / 127, notes 36 / 60 / 84; the rate is about twice as fast at note 84 and 0.75 times at note 36 against note 60). (4) Add `arm_smp_slew_init.py rates` (level and rate word of any sound, velocity and key from the firmware note-on).
- **Why:** Chain + slew is the only variant that reproduces the Part A clip level (RMS -2.2 / -3.0 dB at velocity 100 / 88 against +9.7 / +7.3 for the default) and it brings the hold decay (-1.8 / -2.4 dB against the clip's -2.8; default -4.9 / -4.3) and the release drop (-1.0 / 0.0 against -2.95; default -7.6) closer; the attack metric worsens (120 / 138 ms against 87; default 66) and the spectrum is mixed. The held chord of the DSoundman clip contradicts it: the piano reads -51.4 dBFS (legacy -31.0, chain -28.3) while the clip has the piano at the level of the synth pad, which the legacy default reproduces to about 5 dB, so chain + slew is about 25 dB under the pad. Part A and DSoundman disagree by about 22 dB under the verified words (about 12 dB under the legacy default). A long ARM capture shows no later writes to the voice records, so the missing level is not a later ARM word of the slew; the recording level of each video and the master level word are the unknowns.
- **Alternatives:** Flip chain + slew on the Part A clip (rejected: the second clip is 22 dB off and the sustained piano, the usual use, would be 20 dB quieter than the legacy default); flip only the chain scale (+2.7 dB, rejected: it moves Part A away, +12.3 dB); flip the layer law (rejected: no clip has a piano fader below 127); fit a velocity or a gain to make both clips agree (rejected: that is a fitted constant, not a verified word).
- **Impact:** No audio change by default; `render_hash` and the goldens are unchanged. The opt-in slew changes with the key (rate word). `K = 2.8` is within 0.23 dB of `1.6 * 0.3732 / 0.21875` (the slew gain of the piano): recorded as a lead, not applied ([organ-output-law](research/organ-output-law.md)). OQ20 and OQ24 point at the two-clip disagreement; the next test is a clip with a known recording level or an in-video organ and piano reference.

### D70. The ARM P words drive the MULTI and SUPER stages under `--synth-p verified`; the default stays the EMPIRICAL table

- **Status:** accepted. **Decided by:** the evidence of [synth-p-writer](research/synth-p-writer.md) s8 and [synth-validation](research/synth-validation.md) s11.
- **Context:** D62 tabulated the words the ARM writes per wave type and exposed them as opt-in `--synth-p verified`, which only replaced the gain of the single-oscillator rows. The MULTI / SUPER / SUPER1 / SUPER 2..4 stages still ran on ASSUMED voice ratios, weights, normalisation and spread scale (`SUPER_SPREAD_MAX = 0.1`).
- **Decision:** (1) In the verified mode the four unison stages read the words (`synth/p_stage_words.rs`, `synth/fw_stages/words.rs`): SUPER sixteen voices at `P[-32 + 2 j + lane]` with equal weights 0.7071 and gain `P[16]`, SUPER 1 fourteen, SUPER 2..4 eight, MULTI the record `DM(0xC1596 .. 98)` with the lane weights; the units were derived per stage with the Python stage models (`tools/synth_stage_golden11.py`, 20 cases, 3.6e-4). (2) The spread word is `s5 = clip(DM(0xC271A), 0.5) + 0.5` and the ARM writes `kOscCtrl = -0.5 + knob / 127`, so `s5 = Osc Ctrl` and the SUPER spread scale is 1.0 (the link between the two words is ASSUMED). (3) Unison voices start on a fixed pseudo-random phase set (ASSUMED; a coherent start loses 15 dB from ctrl 0 to 1 against the 1.2 dB of the lesson clip). (4) GUI Firmware bar dropdown entry "ARM words (verified)" (`PSpec::Verified`); `FirmwareOptions::default`, the GUI default and the golden hashes are unchanged: the default stays the EMPIRICAL table.
- **Why:** On the 15 clips the SUPER cutoff factor the words prefer on `pad_silk` is the old 0.45 (score 2.36 to 1.92), but the other unison clips get worse (`mega_line` 1.69 to 2.64 at best, `sci_fi_80s` 0.86 to 1.42, the Super Saw lesson clips 4.38 / 6.02 / 4.96 to 6.05 / 5.55 / 4.91), so the mean is about 3.6 against the default 3.40: the D22 bar is not met. The rows around the words (cutoff factors, the 1.6 level constant, SUPER1 and MULTI gains) were fitted against the placeholder stages and need a joint refit.
- **Alternatives:** Flip the default (rejected: bar not met); refit all rows in this PR (rejected: the full 15-clip level grid did not finish in the time box); map the pending words `a`, `b` to `kOscUnison` (not possible without a loaded-program capture).
- **Impact:** New `StageWords`, `PSpec::Verified`, `PTable::words`; `--synth-p verified[:wave=g/w2/r2/shape/cutoff;...]` accepts row overrides; `ag_synth` sets the stage words. OQ22 and TD-11 updated; defaults and `render_hash` unchanged.

### D76. The on-screen keybed plays velocity 88, not 100

- **Status:** superseded by D84 (the constant 88 is replaced by a hit-position velocity; 88 stays as the computer-keyboard default)
- **Context:** The user hears the GUI's Felt Upright (`A:13`) as much harsher and brighter than the Part A demo video. The program is piano only, Timbre Off, no FX; the sound and the firmware level law are the verified defaults. On the 150 to 190 s clip the spectral shape at velocity 100 matches the recording (3 to 8 kHz +0.5 dB), but the level is +11.0 dB over it, +3.7 dB over the Pipe yardstick ([piano-felt-upright-soft](research/piano-felt-upright-soft.md)). The GUI mouse press sent velocity 100.
- **Decision:** `MOUSE_VELOCITY` of the on-screen keybed is 88. No DSP default, golden or `render_hash` changes; MIDI input keeps its own velocity.
- **Why:** The Velvet Grand clip fits a uniform replay velocity of 88 ([piano-note-velocity](research/piano-note-velocity.md)); the Felt Upright clip is +1.1 dB against the yardstick at 88 (inside the 3 dB target). Velocity 100 plays the loud, bright layer of every velocity-layered piano on every click.
- **Alternatives:** Flip `--piano-slew-fw` or the bus convention (rejected: the absolute level stays ASSUMED, D22); darken the piano with a filter (rejected: the spectrum matches at equal velocity, no evidence for a stage); a velocity-from-click-position control (kept as a follow-up).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-gui/src/panel/keybed.rs` constant and test; `emu_vs_hw_segments.json` gains `piano_felt`; OQ20 and the piano spec updated. A single-note hardware recording per velocity is needed to settle level and brightness ([piano-felt-upright-soft](research/piano-felt-upright-soft.md)).

### D84. The mouse velocity of the on-screen keybed follows the hit position along the key

- **Status:** proposed (pending the user's listening check). Supersedes D76.
- **Context:** D76 fixed the mouse velocity at 88. The 2026-11-15 listening test showed that key force changes the piano sound a lot, and the hardware Felt Upright demo is played at a median velocity of about 43, so a constant 88 is too hard and leaves no way to play soft.
- **Decision:** `keybed_velocity::velocity_at` maps the pointer position along the pressed key linearly to 20..127: top of the key (back) = 20, front tip = 127. A white key is `height` long, a black key `0.62 * height` (its own front tip is loud too). The computer keyboard (D80, default 88, C / V steps) and MIDI In are unchanged. No DSP default, golden or `render_hash` changes.
- **Why:** A real key is struck harder near its front tip; the mouse has no force, so the hit position is the only free axis. It gives the whole range, including the soft layers the hardware demo uses.
- **Alternatives:** Keep 88 (rejected: too hard); a velocity slider (rejected: slower to play, no per-note dynamics); a curve biased to soft values (rejected: no measured mouse-to-force law; the linear map is ASSUMED, a curve can follow the single-note hardware recordings); mouse speed (not available per click in egui).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-gui/src/panel/keybed.rs`, new `keybed_velocity.rs` with tests; `docs/specs/visual-style-and-panel.md` keybed section; `computer_keys.rs` comment. Open: the 20 / 127 ends and the linearity are ASSUMED.

### D81. The MULTI / SUPER stages read the verified ARM words by default; SUPER wave 0 on LP24 has its own cutoff factor

- **Status:** accepted. **Decided by:** the evidence of [synth-validation](research/synth-validation.md) s12 (16 hardware clips, D22 bar).
- **Context:** The user reports that the factory program `Horizon Whl` (`A:16`, SUPER wave 0 on LP24 277 / 554 Hz at -12 semitones, MULTI LP12, wheel, arpeggiator) sounds very far from the hardware. The Part A video (287 s on) gave the first hardware clip of it: the D22 default was +9.9 dB in RMS (sub bass 12 dB hot, 500 Hz and up 10 to 15 dB low, presence -16 dB, score 10.2 against legacy 1.8). D70 kept the verified words opt-in because the neighbouring rows were fitted on the placeholder stages.
- **Decision:** `PTable::FITTED.words` (and the built-in `PTableSpec`) is true: the MULTI, SUPER and SUPER 1 to 4 stages read the verified ARM words in the default. Rows refit: SUPER 2.4 / 0.45, MULTI 3.8 / 2.0, SUPER1 1.6 / 2.2 (gain / cutoff factor); SUPER wave 0 on an LP24 filter gets the type factor 4.4 (`row_type_cutoff_scale`, total 1.98). `horizon_whl` joins the validation set (`synth_horizon` segment). `--legacy-all` / `--synth-chain legacy` keep the classic chain.
- **Why:** Horizon Whl score 10.23 to 1.57 (presence -16.0 to +0.7 dB, RMS +9.9 to +1.1). The 16-clip mean improves 3.82 to 3.34, no Part A clip moves by more than 3 dB in presence or RMS, 13 of 16 clips beat legacy.
- **Alternatives:** A table-only fix (SUPER gain and cutoff, rejected: the stage with ASSUMED constants stays 6 dB per octave steeper than the hardware whatever the factors, best score 8.0); keep the words opt-in and special-case the program (rejected: the same stage plays in 40 factory programs); lower the sub bass with a high pass (rejected: the hardware shows the 21 Hz fundamental).
- **Impact:** `synth/p_table.rs` (`words` default, rows), `synth/p_type.rs` (`row_type_cutoff_scale`), `synth/render.rs`, the two clip files; `render_hash` whole is now `b9a1d073...b846` (D59: `63923599...50a16`; all Rust tests pass unchanged); the 15 original clips read 3.40 to 3.46 (`ag_supersaw_0` 4.38 to 6.06); OQ22 and TD-11 updated. Open: the LP24 SUPER factor has one clip, the mod wheel and the arpeggiator are not part of the comparison.

### D79. Piano comparisons replay transcribed velocities and pedal

- **Status:** proposed (pending the user's listening check of the local piano-compare folder)
- **Context:** The piano clip comparisons replayed the video-detected notes at one uniform velocity (88, earlier 100) without the sustain pedal. The Transkun transcription of the Part A video ([piano-transcription](research/piano-transcription.md)) gives per-note velocities and pedal: the played median is 36 to 65 (Felt Upright 43, Velvet Grand 50), far below 88.
- **Decision:** `tools/emu_vs_hw.py --events-from transkun` replays the committed transcribed stream (velocities, pedal) and is the reference replay for piano segments; uniform-velocity variants stay as labelled `vel-N` rows. No DSP default, golden or `render_hash` changes; `--firmware-piano-chain` and `--piano-slew-fw` stay opt-in (chain + slew is 5 to 9 dB low with a twice as slow attack on four clips). D76 (keybed 88) is not changed by this decision; the evidence says a mouse velocity near 45 reproduces the video's Felt Upright.
- **Why:** At the transcribed velocities the default emulator is within -1.6 to -3.8 dB RMS of the hardware on four independent clips and within about 2 dB per band on three; the level-versus-velocity slope matches (0.127 against 0.128 dB per velocity unit on Velvet Grand). The old +9.7 dB default excess was a replay-velocity artefact.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the uniform 88 replay (rejected: it hides the dynamics and misstates the level gap by 7 dB); wait for the single-note hardware recording (kept as the follow-up that calibrates the transcriber's velocity scale).
- **Impact:** New tools (`transcribe_clip.py`, `midi_events.py`, `clip_flags.py`, `emu_events.py`, `emu_env.py`, `transcribe_agree.py`), `ns4 render-patch --events` reads an optional `pedal` list, committed numbers-only streams `docs/research/data/piano_events_*.json`; OQ20 updated. Transkun velocities are an estimate (recall about 40 % of the video-confirmed notes, onset 50 to 70 ms late).
### D78. Library drawer loads start from an Init Program; the organ ladders follow the section state

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (report "pianos play with a lot of organ" in the GUI; PR `fix/gui-organ-in-piano-program`)
- **Context:** Clicking a sound in the Pianos or Samples tab of the library drawer only loaded that sound and enabled its layer (`SetLayerActive`); everything else of the running state stayed. At start-up that state has Organ A on at level 0.85, so every library piano played under an organ. Program loads were not the cause: through `apply_audio_command` (the cpal callback's function) the factory piano programs render with zero organ energy in the Firmware and Classic presets. Second finding: the Organ drawbar ladders were lit from the stored drawbars whatever the section state, so a piano-only program showed a lit Organ section (the hardware frame of `A:13 Felt Upright` shows none lit).
- **Decision:** (1) A drawer load into Piano A / B or Synth A / B / C first replaces the program with `library::init_program(target)` (only that section and layer on, scene II = scene I, all effects and levels default) on the panel and the audio thread, then loads the sound. A "Merge into current program" checkbox (default off) restores the old behaviour and the drawer says which mode is active. Loads from the panel's own piano / sample selectors keep merging (they change the sound of a layer of the running program). (2) `Stage4PanelState::apply_program` takes the scene mask the engine plays under `FirmwareRouting` (`default_layer_a`, now `pub`), so panel and engine agree on programs with an enabled section and no layer. (3) The ladders are unlit while the Organ section is off.
- **Why:** The hardware selects a sound inside a program; a fresh program is the only state a library sound can be judged in. Tests: a program Y loaded after an organ program renders identically to a fresh Y; a drawer piano after an organ program renders identically to a fresh Init plus the piano.
- **Alternatives:** Reset only the organ (rejected: leaves synth, effects and levels of the previous state); always merge (rejected: the reported bug); apply the init through `Stage4Engine::layer_init(All)` (rejected: allocates, control thread only, and keeps the loaded sounds of other layers).
- **Impact:** `docs/gui-testing.md` (library drawer), `docs/specs/visual-style-and-panel.md` (ladders); the Init Program with synth or piano B alone is ASSUMED (the hardware init is Piano A). Other LEDs of a switched-off section are unchanged (ASSUMED, no frame shows them).

### D80. Computer-keyboard playing claims P / Z / L; the view shortcuts move to Shift while it is on

- **Status:** accepted. **Decided by:** the user request (play the GUI with the PC keyboard).
- **Context:** The GUI could only be played with the mouse (D76 velocity 88) or a MIDI keyboard. The DAW "virtual keyboard" layout (A S D F G H J K L ; ' white, W E T Y U O P black, Z / X octave, C / V velocity) collides with three existing shortcuts: P (panorama), Z (focus zoom) and L (library drawer).
- **Decision:** (1) `panel/computer_keys.rs` maps physical keys (egui `physical_key`, so QWERTY and ABNT2 are the same) to notes C4 = 60 upward; `panel/computer_kb.rs` sends Note On / Off and the sustain pedal (Space, CC 64) through `AudioHandle::send_keyboard_midi`, the keybed path, so Local Control Off keeps them silent and they never use the MIDI-in thread. (2) Default on, velocity 88 (1..127, step 8), base octave C4 (0..7). Auto-repeat is ignored; everything is released when the window loses focus, a text field takes the keyboard or the feature is switched off. (3) While it is on, P, Z and L play; the view and library shortcuts become Shift+P, Shift+Z, Shift+L (digits, Esc, arrows and +/- are unaffected). Off, they work as before.
- **Why:** Playing is the primary use of the app and the DAW layout is the one users already know; the three view shortcuts have a header click or the library button as a mouse path, and Shift keeps them reachable without a mode switch.
- **Alternatives:** Drop P / L from the map (rejected: breaks the black-key row and the second white octave); a push-to-play mode key (rejected: hidden state, a stuck mode silences the keys); remap the view shortcuts to other letters (rejected: more collisions).
- **Update (same PR):** (4) The bar also gets a MIDI input port selector with a Rescan button (`audio/midi_ports.rs`): the MIDI thread keeps the connection, the GUI only sends `Rescan` / `Select` messages and reads a snapshot, so nothing locks on the audio thread. MIDI-in velocity is untouched.
- **Impact:** `view.rs` and `library/ui.rs` use `computer_keys::shortcut_pressed`; tests `panel/computer_kb_tests.rs`; [GUI testing](gui-testing.md) lists the keys. The velocity step and the map are `ASSUMED` (no Nord source).

### D83. The PURE noise row of the P table takes the saw gain (VERIFIED ARM word); the synth layer level law stays open

- **Status:** proposed (pending the user's listening check of `Leaky Stabs II`)
- **Context:** The user hears the factory program `Leaky Stabs II` (`A:46`, Part A video 1265 to 1317 s) as odd against the hardware. Decode: synth only (`section_enable_i = 1`), layer A SHAPE saw (`osc_category 3`, wave 1, level 0.82), layer B **PURE wave 6 = white noise** (the JSON `waveform: Saw` is a stale label; level 0.32), both Ladder24 at 2500 Hz with a 1.21 s filter-envelope decay (amount 0.74), amp attack 3 ms, decay Hold, release 3 ms, unison 3, reverb type 2 at 0.30, amp type 256. The noise layer rendered with the **idle P block (gain 1.0)** while the ARM writes `P[16] = 0.25` for noise exactly as for the PURE saw ([synth-p-writer](research/synth-p-writer.md) s3), and the saw row is 0.4: the noise sat 8 dB too loud and the 1.5 to 8 kHz bands read +7 / +12 dB against the video.
- **Decision:** New row `PWave::Noise` (`PURE 6`) with the saw gain 0.4 and cutoff factor 1.0 (ASSUMED); the other `Other` waves keep the idle block. Default path (strip + stages + table), `--synth-p table:noise=1/0/1/0.5/1` restores the old level. The firmware layer level law of the synth (handler `0xC0114588`, ids `0xA4..0xA6`) was disassembled but not run: it stores an integer `v * k` in a 16-bit word, **not** the `(v/127)^2` of the organ and piano handlers; whether the DSP squares it is open (OQ22).
- **Why:** Verified word equality (noise = saw = 0.25) instead of a fit: the default flips only the one wave that has the word evidence and a clip. Gap (audio-estimated notes, 35 s, 552 events): score 5.19 to 3.19, presence +7.0 to +4.3, brilliance +12.1 to +7.5, centroid +1317 to +502 Hz (layer B alone is +9.4 / +15.4); with the Transkun notes (162 events): 7.53 to 5.66. Layer A alone scores 1.49 / 4.09, so the saw layer is right and the remaining excess is noise. Squaring both layer levels (an unverified law) would bring the clip to 1.93 / 4.50.
- **Alternatives:** Apply `(v/127)^2` to the synth layers (rejected: handler shows a linear integer, D22 needs the DSP side); lower the idle block for all `Other` waves (rejected: sine, triangle, SUB, SYNC, MISC have no clip, D22); fit a noise gain to this clip (rejected: it mixes with the saw, the words are the better evidence).
- **Impact:** `synth/p_table.rs` (row, test), `emu_vs_hw_segments.json` gains `synth_leaky`, `synth_validation.json` gains clip `leaky_stabs` (holdout). Only layers on PURE wave 6 change; no golden or `render_hash` moves (workspace tests pass, 1546 lib tests, no re-baseline), and the P rows of the other 14 clips are untouched (their waves are not PURE 6; not re-run, 30 min each). The program sweep rows of programs with a noise layer move (not regenerated, TD-11). Not the cause of `Horizon Whl`, `Grand Pad` or the pianos with organ: they have no noise layer.

### D82. The synth layer level law is an opt-in `(v / 127)^2` (Grand Pad clip)

- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), pending the user's listening check
- **Context:** The factory program `A:43 Grand Pad` (piano Grand + SuperSaw pad, synth layer level 0.567) sounds odd against the Part A video at 1169 s ([grand-pad-vs-video](research/grand-pad-vs-video.md)). With the Transkun notes the default render is within 2.3 dB RMS, but the pad is +4.1 dB hot below 130 Hz. The piano and organ layer faders follow the verified `(v / 127)^2` (D26); the synth layer handler (`0xC011BB48`) was not run and the DSoundman fader move at 0.7 reads linear (-2.7 dB against -3.1).
- **Decision:** `Stage4Engine::synth_layer_law` and `--firmware-synth-layer-law` apply `(v / 127)^2` to the synth layers (`piano::downstream::layer_gain`). Off by default; `firmware_all` does not set it.
- **Why:** The Grand Pad score is best at the squared level (1.24 against 1.46 linear and 2.69 at level 1.0; RMS +0.6 dB against +2.3), but it is one clip with estimated notes and the other clip contradicts it, so the evidence does not meet the D22 bar for a flip.
- **Alternatives:** Flip the default (rejected: conflicting clips, Space Drama II and the 15 synth validation clips not re-run); leave the flag out and only document (rejected: the clip comparison needs the switch).
- **Impact:** `engine/mod.rs`, `engine/mix.rs`, `render_opts.rs` (test `synth_layer_law_squares_the_fader_only_when_selected`), `emu_vs_hw_segments.json` segment `pianopad_grand`, OQ22. Default renders and golden hashes unchanged.


### D87. The synth layer level law `(v / 127)^2` is the verified default

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15 (supersedes the default of D82; settles the open point of D83)
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), D22 bar met; evidence [synth-layer-level-law](research/synth-layer-level-law.md). Pending the user's listening check.
- **Context:** D82 found Grand Pad (A:43) better squared but left it opt-in (the handler was only disassembled, the DSoundman clip read linear); D83 found Leaky Stabs II (A:46) 8 dB too loud in the noise layer and named handler `0xC0114588`. OQ22 asked which law the firmware uses.
- **Decision:** The level handler is `0xC011BB48` (ids `0xA4..0xA6`); `0xC0114588` is a display path (16-bit word, never sent). Run in the ARM harness (`tools/arm_layer_level.py`, 30 synth cases, 70 of 70 with organ and piano): the stored word is `(v / 127)^2`, `-0.05` at 0, sent to FX core 1 rows 17..19, whose consumer is linear in the word. `synth_layer_law` is on by default (`Stage4Engine`, `FirmwareOptions`, `--firmware-all`); `--legacy-synth-layer-law` (and `--legacy-all`) restore the linear fader; `--firmware-synth-layer-law` stays accepted.
- **Why:** D22 criteria: (1) algorithm VERIFIED by the live run, (2) the mapping (edit id to word to FX row) VERIFIED, (3) `render_hash` re-baselined. Clips: `synth_leaky` 3.19 to 1.95 (Transkun notes 5.38 to 3.97), `pianopad_grand` 2.14 / 2.19 (audio notes, tie) and 1.48 to 1.42 (Transkun notes). The DSoundman layered clip (-2.7 dB at fader 0.7, linear -3.1, squared -6.2) disagrees, but its fader taper is unknown and the word is firmware evidence.
- **Alternatives:** keep linear (rejected: contradicts the run); keep the flag opt-in (rejected: criteria met); fit a power (rejected: the word is exact).
- **Impact:** `engine/mod.rs`, `render_opts.rs` (defaults, `--legacy-synth-layer-law`, summary, tests), `tools/arm_layer_level.py` (synth handler) and its test, `emu_vs_hw_segments.json` (`legacy-synth-layer` variants replace the hand-squared ones). `render_hash` whole `b9a1d073...b846` to `46f4dc64fc6de62eac40f1ee8549548d3965eea2d86aa5d59b828ed6566da506`; only segment `engine` moves (peak 0.995080 to 0.951197, RMS 0.212688 to 0.208288). Program sweep rows of programs with a synth layer level below 127 move (TD-11, not regenerated). Open: Reverb GLOBAL on (scale 3.25) is not modelled.
### D86. Piano decay and attack: no engine change; the T60 gap was a replay artefact; per-octave T60 is measured by `tools/piano_decay.py`

- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** [piano-felt-upright-soft](research/piano-felt-upright-soft.md) s2 reported a hardware T60 of 3.2 s against 1.3 s in the emulator, and [piano-transcription](research/piano-transcription.md) s4 an attack of 121 / 99 ms (chain + slew) against 64 / 56 ms. Both came from the uniform-velocity, pedal-less replay or an older build. D22 forbids flipping defaults on weak evidence.
- **Decision:** No change to `piano.rs`, the slew voice or any default. Add `tools/piano_decay.py` (per-octave fundamental T60 of a hardware clip against emulator renders of the same transcribed notes and pedal) and commit the derived numbers (`docs/research/data/piano_decay_per_octave.json`, [piano-decay-attack](research/piano-decay-attack.md)). `rate_for` (Velvet-only rate table, ASSUMED) and the absolute level of the slew gain stay open (OQ20).
- **Why:** With the transcribed pedal and velocities the median emulator-to-hardware T60 ratio of the default engine is 1.00 over 9 octave groups of four clips (0.59 to 2.86); Felt Upright tail 4.25 s against 3.34 s hardware, so the old "1.3 s" is not reproduced. Default attack is about 10 ms faster than the hardware (17 %); chain + slew measures 47 to 70 ms against 56 to 64 ms at this HEAD, not 2x. Fitting a decay or release constant would break octaves that now agree (Felt Upright 9.01 / 9.00 s) to chase scatter (1 to 13 notes per octave).
- **Alternatives:** Fit a damper / release time constant to the clips (rejected: no gap to fit, EMPIRICAL on lossy reverberant audio, D22); replace the default release envelope by the firmware 44 ms release (rejected: the firmware release is the slew path's, already opt-in, and its absolute level is open); flip chain + slew (rejected: level 1 to 8 dB under the default on three clips, D72).
- **Impact:** New tool and tests (`tools/tests/test_piano_decay.py`), research note, data file, spec paragraph in `docs/specs/piano-engine.md`, OQ20 update, ROADMAP item. No Rust change; `render_hash` and every golden are unchanged.

### D88. The Piano program Touch plays the firmware velocity curves (opt-in)

- **Status:** proposed
- **Context:** The 2026-11-15 listening test showed that key force changes the piano sound strongly, and the emulator played raw MIDI velocity through one fixed curve (the `.npno` level law already used the firmware's Medium curve whatever the program's Touch; the other sounds used ASSUMED powers 0.65 / 1.0 / 1.55). Bean `ns4-yp85` asked where the per-program Touch (Piano, Organ, Synth) lives in the firmware and whether it applies to MIDI notes.
- **Decision:** (1) Only the Piano section has a Touch: edit id `0x159`, values 0 / 1 / 2 (heavy / medium / light, default 1), which selects `curveId` 10 / 11 / 13 of the byte table `0xC0532664 + (curveId << 10)` that gives the velocity position `T` of every note (stroke layer and level attenuation `X`). Organ and Synth have no Touch id (organ key messages carry no velocity, the Synth keeps its amp velocity parameter). The tables are `piano/touch_curve.rs` (`TouchCurve`, three 127-byte derived tables, curve 11 reused from `velocity_curve.rs`). (2) `PianoLayer::firmware_touch` (`--firmware-piano-touch`, GUI Firmware bar "Touch curves", default off): `.npno` sounds with a level model play the layer's curve through `NpnoLevel::voices_touch`; other sounds multiply the Medium response by `2^(-2 (X - X_medium) / 60)`. (3) The program file field sits at file bit `1991..1992` of layer A and `2087` of layer B and equals the edit value; the Rust decoder reads other bits (always 0, `Medium`), so the decoder realignment is a follow-up and the default stays off.
