- **Why:** All three tables equal the byte the ARM reads for a MIDI note-on in 27 of 27 runs and the file field equals the edit value in the program recall in 6 of 6 ([piano-touch-curves](research/piano-touch-curves.md)); the Touch applies to MIDI notes, not only to the keybed. The default does not flip because D22 needs the mapping into the engine verified end to end: the decoder does not yet deliver the program's Touch, and the clips are Medium, so no clip can judge the other two curves.
- **Alternatives:** Flip the default now (rejected: no program from a file would change, and the renders would differ only for a hand-set Touch); fix the decoder in the same change (rejected: realigning `variation`, string resonance, soft release and pedal noise changes every program and needs its own check, `ns4 preset-check` 937 of 937); replace the ASSUMED powers for the non-`.npno` sounds by the absolute firmware law (rejected: strokes carry level offsets those sounds lack, so only the Touch difference is applied).
- **Impact:** `piano/touch_curve.rs`, `piano/touch_layer.rs`, `npno_level.rs` (`*_touch` methods, same results for Medium), `velocity_curve.rs` (`x_from_position`), `FirmwareOptions::piano_touch`, GUI bar switch, `tools/arm_touch_curves.py`. Defaults, goldens and `render_hash` unchanged. Follow-ups in the research note: the System-menu MIDI velocity curves. The decoder realignment is done in [D99](#d99-the-piano-layer-decoder-reads-the-firmware-bit-layout-the-touch-default-stays-off).

### D85. Organ level `K = 2.8` stays EMPIRICAL: the image audit finds no `1 / 0.21875` lane word; the D72 lead is pinned by a test
- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent)
- **Context:** D72 noted `K = 2.8` is within 0.23 dB of `1.6 * 0.3732 / 0.21875` (organ chain word times FX path over the piano SMP slew gain). The task was to replace `K` by a firmware-derived value, promoting it only if verified end to end (D22).
- **Decision:** Audit the FX, PNO and ORG DSP images and the ARM code for a stored word equal to `2.8`, `0.21875` or `1 / 0.21875` ([organ-output-law](research/organ-output-law.md) s6). None is a stored float in a DSP image (all matches are at non-word-aligned offsets); the first-pass ARM matches were big-endian byte noise. Second pass (unpacked SRAM, little-endian aligned ARM scan, s6.1): 2.8 occurs only as an element of interpolation tables, and `0.21875`, `1 / 0.21875`, `0.357` occur nowhere; the FX run shows no piano-side attenuation. `K` stays `EMPIRICAL`, `Chain` stays opt-in, no default moves. A unit test pins the lead so a later decode has the number to hit.
- **Why:** A numerical coincidence of two unrelated words is not a derivation (provenance rule: do not upgrade a tag without new evidence); the clips cannot separate 2.73 from 2.8.
- **Alternatives:** Add a `chain / 0.21875` law (rejected: same audio as `K` within 0.23 dB, would carry a false VERIFIED look); flip `Chain` as default (rejected: 13 dB under B3 / Vox clips).
- **Impact:** Doc comment and test in `organ/output_level.rs`; research s6; OQ24 update. No audio change, `render_hash` unchanged.

### D89. The SY pitch table entry writer is not found; the index comes from an ARM slot object; `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (task from the parent session, review notes applied; evidence [sy-pitch-entry-writer](research/sy-pitch-entry-writer.md))
- **Context:** D65 left the writer of the 16-bit packed float `e` at `DM(0x9354A + index)` open; D22 needs the firmware path verified end to end before `--sample-pitch fw` becomes the default. The task was to trace the writer in the ARM harness the way [synth-p-writer](research/synth-p-writer.md) found the P words.
- **Decision:** (1) No writer exists on any path the harness runs: no ARM message (boot, load, bind, note-on with the voice update), no ARM literal or table outside the DSP image streams, no DSP store in the SY listing, no SMP operand in the window. (2) New: the index is not the DSP reset value `0x36`; the ARM writes pair block word 0 as `slot | spec` (`0xC01E2B08`, `0xC01E2CC8`), the slot is copied from a two-word object (`0x9062F0F0`: `0, -1`, nothing writes it in the run), so the first SAMPLES voice reads `DM(0x9354A)`, which is zero in the image. (3) `--sample-pitch` stays off; `step_join::table_entry_for_key` stays a model; no engine change, no golden or `render_hash` moves. (4) New `tools/arm_sy_slot_probe.py` reproduces the chain and the watches.
- **Why:** The entry value cannot be verified end to end, so D22 is not met; the modelled step is already within 0.5 % of the semitone law (below audibility), so a flip would change renders for no verified gain.
- **Alternatives:** Flip on the model (rejected: same reason as D65); poke an invented slot allocator into the harness (rejected: it would only restate the model).
- **Impact:** New research note, tool and test; OQ22, TD-08 and the ROADMAP item updated; section 14 of [synth-sample-kernel-rule](research/synth-sample-kernel-rule.md) gets a pointer (its "index stays `0x36`" is the DSP reset only).

### D96. FM voice wiring: pitch, P words and ARM words drive the FM stages opt-in; the table depends on strip `+0xA4` / `+0xA8`
- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task
- **Context:** D92 ported the FM stages and left the voice words open. An outside review noted that the "type only" claim for the table varied only `a` and `b`. The position word `W`, the pitch term `p0` and the ARM block turned out to be known already: `W` and `p0` are the SYNC pitch words, the ARM block is `P[-257 .. -246]` of the committed P capture.
- **Decision:** (1) Add `synth/fw_fm/voice.rs` (`FmVoice`: `set_note`, `render4`) wiring `W = semi 2^24`, `p0 = 2 f / F_SY`, the type's P words and ARM words to the two stages with the default table, opt-in. (2) Add `tools/synth_fm_tables.py probe`: of the 38 strip offsets the apply reads, only `+0x00`, `+0xA4` (mode flag) and `+0xA8` (scale, `r = a8 / 2^19`) change the table; the ramp and decay laws are recorded as VERIFIED probes. (3) Correct the "type only" statements in the note and spec 4.5. (4) The default table stays the `+0xA4 = 0`, `+0xA8 = 0x80000` one; no table family is modelled in Rust.
- **Why:** The words that are known are wired; the table family has no generator formula yet, and a law swept for two types would be a guess for the other three (D22: no default flip without end-to-end evidence; no hardware FM clip exists).
- **Alternatives:** Model the `a8` scaling of the ramp and decay segments for all types now (rejected: types 49 and 52 only were swept, the other segments are not decoded); wire `FmVoice` into `SynthLayer` (rejected: level and timbre cannot be validated, the wave id to `a8` path is open).
- **Impact:** New `voice.rs` and tests, a probe subcommand and its test, doc updates. `render_hash` and golden hashes are unchanged. Open (OQ22): writer of `+0xA4` / `+0xA8`, generator of the 361 words, writer of `DM(0xC2716)` and of `x`, level against a hardware clip.

### D92. The FM stages `0x124269` / `0x12453F` are ported as opt-in equation models; the FM band tables are committed as derived numbers
- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task
- **Context:** OQ22 listed the FM banks (strip types 49..53, stages `0x124269` / `0x12453F`) as open and the first feasibility note said the table source was not reachable. The strip apply `0xC01CE7D8` with a pending type 49..53 does send 361 float words to `DM(0xE0900 .. 0xE0A68)` of core 2 (ARM generated, type dependent only, [fm-banks-1-2](research/fm-banks-1-2.md) s1).
- **Decision:** (1) Commit the 5 x 361 captured words as [data/synth-fm-tables.csv](research/data/synth-fm-tables.csv) (1,805 derived numbers, D56 sizing, no firmware bytes). (2) Add a listing interpreter (`tools/sharc_text_vm.py`) and an oracle driver (`tools/synth_fm_oracle.py`): the interpreter equals `sharc-org` on 620 of 620 (stage 1) and 520 of 520 (stage 2) written words. (3) Python equation models `tools/synth_stage_fm.py` equal the interpreter on 1240 of 1240 and 1040 of 1040 words (20 trials x 5 types, `synth_fm_oracle.py --model`). (4) Rust `synth/fw_fm/` (`fm_stage1`, `fm_stage2`, `fm_table`) as f32 models checked by 20 Python goldens (max error 6e-7). Nothing is reachable from the default render path.
- **Why:** The stages are now word-exact against the oracle in Python, so the port can be checked; the voice wiring needs words that are not decoded (below), and D22 forbids a default change without a hardware bar.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the note as a blocker (rejected: the data is readable); wire the stage into `SynthLayer` FM mode now (rejected: position word `W`, pitch term and the ratio table of `bank0_CODE@0x2D82A8` have no decoded writer, the result could not be validated against the hardware clips).
- **Impact:** New files only: `tools/synth_stage_fm.py`, `synth_fm_golden.py`, `synth_fm_oracle.py`, `synth_fm_tables.py`, `sharc_text_vm.py`, `crates/nord-core/src/synth/fw_fm/`, goldens and tests. `render_hash` and the golden hashes are unchanged. Open (OQ22): the writer of the position word `W = DM(I2-31)`, the pitch term `DM(I2-29)`, the ARM word block `DM(0xC1599 ..)` and the P words of types 49..53 per FM wave, the table dependence on strip state other than the type (only `a`, `b` were varied), and the voice wiring.
### D91. The reverb colour words are captured per type and ported opt-in; the engine FDN stays the default

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task (audio item 7)
- **Context:** Audio item 7 asked which parameters the firmware writes per reverb type and variation and which tail time it uses (Grand Pad clip: 13.4 s hardware against 3.7 s emulated). The type map and the multi-tap routines were done (D31, D64), but `kRvbColor` (Dark, Bright) was never read from the ARM and the engine applied only an FDN damping coefficient.
- **Decision:** (1) Capture the colour handler for the 10 types 2..11 and both colours on the synth unit L6 (`tools/fx_rvb_color_capture.py`), commit the deltas against each type's own block as [data/fx-reverb-colors.csv](research/data/fx-reverb-colors.csv) (derived numbers, no firmware bytes) and generate `fx/fw_rvbm_colors.rs`. (2) `ReverbMulti::set_color` applies them in place; `LayerFxChain` maps Normal / Dark / Bright to 0 / 1 / 2 behind the existing `FirmwareFx::reverb`; Chorale keeps colour 0. (3) Record the tails: firmware 0.52 to 1.35 s, engine FDN Hall 3.4 s and Cathedral 7.9 s. (4) Keep every default and `render_hash`.
- **Why:** The words are VERIFIED against the ARM and the routine is the verified one, so the opt-in is safe; but no hardware clip isolates a reverb tail and the committed tap words differ from this harness's block ([fx-reverb-colors](research/fx-reverb-colors.md) s3), so the end-to-end bar of D22 is not met. The Grand Pad tail gap is explained structurally: the program's reverbs (types 3 and 4) have firmware T60 under 0.8 s, so the 13.4 s is the sustain pedal and the pad.
- **Alternatives:** Flip `FirmwareFx::reverb` on (rejected: D22, and the FDN Hall is 2.9 times longer than the routine with no hardware tail to arbitrate); retune the FDN decay to the firmware T60 now (rejected: same missing hardware tail); port type 6 from the harness block (rejected: it is the initial block without taps, not the form of the other types).
- **Impact:** New files `fx_rvb_color_capture.py`, `fx_rvb_colors.py`, `fx/fw_rvbm_colors.rs`, `tests_fw_rvbm_color.rs`, `tests_fw_rvbm_tail.rs`, [fx-reverb-colors](research/fx-reverb-colors.md); `fw_rvbm.rs` gains `set_color`; defaults and `render_hash` unchanged. OQ21 gains the open list (tap word origin, type 6, Spring colour, hardware tail).


### D90. Mod 2 parameter laws are verified on a dense ARM grid; the shared amount / rate words get their joint law; `mod2` stays opt-in

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15; the "stays opt-in" part is superseded by D163 (Mod 2 is the default since 2026-11-18)
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (task `fx-mod2-validation`; evidence [fx-mod2-validation](research/fx-mod2-validation.md))
- **Context:** The Mod 2 stages (`FirmwareFx::mod2`: CHOR, VIBE, FLANG, PHAS, SPIN) read the words of `kFx2Rate` / `kFx2Amt` from 11 captured samples interpolated linearly, and assumed `A(amt) R(rate) / R(64)` for words both ids write. Only CHOR has a hardware clip (D60), and its delay is 1.7 times the firmware's.
- **Decision:** Capture every `kFx2Amt` / `kFx2Rate` word on the booted ARM at 44 values (organ, piano, synth units, `tools/fx_mod2_dense.py`) and the words both ids write on a 3 x 44 rate / amount grid (`tools/fx_mod2_joint.py`). The law stage interpolates the 44 knots (`fw_mod2_dense.rs`, generated); PHAS 0 `c[-28]` is bilinear in the rate (VERIFIED 1.3e-7), FLANG `c[-16]` keeps the product law (VERIFIED 9.5e-8), CHOR 5 delays `c[-0x1C]`, `c[-0x1B]` take the joint closed form (VERIFIED 1.4e-5 frames); CHOR 4 mix constants are corrected to slope 0.0111339 and cap 0.707 (`0x3F34FDF4`). VIBE and CHOR 4 closed forms are pinned to the ARM words at all 44 values. ENS stays unwired (its left voice needs the circular tap registers `B4 / L4 / B7 / L7`, not decoded; test records the finding). `FirmwareFx::mod2` stays in `ALL`, not `DEFAULT`.
- **Why:** The 11 sample interpolation was off by up to 3.9 % of a word's range (FLANG swing) and the PHAS 0 product law by 8x off the axes; both are fixed by data the harness gives. Defaults flip only with a hardware comparison (D22); the CHOR clip did not pass it and no other Mod 2 type has a clip.
- **Alternatives:** Closed forms for every type (rejected: PHAS 0, FLANG and SPIN tables are not simple functions, the knots reproduce them within 0.5 %); flip CHOR to default because its words are now exact (rejected: the delay miss of D60 is not a words problem); wire ENS with `L4 = L7 = 0x200` (rejected: ASSUMED register state).
- **Impact:** Opt-in audio of PHAS 0, CHOR 4 (mix), CHOR 5 (delay) changes; `render_hash` unchanged (`DEFAULT` has no `mod2`). OQ21 gains the ENS register question and the per-kind words follow-up. New data files `fx-mod2-dense.csv`, `fx-mod2-joint.csv`.

### D93. Grand Pad leftovers: the pedal replay closes most of the width and tail gap, the synth FX rack is irrelevant here; `--synth-sustain-pedal` is opt-in

- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent); no default changes (D22)
- **Context:** D82 left four Grand Pad (A:43) points open against the Part A clip: synth FX rack (amp, EQ mid -5 dB, reverb 0.37) not decoded, stereo width 0.54 against 0.37, tail 13.4 s against 3.7 s, sustain pedal not replayed ([grand-pad-leftovers](research/grand-pad-leftovers.md)).
- **Decision:** (1) No engine default changes. (2) New opt-in `--synth-sustain-pedal` (`FirmwareOptions::synth_sustain_pedal`, sets `PedalState::sustped` of the three synth layers so CC 64 defers the synth note release like the organ layers). (3) Clip variants `synth-sustain-pedal`, `synth-eq-neutral`, `synth-reverb-off`, `fx-ports-all` and the combination are added to segment `pianopad_grand`. (4) The tail and width remainder go to OQ21 (reverb per type, synth stereo path); the SUSTPED default of the synth layers goes to the ARM harness.
- **Why:** With the committed Transkun stream (notes, velocities, pedal) the default reads width 0.46 (was 0.37), tail 6.7 s (was 3.7 s), RMS -1.0 dB, score 1.42: the D82 numbers came from a render without the pedal. The rack is irrelevant here: the program EQ mid sits at about 700 Hz where the 294 Hz pad has no energy (mid 0 dB: at most 0.1 dB per band, score 1.42 to 1.38), amp type 0 drive 0 is already the firmware amp, and both reverbs off move the T60 by under 0.25 s. The synth pedal hold gets the tail to 10.9 s and hold / release closer, but the score rises to 1.68 and the SUSTPED default is not decoded (manual p. 28 only names the button); one clip, not a D22 flip.
- **Alternatives:** Hold the synth on the pedal by default (rejected: spectral score worse, hardware default unknown); decode the synth unit EQ words first (rejected: cannot change this clip by more than 0.1 dB); change the reverb mapping (rejected: owned by the reverb per type work).
- **Impact:** `render_opts.rs` (flag, summary, test `synth_sustain_pedal_is_opt_in_and_only_touches_the_synth_layers`), the GUI firmware panel preset copy, `emu_vs_hw_segments.json` (5 variants), [grand-pad-leftovers](research/grand-pad-leftovers.md), [audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md) item 8, `midi-and-control.md` (SUSTPED row), OQ21. Default renders and `render_hash` unchanged.

### D94. The Leslie feeder is traced and carries no gain; the B3 note set is 96 sustained keys; no default or code change
- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task (audio item 9)
- **Context:** Audio item 9 ([audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md)) left the B3 presence (-5.7 dB firmware Leslie, -20 dB engine rotary), the Leslie level and the "48 dB" open, with the feeder of `0xB6000..3` untraced and a Transkun note stream of 525 short notes.
- **Decision:** (1) Record the trace ([organ-preamp-search](research/organ-preamp-search.md) s6.1): `0xB6000..3` is the harness pair; the processor input is the chain lane (scaled by `wet`) plus a gated aux pair (`DM(0xB2F3C)` = 0) plus two DMA lanes `0xB0078 / 0xB008C`; all feeds see the same linear -48 dB processor and the chain mix has no gain above 1, so no stage is ported. (2) Add `tools/note_offsets.py` (merge same-key re-triggers; audio offsets as an upper bound) and replay the clip with 96 sustained keys. (3) Keep `--leslie firmware` opt-in and every default as is.
- **Why:** With the realistic note set the firmware Leslie presence is +1.5 .. +2.8 dB, so presence was the replay, not a stage; the remaining sub +14 dB is a flat 0..90 Hz rumble of the surrogate history ring (ring variants move it from +4.1 to +13.7), RMS stays 4.9 dB under, mid +3.1 .. +3.8 and brilliance +4.5 .. +5.8 are outside the D22 bar, and there is one clip with an unknown speed history.
- **Alternatives:** Add a `tanh` preamp or a gain before the Leslie (rejected: no firmware stage exists, it would be fitted to one clip); flip `--leslie firmware` to default (rejected: D22); keep the 525-note replay (rejected: it is Leslie amplitude chatter, re-triggering key click and envelope).
- **Impact:** New `tools/note_offsets.py` and tests; docs only otherwise; `render_hash` and goldens unchanged. Open (OQ21): the DMA ring of the Leslie history, `DM(0xB00EE)`, a lone-note hardware clip.

### D95. `mod2` wires ENS with ASSUMED circular tap registers; SPIN was already wired; defaults unchanged
- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task (audio item 6)
- **Context:** [D90](#d90-mod-2-parameter-laws-are-verified-on-a-dense-arm-grid-the-shared-amountrate-words-get-their-joint-law-mod2-stays-opt-in) left ENS (`kFx2Type` 8, 9) unwired because its left output stays exactly 0 with the boot row; `audio-remaining.md` row 6 still said SPIN diverged. The law stage already builds SPIN (10, 11) from the boot row `ROW_SPIN`, 44-knot laws and the second ring; the dense capture has one type-switch message for ENS and no amount or rate word.
- **Decision:** (1) `FwLawStage::new` accepts types 8 and 9 and sets `Mod2State::circular_taps` (`B4 = B7 = ring`, `L4 = L7 = 0x200` before each ENS call). The register state is ASSUMED (the dispatcher's state is undecoded, OQ21a); the opt-in `mod2` switch is the only way to reach it. (2) ENS has no rate or amount law: the ARM writes neither, so Rate and Amount do not change the output (test). (3) SPIN stays as built; the stale doc claim is corrected. (4) `FirmwareFx::mod2` stays opt-in and `render_hash` is unchanged (D22).
- **Why:** A stereo ensemble with a silent left channel is the worst parity error of the type; the circular taps are the only register state that makes the ported routine run both voices, and the choice is opt-in and tagged. Hardware parity of the left voice is unproven (no ENS clip, three factory programs use it).
- **Alternatives:** Keep ENS on the engine (rejected: engine ENS is a different model, ASSUMED throughout, and the firmware right voice is verified); flip `mod2` to default (rejected: D22, no clip for ENS, PHAS, FLANG, SPIN); fit an ENS rate or amount law (rejected: the ARM sends none).
- **Impact:** Opt-in `mod2` audio for types 8, 9 changes (firmware stage instead of engine). New tests: type to model mapping, determinism, no-rate/amount law, no allocation in steady state (`tests/no_alloc_audio_path.rs`). OQ21a keeps the dispatcher register state and the per-kind words.

### D97. The Synth SUSTPED default is on in the firmware and stored per program; the opt-in follows the program bit, the default render stays

- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent); no default render changes (D22)
- **Context:** D93 added `--synth-sustain-pedal` and left the hardware default of the Synth SUSTPED flag open ([synth-sustped](research/synth-sustped.md)).
- **Decision:** (1) Record the firmware default (`Edt_Syn_kSustainEnable` `0xF8`: 0..1, default 1) and the per-program bit (Synth layer block offset +71, blocks at section byte 327 bits 248 / 656 / 1064; 381 / 383 / 383 of 384 factory programs store 1, five exceptions). (2) Decode the bit (`cbin/synth_sustain.rs`, `synth_sustain` in `GlobalProgramDecoded`, serde default on) and let `--synth-sustain-pedal` apply the program bits (the firmware default without a program). (3) `tools/synth_sustped.py` and `data/synth-sustped-programs.csv` (derived numbers). (4) The engine default of `sustped` for the synth layers stays off.
- **Why:** The hardware default is on and Grand Pad stores 1, so the opt-in is the hardware behaviour for factory programs. D22 is not met for a default flip: the ARM note logic that consumes the flag is not traced (the DSP side is silent) and the clip is mixed (tail and hold closer, spectral score 1.42 to 1.68).
- **Alternatives:** Flip the default now (rejected: hold logic untraced, one mixed clip); keep the blanket opt-in without the program bit (rejected: it would hold the five programs whose bit is 0).
- **Impact:** `cbin/synth_sustain.rs` (tests), `cbin/presets.rs`, `engine/routing.rs`, `engine/mod.rs`, `render_opts.rs` (`apply_synth_sustain`, test), `tools/synth_sustped.py` (tests), `docs/research/synth-sustped.md`, `data/synth-sustped-programs.csv`, `midi-and-control.md`, OQ21b, [audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md) item 8, ROADMAP. Default renders and `render_hash` unchanged.

### D107. The FM table generator, the strip words and the pitch words are decoded; `FmVoice::select` follows the wave edit (opt-in)
- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task
- **Context:** D92 and D96 left the table family `(type, a4, a8)`, the writer of strip `+0xA4` / `+0xA8`, the note term `DM(0xC2716)` and the operator ratio source open (OQ22, audio-remaining row 10).
- **Decision:** (1) Port the ARM table generator (`0xC01CB2A4..`, a soft float loop of 129 indices building three curves) as `tools/synth_fm_generator.py` (30 of 30 variants bit exact against the ARM harness) and `fw_fm/generator.rs` (`fm_table_for`, within 1 ulp). (2) Record the writers: the `kOscWave` handler stores `+0xA8` at `0xC00185B0` and `+0xA4` at `0xC00185D8`; `tools/synth_fm_tables.py select` sweeps bank 1 and 2 x category x wave into [data/synth-fm-select.csv](research/data/synth-fm-select.csv); `FmVoice::select(bank, category, wave)` builds the voice from it. (3) `DM(0xC2716)` is a DSP store at pc `0x1208A9` of `(midi - 60) 2^24`; correct the pitch words of D96: `W` is zero at MIDI 60, `p0 = f / F_SY` (D96 / s6 said `2 f / F_SY` with zero at MIDI 48, an octave low); the `W` clip is `+-0x58000000`. (4) The `0x2D82A8` table is the OLED label table, the operator ratios are the P words. (5) No default change.
- **Why:** The generator is a pure function of three words, so a port replaces committing a table family; the pitch correction comes from the replay of the DSP note term for MIDI 48, 60 and 72.
- **Alternatives:** Commit tables for every partial ratio and type (rejected: 5 x 86 tables, and `a8` is a free word); fit the ramp and decay laws only (rejected: the third curve and the other types would stay guesses); flip the FM voice into `SynthLayer` (rejected: no hardware FM clip exists to validate level, D22).
- **Impact:** New `generator.rs`, `voice.rs` changes, `tools/synth_fm_generator.py`, `select` subcommand, two data files, tests, doc updates. `render_hash` and golden hashes are unchanged. Open (OQ22): level against a hardware FM clip, `x`, the `SynthLayer` hookup.

### D98. The Leslie drive words are ported (opt-in `--leslie-drive program`); the drive does not close the B3 residuals; the oracle cannot run `0x125371` / `0x1235AA`
- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task (B3 / Leslie leftovers)
- **Context:** After D94 the B3 clip still read mid +3, brilliance +5 and RMS -5 .. -7 dB under the firmware Leslie. The linearity probe had run with the drive words at 0 and the boot chain elements `0xB0476` / `0xB047E` had never run.
- **Decision:** (1) `Edt_OrgCom_kEffectsLeslieDrv` `v = 0..127` writes the `f32` `v / 127` into `DM(0xB2F2D / 0xB2F2E / 0xB2F31 / 0xB2F32)` of PNO core 1 and nothing else (ARM capture, [organ-preamp-search](research/organ-preamp-search.md) s10). (2) `FwLeslie::set_drive` writes them; the three 129 word tables `0xB0276 / 0xB02F7 / 0xB0378` and the window `0xE05D0..0xE0630` are now whole in the data files, because non-zero drive indexes all of them; 2,000 calls are bit exact against the oracle (golden `DRIVE`). (3) Opt-in `--leslie-drive program` feeds the program's Leslie drive (B3 Chords n Solo: 2.44 of 10, word `0.244`); the default stays the boot words (0). (4) No default changes (D22).
- **Why:** The drive stage is real (about +10 dB small signal and a hard knee at drive 1) but on the clip it moves presence +1.9 dB, brilliance +3 dB, RMS +1.1 dB and mid 0 dB, so the spectral score gets worse (4.06 to 5.02 merged, 4.22 to 5.69 audio offsets): it is not the missing piece, and whether the hardware program really runs it at 0.244 is unverified.
- **Alternatives:** Make the program drive the default (rejected: D22, score worse, one clip); skip the table windows and accept a mismatch (rejected: the port would silently read zeros).
- **Impact:** `fx/fw_leslie*.rs` (set_drive, windows), `organ/mod.rs` and `rotary.rs` (flag), `render_opts.rs` (`--leslie-drive`), goldens and fingerprints regenerated (`tools/fx_leslie_words.py`, `tools/fx_leslie_golden.py`); `render_hash` unchanged. Open (OQ21): `0x125371` and `0x1235AA` stop in `sharc-fx` (unimplemented instruction forms), so they stay unrun.
### D101. The FX default words are derived per block from the ARM edit table and applied opt-in; the engine defaults stay

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task (audio item 13)
- **Context:** The emulator's FX defaults (`LayerFxChain::new`, `FxDecoded::default`) were chosen by ear. The ARM edit-parameter table holds the firmware default word of every `Edt_*` id (the D97 method, SUSTPED).
- **Decision:** (1) Convert every FX default word with the emulator's laws and compare it with the Rust defaults: [data/fx-defaults.csv](research/data/fx-defaults.csv), generated by `tools/fx_defaults.py` (`--check` in the tests). 21 rows match, 17 differ, 4 are enable-flag representation, 2 are not compared. (2) Write the 8 differing chain fields from `fx/fw_defaults_table.rs` with `LayerFxChain::apply_firmware_defaults`, behind `--fx-defaults-firmware` (`FirmwareOptions::fx_defaults`). (3) Keep every default and `render_hash`.
- **Why:** The words are VERIFIED, but no hardware clip of an init patch shows that a fresh layer sounds from them, and program loads overwrite all of them, so the end-to-end bar of D22 is not met.
- **Alternatives:** Flip the defaults now (rejected: D22); also port the preset decoder defaults (rejected: file-format defaults, no audio effect); compare Mod 1 / Mod 2 rates in Hz (deferred: the law depends on the type).
- **Impact:** New `tools/fx_defaults.py`, `fx/fw_defaults.rs`, `fx/fw_defaults_table.rs`, [fx-defaults](research/fx-defaults.md); `render_opts.rs` gains `fx_defaults`. OQ34 holds the open init-patch check.

### D99. The Piano layer decoder reads the firmware bit layout; the Touch default stays off

- **Status:** proposed
- **Context:** D88 found the program Touch at file bit `1991..1992` (layer base + 63) but the Rust decoder read it at base + 73, so every factory program decoded as Medium. The neighbouring `variation`, `acoustic_level` and `pedal_noise` sat on shifted bits too.
- **Decision:** (1) `cbin/piano_preset.rs` reads and writes the layer block as `+26` variation (2b), `+60` `kAcoustic` (2b: bit 1 soft release, bit 0 string resonance, default 1), `+62` pedal noise, `+63` touch (0 heavy, 1 medium, 2 light, a stored 3 decodes as Medium), `+65` unison, `+67` dynamic compressor, `+70` EQ / timbre. (2) `firmware_touch` (`--firmware-piano-touch`) stays opt-in.
- **Why:** Single-bit flips of a factory program in the firmware recall reach the matching setters with the matching values for every field (table in [piano-touch-curves](research/piano-touch-curves.md) s4); the layout equals `ns4decode`; 12 real factory blocks are pinned in unit tests that fail on the old decoder; `ns4 preset-check` stays at 0 problems (544 presets, 384 programs) and `to_file_like` keeps unedited bits. The default does not flip (D22): the Light and Heavy curves have no hardware clip, and the sounds without a level model apply only the curve difference to the Medium response. Which bit of `kAcoustic` is soft release stays EMPIRICAL.
- **Alternatives:** Realign only the Touch bits (rejected: the neighbouring fields read the same shifted bits and would collide); flip `firmware_touch` now (rejected, see Why).
- **Impact:** A loaded program now carries its Touch, string resonance, soft release and pedal noise from the real bits (`PianoLayer::touch`, `string_resonance_enabled`, `soft_release`, `pedal_noise_enabled`); with the switch off only the sounds without a level model feel the Touch (ASSUMED powers). `render_hash` and the goldens use synthetic setups and do not change.

### D117. The program recall to level chain is verified end to end for a Heavy and a Light program; the Touch default stays off

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-16
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (task from the parent session, review notes applied; evidence [piano-touch-curves](research/piano-touch-curves.md) section 4a)
- **Context:** D99 verified the program file bits with the recall, but the level check (`arm-check`) still set `[ctx + 0x1C]` by hand. An `agy` read-only pass ranked the program recall for Heavy and Light programs and the ASSUMED fallback gain for non-`.npno` sounds as the weakest links of the opt-in Touch chain (D88, D22).
- **Decision:** (1) `tools/arm_program_touch.py` recalls a factory program through the firmware (no poke) and reads `curveId`, `T` and the level calls `0xC00A1EB4`; Pearl Upright (A:11, Heavy) gives `curveId` 10, Shimmer Upright (A:43, Light) gives 13. (2) `T`, `X` and the Q23 amplitudes of both voices equal `NpnoLevel::voices_touch` with `TouchCurve` in 18 of 18 rows (9 velocities x 2 programs), max error 0. (3) `nord-tools` gains `piano_touch_census` and `touch_model`; the factory set reads 156 / 208 / 20 (layer A), 0 invalid. (4) The stale ASSUMED note on the program file bits in `piano/touch_curve.rs` is replaced by the VERIFIED one (D99). (5) `firmware_touch` (`--firmware-piano-touch`) stays opt-in.
- **Why:** The recall to level chain is closed for `.npno` sounds, but D22 also needs the sounds without a level model (ASSUMED `touch_fallback_amp`), the System-menu MIDI velocity curves and a hardware clip of the Heavy and Light curves; none exists.
- **Alternatives:** Flip the default for `.npno` sounds only (rejected: renders would mix a verified and an assumed path, and the hardware level of the two curves is unmeasured); poke a second `curveId` and call it a recall (rejected: that is the old check).
- **Impact:** `tools/arm_program_touch.py` (+ test), `crates/nord-tools` (`touch_census`, bins `piano_touch_census`, `touch_model`), the `touch_curve.rs` provenance comment, research s4a and the blocker table in s6. No engine, golden or `render_hash` change.

### D116. SUPER LP24 factor 4.4 is not confirmed by three independent clips; 2.0 is added as an opt-in value; the default stays

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15 (findings, one opt-in switch, tool; no default changes, D22 not met)
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task; evidence [synth-super-lp24-clips](research/synth-super-lp24-clips.md), [super_lp24_clips.json](research/data/super_lp24_clips.json)
- **Context:** D81 fitted `SUPER_LP24_TYPE_SCALE = 4.4` on `Horizon Whl` (A:16) alone, entangled with the SUPER row factor 0.45; D108 left it EMPIRICAL. Three more factory programs with a SUPER layer on LP24 appear in the Part A video (A:25 Sidechain Whl II, A:76 Oo Pad Whl, A:81 Believer).
- **Decision:** (1) Four segments `super_lp24_*` (grid of `--synth-super-lp24` 1.0 / 1.5 / 2.0 / 2.2 / 3.0 / 6.0, `--synth-filter-law` 1.015 / 0.507, `--synth-fw-resonance`) in `emu_vs_hw_segments.json`; A:25 is cut at 622 s (the player turns the filter knob from 624 s, OLED `Filter Frequency`), A:76 at 2175 s, A:81 at 2325 s. (2) Result under two note lists (video-detected, Transkun): 4.4 is not stable. Horizon and Sidechain prefer 1.0 to 3.0 (Horizon 1.89 to 0.74 / 0.85, Sidechain 4.32 to 2.87); Oo Pad is not usable for the factor (layer B, LP12 Saw, carries the sound; the SUPER layer is 18 dB below the mix) and Believer is insensitive; Sidechain is usable for brightness only (Mod 1 PUMP at 2.11 Hz is missing in the default render, level +5.7 dB, `--firmware-fx` free-runs it at 6.1 Hz); an `agy` note list hypothesis (user confirmed) was truncated and at chance, not used; 2.0 is within 0.11 (video-detected) / 0.41 (Transkun) of the best grid value on all four clips; four-clip mean 3.17 / 3.95 at 4.4 against 2.44 / 2.58 at 2.0. Resonance (Oo Pad 0.61) is the only clip where `--synth-fw-resonance` helps and it hurts the low-resonance clips; its optimum factor does not move. (3) Opt-in `--synth-super-lp24 <factor>` (`SynthLayer::super_lp24_scale`, `row_type_cutoff_scale_with`, tests); `tools/note_list_check.py` scores a note list against the hardware audio (chroma, with a time-shift null). (4) The note lists are not verified (lift over the null 0.01 to 0.08, no list better than chance with confidence; Transkun at chance on three clips); the conclusion is drawn only where both lists agree. (5) The default stays 4.4.
- **Why:** D22 needs a value verified end to end. The evidence is two informative clips, an unread mod wheel, unverified note lists and a fit clip whose own optimum moved since D81 (D102 changed the voice cutoff law afterwards); that argues against 4.4, but it does not make 2.0 a derived value. **A flip to 2.0 would need:** a trusted note list per clip (hardware pitch track or a second transcriber, section 3 of the note), the player's wheel position, at least one clip outside the four (a hold-out, the other seven factory SUPER / LP24 programs have none), `render_hash` and the goldens regenerated in the same change, or the stage-level derivation of D108 section 4.
- **Alternatives:** Flip to 2.0 now (rejected: see Why); drop the factor and use `--synth-filter-law` (rejected: mean 2.88 / 3.52, behind 2.0); fit a per-clip factor (rejected: it would hide the entanglement with the row factor).
- **Impact:** `p_type.rs` (`row_type_cutoff_scale_with`, doc update, test), `synth/mod.rs`, `render.rs`, `render_opts.rs` (`--synth-super-lp24`, tests), `tools/note_list_check.py` (+ test), segments, note, [synth-engine](specs/synth-engine.md) s4.6, OQ22, audio-remaining row 12, ROADMAP. Default render, `render_hash` and goldens unchanged (the override is `None` by default).
### D115. The Transkun synth adapters are not adopted; the base model stays; the dataset has defects and the cause of the hardware gap is open

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (follow-up task from the parent session; evidence [transkun-synth-finetune-plan](research/transkun-synth-finetune-plan.md) section 10)
- **Context:** Phase 2 trained a LoKr (r16, 1.04 M parameters) and a LoRA (r16, 1.87 M) adapter on 20 h of emulator renders. Held-out emulator F1 rose (base 0.545, LoKr 0.629, LoRA 0.748), but recall at +-150 ms on the three hardware clips fell below base on every clip (base 0.203 / 0.146 / 0.233; LoRA 0.006 / 0.042 / 0.082; LoKr 0.012 / 0.035 / 0.062). Stop rule 7.3 step 3 applies.
- **Decision:** (1) Neither adapter is adopted; the shipped Transkun 2.0.1 weights stay the only transcriber and the piano path is untouched. (2) The negative T5 result is not final for the method, only for this dataset. `synth_dataset.py` renders without `--backup` (CONFIRMED on Grand Pad: placeholder piano, peak 0.23, RMS 0.048; with the backup Astoria Grand + White Grand, peak 0.51, RMS 0.080), but this is not the main cause: on 40 val items rendered both ways the LoRA F1 is 0.762 without and 0.748 with the backup (base 0.571 both). The cause of the hardware gap is open; label mismatch (octave / split / arp), density, polyphony and noise in the hardware reference are suspected, not measured. (3) The next experiment is a corrected dataset (labels after octave / split / arp, denser overlapping phrases, `--backup` renders) plus the mixed-data run; it awaits the user's approval and has not started. No further training starts until the user approves a corrected dataset (backup renders, labels after octave shift / split / arpeggiator, overlapping polyphony, pedal handling).
- **Why:** Hardware recall is the goal (T5); the emulator split cannot stand in for it while its sound differs from the hardware clips. Scaling the adapter from 0 to 1 degrades hardware recall monotonically, so no partial blend helps.
- **Alternatives:** Full fine-tune now (rejected: the stop rule forbids it and the data defect would be baked in); ship the LoRA for synth only (rejected: below base on all three clips).
- **Impact:** Tools `synth_train` (resume, WSD schedule), `synth_eval`, `synth_hw_eval`, `synth_vel_check`, `synth_adapter` (scaling); plan section 10, ROADMAP, audio-remaining row 14. No renderer change; no weights, audio or datasets committed.

### D114. ENS needs no tap registers: the model wrapped a pre-modify; the circular taps are removed

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task (audio item 6, OQ21a); evidence [fx-ens-tap-registers](research/fx-ens-tap-registers.md)
- **Context:** D95 / D109 left `B4 / L4 / B7 / L7` of ENS (`0x123A7B`) ASSUMED because no code sets them and the oracle state (`L4 = 0`) silenced the left voice. The task: search the full core 1 and core 2 images (no slice), name the writer or prove there is none, check PHAS 1.
- **Decision:** (1) Record the full-image search: no write of `L4` / `B4` in either core below `0x180000` (raw parcels `0x0FB4`, `0x0FC4`, listings of 232k lines per core), no call or pointer to `0x123A7B` / `0x123BD0` outside the ARM stage record, and the library init selects the secondary DAG banks without initialising `L4` / `B4`. (2) Show that nothing has to set them: ENS stores its second ring with the register pre-modify `dm(m6, i6)` (`I6 + 0x202`), which does not wrap (PRM fig 6-5; the oracle `moves.rs`); the buffer is two `0x200`-word rings plus two guard words each, `0x404` words, as the init clears it. (3) Fix the model: `Pe::plain` and the Python `ops()` pre-modify are plain adds; `Mod2State::circular_taps` is removed; the ENS and SPIN goldens are regenerated; `fx_models.py check ens` / `spin` go from 0/100 to 200/200 against the current oracle. (4) PHAS 1 needs nothing (oracle 100/100, boot row words). (5) ENS stays opt-in (`mod2`, D22): no hardware clip.
- **Why:** The only evidence that the left voice needs circular taps was a model that wrapped a pre-modify against the PRM and the oracle. With the PRM semantics the routine is oracle-exact at the reset registers, uses the second ring it clears, and needs `L4 = 0` for its guard-word copy. An ASSUMED register state is replaced by none.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the circular taps as ASSUMED (rejected: contradicts the PRM and the routine's own buffer layout); run the ARM module install first (not needed: the ARM writes only the pc and data words).
- **Impact:** `fw_pe_ops.rs`, `fw_mod2_run.rs`, `fw_mod2_law_stage.rs`, `tests_mod2_golden.rs` (ENS, SPIN), `tools/fx_leslie_ops.py` and a test; supersedes the circular-tap parts of D95 and D109; [fx-mod2-validation](research/fx-mod2-validation.md) s5, [fx-mod2-types](research/fx-mod2-types.md) s8.3, [fx-mod1-validation](research/fx-mod1-validation.md) s3, OQ21a, [audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md) row 6, ROADMAP. The `mod2` output of ENS (left voice now runs) and SPIN changes; defaults and `render_hash` unchanged.

### D112. The real packed sample stream decodes in the SY unpacker and carries no pitch entry; the handler sweep finds no writer; `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (follow-up task from the parent session; evidence [sy-pitch-entry-writer](research/sy-pitch-entry-writer.md) section 8)
- **Context:** D111 left the real SDRAM stream through the external-port DMA as the best open lead for the writer of `e`.
- **Decision:** (1) `sharc-ref stream VOICE path=FILE` models the DMA with the real `.nsmp4` stroke payload (big endian words byte swapped); the first 32 frames of the ring equal `sample_dump` times `2^-6` after the tail filter (max error 1.686e-11, VERIFIED). (2) The stream has no pitch word; 400 ISRs write nothing into `0x93500..0x935A0`. (3) 224 header fills over all unpacker handlers (`refill VOICE fill=WORD`), vectors 1 and 12 and opcode 9 write nothing into it. (4) `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in; no engine change, no golden or `render_hash` moves. (5) Open: blocks with `ns` not a multiple of 32 desynchronise the model after the first block.
- **Why:** D22 needs a written value verified end to end; none exists to port. The stream result closes the page-borne lead.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the synthetic header only (rejected: it cannot show whether the page carries `e`).
- **Impact:** `sharc-ref` commands `stream` and `refill fill=`, scenario `sy_isr_stream.scn`, research section 8, OQ22, ROADMAP, audio-remaining row 2; no render change.

### D111. The SY initialisation script is replayed and the ring refill modelled; still no writer of the pitch entry; `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (follow-up task from the parent session; evidence [sy-pitch-entry-writer](research/sy-pitch-entry-writer.md) section 7)
- **Context:** D105 blocked the SHARC ISR sweep on the unmodelled ring refill and named the replay of the ARM initialisation script as the next step.
- **Decision:** (1) The SY part of the script (`0xC0503740 .. 0xC05038B0`, 53 words) is replayed from the firmware by `tools/ns4_arm_init_script.py`; it supplies the unpacker pointer table `DM(0x95C7B ..)` and the scan pointer, and no record covers the pitch table. (2) The refill is modelled by `sharc-ref refill VOICE` (a valid synthetic block header in the 128-word staging buffer `DM(rec + 0x2F)`, ASSUMED), so 400 audio ISRs run through the scheduler and the unpacker without the `rec+0x10` poke. (3) Under `--watch 0x93500:0xA0`: vectors 0 to 31, the opcode handlers 1 to 15 (bodies run directly; the core does not take the call at the loop end `0x1C064B`) and 545 ARM messages (six note-ons, 3000 idle ticks) write nothing into the table. (4) `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in; no engine change, no golden or `render_hash` moves.
- **Why:** D22 needs a written value verified end to end; none exists to port. The remaining unmodelled path is the real SDRAM sample stream through the external-port DMA.
- **Alternatives:** Poke a table value (rejected, restates the model); model the real packed format first (deferred: the sweep does not need it).
- **Impact:** New tool, `sharc-ref` commands `refill` / `dump` / `regs`, two committed scenarios, research section 7, OQ22, TD-08, ROADMAP, audio-remaining row 2; no render change.

### D105. The SY pitch entry writer is still not found; the table index is the voice number; `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15
- **Decided by:** Yuri Olive (follow-up task from the parent session; evidence [sy-pitch-entry-writer](research/sy-pitch-entry-writer.md) section 6)
- **Context:** D89 left the writer of the 16-bit entry `e` at `DM(0x9354A + index)` open and listed follow-up checks (pool of slot objects, allocator, SHARC ISRs, other members).
- **Decision:** (1) The pool object index is allocated at note-on by `0xC01E31C8` (stores at `0xC01E3AB8..`), and equals the voice number; the pool stride is `0x20`. (2) No ARM message, literal, packer or SY / SMP store writes the window in any run here (cold boot, load, bind, note on / off, pitch bend, mod wheel, sustain). (3) The SHARC ISR sweep is blocked by the unmodelled ring refill; the replay of the ARM initialisation script is the next step and is recorded, not done. (4) `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in; no engine change, no golden or `render_hash` moves.
- **Why:** D22 needs the written value verified end to end; no written value exists to port. The step model stays within 0.5 % of the semitone law.
- **Alternatives:** Invent the missing refill path in the harness (rejected: would restate the model); flip the model (rejected, as D65 / D89).
- **Impact:** Docs only (research section 6, OQ22, TD-08, ROADMAP, audio-remaining row 2); no code, test or render change.

### D104. Synth velocity follows the ORG core 2 laws behind `--firmware-synth-velocity`; the default stays off

- **Status:** proposed
- **Context:** The 2026-11-15 listening check showed that synth velocity is not captured right: the engine blends `amp_velocity / 3` linearly with `g / 127` (ASSUMED) and scales the filter envelope with the linear velocity. Bean `ns4-9l1f` asked how the firmware uses note velocity per program and layer.
- **Decision:** (1) Only three synth ids carry velocity: `kAmpEnvVel` (0..3), `kFltEnvVel` and `kOscEnvVel` per layer; the Synth has no Touch curve. (2) The ORG core 2 pass turns the ARM key byte `g` into the amp gain `V` at `DM(0xC10B4)`: `bits(V) = bits(a) + ((bits(a + b) - bits(a)) * G >> 7)` with the strip words `a`, `b` of the setting (`-6 s` dB at `g = 0`, `+2 s` dB at the top, setting 0 flat) and into the filter-envelope factor `F = G / 128` at `DM(0xC2723)` (`G = g`, `128` for `g = 127`). (3) `SynthLayer::firmware_velocity` (`--firmware-synth-velocity`, default off) uses `V` for the amp and `F` for the filter envelope depth (`synth/fw_velocity.rs`); derived series in `docs/research/data/synth-velocity-law.csv` (`tools/synth_velocity_law.py --check`) and the factory census in `synth-program-velocity.csv` (`synth_velocity_census`).
- **Why:** 768 of 768 oracle words equal the law within 1 float ulp ([synth-velocity](research/synth-velocity.md)). The default does not flip (D22): that `V` and `F` multiply the voice the way the engine applies them is ASSUMED, the file bits of the three fields are taken equal to the edit value by analogy with the Touch, the oscillator envelope velocity is not isolated, and no clip varies the synth velocity.
- **Alternatives:** Flip the default now (rejected: not verified end to end, `render_hash` would change for every synth program); model only the amp (rejected: the filter law is just as cheap and Grand Pad is a filter velocity program).
- **Impact:** `synth/fw_velocity.rs`, `SynthLayer::firmware_velocity`, `FirmwareOptions::synth_velocity_fw`, `program_sweep --synth-velocity`. Defaults, goldens and `render_hash` unchanged. Census: 213 of 287 factory programs with a synth layer have a velocity sensitive layer (amp or filter); amp 40 to 100 gains of 4.9 / 10.0 / 15.8 dB for settings 1 / 2 / 3 against 2.4 / 6.6 / 18.2 dB of the older law.

### D102. The synth cutoff with a sounding voice is exponential; no type factor is replaced yet

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15 (findings and tool; no default changes)
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent); evidence [synth-cutoff-voice-law](research/synth-cutoff-voice-law.md)
- **Context:** The cutoff type factors (LP12 / BP 1.4, Ladder24 0.8, SUPER LP24 4.4, `p_type.rs`) were fitted on clips. The ARM word `(knob - 52) << 24` is type independent, and the idle pass maps it linearly (`R` ramp), which disagreed with the displayed Hz (4.8 kHz against 277 Hz at knob 52).
- **Decision:** Record the voice-state law as VERIFIED (ORG core 2 in `sharc-org` with the key word of `0xC01EC690` in `DM(0xC1DCC)`): `atan(R)` is exponential in the knob, 12 knobs per octave (max deviation 0.0023 semitone, 128 knobs), `fc_nat = 140.626 Hz * 2^((knob - 52 + trk (note - 60)) / 12)`, key tracking `trk` in `{0, 1/3, 2/3, 1}`; display over `fc_nat` = 1.971. Keep every fitted factor and every default; add `tools/synth_cutoff_note_probe.py`, its test and the table.
- **Why:** The law of the voice is derived, but the Hz unit of the Rust stages needs one more fact (stage calls per DSP frame, `F` against `2 F`), and the resonance word to table position and the `kFltType` to stage link are still ASSUMED; replacing the factors without them would swap one fit for another (D22).
- **Alternatives:** feed `fc_nat` into the engine now (rejected: the unit is open and would move the three clips by a factor of about 2); drop the factors (rejected: no clip evidence).
- **Impact:** docs, one tool, one test, one data table; OQ22 and audio-remaining item 12 updated; no Rust change, no `render_hash` change. The four missing captures are listed in the research note s4.

### D106. Organ `K` per engine measured on the Transkun streams; no ARM or SHARC gain word found, `K = 2.8` stays EMPIRICAL
- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task
- **Context:** D85 left `K` open after the image audit. The follow-up asked for the ARM write capture, the FX mixer and hand-over, the ARM integer-table gains, and `K` per engine with the Transkun streams.
- **Decision:** (1) The 96,111 logged ARM-to-DSP writes hold no writer of `DM(0xB6518)`, no organ lane gain and no word tied to `2.8`, `2.729`, `1 / 0.21875`, `0.357` or `0.597` outside filter and parameter regions ([organ-output-law](research/organ-output-law.md) s6.3). (2) `K` needed per engine on the Transkun stream, whole-segment RMS: Vox 1.7 to 1.8, Pipe organ-only 3.6 to 6.2, B3 4.9 to 8.4 (rotary drive); data in `docs/research/data/organ_k_per_engine.json`. (3) `K = 2.8`, the `Chain` opt-in and every default stay as they are.
- **Why:** The 13 dB spread follows the rotary drive and the velocity scale, so a new constant would be another fit, not a derivation (D22 not met).
- **Alternatives:** Per-engine `K` defaults (rejected: Transkun velocities are ordinal, B3 and Pipe are not linear in `K`); promote `Chain` (rejected: 12 to 20 dB under the clips).
- **Impact:** Docs and data only; no audio or `render_hash` change. Not run: Piano to Organ program-recall ARM capture, PNO lane to FX hand-over (OQ24, audio-remaining row 3).


### D109. The ENS init and the loader kernels do not set the tap registers; ENS stays opt-in with ASSUMED circular taps; the missing run is named
- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task (audio item 6)
- **Context:** D100 left the ENS tap registers (`B4 / L4 / B7 / L7`) ASSUMED. The task was to read where they are written (the ENS init), run the init from the module reset state and compare the tap values.
- **Decision:** (1) Record the init (`0x123BD0`): it sets `B7 = c[-0x20]`, `L7 = 0x404`, zero-fills from `I7 = c[-0x1C]`, stores `I7` back and restores `L7 = 0`; it never writes `B4` / `L4`. (2) Record that the CCES loader kernels zero `L0, L4, L8, L12, L15` explicitly, so a booted image starts with `L4 = 0` (the oracle's state). (3) Record two negative experiments (PEy address `A ^ 1`; weights non-zero), and that PHAS 1 and every Mod 1 / Mod 2 type are now compared with the ARM words (D90, D100). (4) Keep ENS in `FwLawStage::new` with circular taps as an ASSUMED opt-in; no code change. (5) Name the missing run in OQ21a: a DSP-side pass with the ARM type switch to `kFx2Type` 8 (the module install that sets the dispatcher's `I8`).
- **Why:** The only register state under which the ported routine runs both voices (circular `L4 = L7 = 0x200`) is not produced by any code in the image; the state the code does produce silences the left voice of a shipped stereo effect, so the model, the dispatcher context or an install step is incomplete. Guessing further would be fitting.
- **Alternatives:** Drop ENS from `mod2` (rejected: the right voice and all words are exact, the engine ENS is ASSUMED throughout); set `L4 = 0` as verified (rejected: left silent, contradicts the product).
- **Impact:** Docs only; `render_hash` unchanged.

### D110. The slew rate word is a stroke record field; the oracle chain equals the emulator and has no attack word

- **Status:** accepted
- **Context:** The piano attack gap (chain + slew 62 to 69 ms against 56 to 64 ms hardware) was to be located by running the firmware oracle (ARM note-on words into the SMP image) for single notes and comparing it with the emulator, not the video.
- **Decision:** (1) The oracle gain is flat from the first update, so its attack is the decoded sample's own onset, the same as the emulator's: no upstream attack word exists. (2) The ARM stores the hold-decay count of a voice as `F17 = 1 - d / 2^24`, `d = 2 * round(-s * 2^(semitones / 12))`, `s` the big-endian `i16` at stroke record byte `0x3C`, `semitones` the playback pitch above the stroke root: 48 of 48 Felt Upright and 7 of 7 Velvet Grand firmware points match (nearest rounding and the cents term ASSUMED). The slew voice (`--piano-slew-fw`) uses it (`slew_voice.rs::rate_from_record`, `npno_level.rs::VoiceLevel::decay`) in place of the Velvet-only `rate_for` table. (3) Defaults, `render_hash` and goldens unchanged.
- **Why:** The old table was fitted to Velvet Grand note-on words and applied to every sound: up to 21 % off on Velvet and 73 % off on Felt Upright (note 57, velocity 100: `d = 184` against 318). The clips barely move (Velvet 68.9 ms / 3.74, Felt 62.1 ms / 1.31, as before) because the 0.5 s hold and 0.25 s release windows are insensitive to a 2 to 5 s time constant; the attack gap is not in the SMP chain.
- **Alternatives:** an attack ramp fitted to the clips (rejected: the oracle has none); flipping chain + slew (rejected: the two clips still disagree, D22); keeping the Velvet table (rejected: wrong for other sounds).
- **Impact:** `slew_voice.rs`, `npno_level.rs`, `extra_voice.rs` (tests `rate_law_matches_the_firmware_words`, `felt_upright_note_on_gains_follow_the_oracle`); [piano-attack-oracle](research/piano-attack-oracle.md); OQ20, ROADMAP, `audio-remaining` row 11, `docs/specs/piano-engine.md`, [smp-slew](research/smp-slew.md).


### D108. The synth filter type factors are not stage physics; the resonance word maps to table position 47.38 to 80.62

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-15 (findings, tools and two opt-in switches; no default changes, D22 not met)
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task; evidence [synth-filter-type-corners](research/synth-filter-type-corners.md)
- **Context:** D102 showed the cutoff of a sounding voice is exponential and type independent in the ARM word, so any per-type factor must live in the SHARC stage. The fitted factors LP12 / BP 1.4, Ladder24 0.8, SUPER LP24 4.4 (`synth/p_type.rs`) were to be derived from the stages.
- **Decision:** (1) Every filter stage (`0x1253BD`, `0x12566E`, `0x125913`, `0x125A50`) is a bilinear section of the frame word `R` with the nominal cutoff `atan(R) / pi`; the shape around it depends on the damping `P(idx)`. `tools/synth_filter_type_corners.py` (generator, `--check`, oracle-match test against the bit-exact models) writes [data/synth-filter-type-corners.csv](research/data/synth-filter-type-corners.csv): at equal `R` and position LP12 sits 1.10 to 1.12 times above LP24 (not 1.4), the band pass peaks at the nominal cutoff, the high pass corner is 0.72 to 0.82. There is no per-type cutoff factor in the firmware. (2) The pass maps the `kFltRes` word to the stage position `idx = 64 + 33.25 (knob / 127 - 0.5)`, 47.38 to 80.62 (`tools/synth_filter_res_probe.py`, oracle run, fit error 0); the engine map `TablePos::from_resonance` (0..127) is ASSUMED and wrong at the idle LFO scale. (3) Opt-in `--synth-filter-law <scale>` (one factor on the display Hz for every type and row, plus the firmware position; `1.015` is the `2 F` reading, `0.507` the `F` reading) and `--synth-fw-resonance` (position only). Both default off.
- **Why:** The stage equations are verified word for word, so a type factor that the equations do not contain cannot be derived; the clips decide the rest. Over 12 clips the default (fitted) mean score is 2.18, `--synth-filter-law 1.015` 3.88, `0.507` 5.42, `--synth-fw-resonance` 3.20 (one clip better, five worse): D22 (default flips only when verified end to end) is not met, so nothing flips and the fitted factors stay EMPIRICAL.
- **Alternatives:** Replace the factors with the derived uniform one by default (rejected: 12 clip mean 2.18 to 3.88); fit a new per-type table (rejected: the missing terms are captures, not fits); drop the fitted factors silently (rejected).
- **Impact:** `synth/fw_filter_law.rs` (constants, tests), `SynthLayer::{fw_filter_law, fw_resonance}`, `render_opts.rs` flags (tests), `render.rs` (two branches, default path unchanged), `tools/synth_filter_type_corners.py`, `tools/synth_filter_res_probe.py` and their tests, `emu_vs_hw_segments.json` (two variants per synth clip), docs. `render_hash` unchanged. Open (OQ22): stage calls per frame (`F` or `2 F`), the LFO scale and strip words 10 / 20 in `W28`, the `kFltType` to stage link, the Ladder24 ARM word to its log-frequency position, SUPER LP24 4.4.

### D113. The 13 dB organ `K` spread is the replay stimulus, not the engine; band, gate and matched-range controls explain none of it; one `K` stays
- **Status:** accepted
- **Decided by:** Claude (agent), on the parent session's task
- **Context:** D106 measured `K` needed per engine on the Transkun streams as Vox 1.7 to 1.8, Pipe 3.6 to 6.2, B3 4.9 to 8.4 and read the spread as rotary drive plus velocity scale. The question was which part is a measurement effect (band, sub, horn hole, silence, note range, stereo downmix) and which is the engine.
- **Decision:** (1) Controls recomputed offline on the cached clips with `tools/organ_k_controls.py` ([organ-k-controls](research/organ-k-controls.md)): a 160 Hz to 1.3 kHz band, silent-frame gating, note-active frames and a note-pure range applied to the emulator and the hardware alike change `K` needed by at most 1 to 3 dB per engine and leave the cross-engine spread at 11 to 14 dB. (2) The emulator level is linear in `K` in that band (slope 0.97 to 1.00 between `K = 2.8` and chain 0.597, `k_needed` gap 0.03 to 0.48 dB); the D106 "B3 and Pipe are not linear" reading came from out-of-band content (broadband gap 2.2 dB, crest delta -10 dB on Pipe), so no rotary or compressor nonlinearity is shown. (3) On the 36 to 101 video-confirmed notes at uniform velocity 100 the same renders need B3 3.5, Vox 2.3, Pipe 2.7 (3.7 dB spread, all within 2 dB of 2.8); the Transkun stream carries 0.86 to 1.7 concurrent notes against 1.7 to 5.5, and rescaling it to the video concurrency cuts the spread from 13.8 to 6.7 dB. (4) A single `K = 2.8` is the supportable value; a per-engine `K` has no demonstrated physical cause. (5) No default changes (D22); `K` stays EMPIRICAL.
- **Why:** The spread follows the note stream (density, duration, concurrency) more than any measurement control or engine path, and the B3 rotary drive law alone moves `K` needed by 4.8 dB (6.1 unity against 3.5 engine on the video notes), more than the 3.7 dB cross-engine spread there.
- **Alternatives:** Per-engine `K` table (rejected: fits the replay choice, not a firmware word); read the nonlinearity as Leslie drive (rejected: slope 0.97 to 1.00 in band); promote the Transkun-rescaled `K` (rejected: ASSUMED incoherent-sum scaling, Vox residual 6.7 dB unexplained).
- **Impact:** `tools/organ_k_dsp.py`, `tools/organ_k_controls.py`, their tests, `docs/research/data/organ_k_{segments,controls_transkun,controls_video}.json`, OQ24, audio-remaining row 3. No audio or `render_hash` change. Not done: `agy` and a second hand-written script both reproduce the band numbers to 0.01 dB; the Piano to Organ program-recall ARM capture stays out of scope.

### D119. The SHARC oracle becomes the DSP half of a minimal system run (opt-in, ORG Core2 only, no default change)

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-16
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session, review notes applied; evidence [org-system-run](research/org-system-run.md))
- **Context:** [sharc-core](specs/sharc-core.md) defined the oracle as a routine-level test oracle, "not a full-system emulator". The 8-voice pass runs started voices by poking stage-A slot pointers (`DM(0xC10B8 + 0x18 k) = 0x1215FB`), a proxy that no ARM message produces, and nobody had measured the ARM side, the DMA or the interrupts next to the SHARC pass loop, so real-time feasibility of the whole system was unknown.
- **Decision:** (1) The oracle may run as the DSP half of a minimal system run: the ARM Unicorn harness (`tools/arm_org_stream.py`) boots the firmware with a factory program, captures the real ARM to DSP messages per 1 ms tick, and `sharc-sys` (`runner/system.rs`) replays the ORG Core2 messages between interpreter passes and times every component. (2) Scope: ORG Core2 only, interpreter only, one SHARC core, ARM in Unicorn, everything opt-in (new binary and tools; no existing behaviour changes). (3) Interrupts, the audio and external-port DMA and the other cores stay unmodelled; the only DMA stand-ins are an input-refresh stub and a pre-set of `DM(0x3E021)` (section 5 of the note). (4) The measured numbers are EMPIRICAL (this host, noisy), the frame rate and 2 frames per pass stay ASSUMED for ORG Core2.
- **Why:** The real stream is the only way to know which ORG code runs after a program load and whether a key press reaches ORG at all; the measurement gives the first whole-system real-time figure before any optimisation (D118 whole-pass AOT) is judged.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the poke proxy (rejected: the real load runs 6,377 steps per pass and a different code range, the proxy runs 4,265 to 5,953 and never the real one); model the audio and external-port DMA first (rejected for this step: needs the register map, open); extend `sharc-org` instead of a new binary (rejected: it snapshots 0.7 M words per pass, which would distort the timing).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-sharc` (`runner/system.rs`, bin `sharc-sys`, one `pub mod` line in `runner/mod.rs`), `tools/arm_org_stream.py`, `tools/org_system_timing.py` (+ tests), `docs/research/org-system-run.md`, `docs/research/data/org-system-*`, [sharc-core](specs/sharc-core.md) section 7. No engine, golden, `render_hash` or default change.

### D120. DSP peripherals for the system run: an opt-in range-register block, a census and a lane capture; the PNO lane is live, ORG Core2 stays silent

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-16
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session, review notes applied; evidence [dsp-peripherals-audio](research/dsp-peripherals-audio.md))
- **Context:** D119 preset `DM(0x3E021)` for ORG Core2 and left the PNO lane silent. The question was which peripheral registers and DMAs the real passes need for live audio.
- **Decision:** (1) `core::periph::epdma` models `DM(0x3E000..0x3E03F)`: a `DM(0x3E010)` write with `DM(0x3E002)` bits 1:0 set opens a range, a read of `DM(0x3E021)` returns the opened count and clears it (`--epdma`, ASSUMED semantics). (2) `core::periph::access_log` counts every access outside SRAM (`--census`). (3) `--sec-id ID` raises the pass-start through the SEC model. (4) `--lane SET:PAIR` / `--wav` capture the PNO output pointer set after each pass. (5) The PNO silence of D119 was the missing boot message log, not a peripheral; with it the lane is live and follows the keys. ORG Core2 is silent by construction (null stage slots). Everything is opt-in; no default changes (D119 rule).
- **Why:** The census shows 5 accesses per PNO pass outside SRAM (all start-up handshake) and a 6-range start/count handshake for ORG Core2; no data DMA is needed. `--epdma` equals the old preset (0 differing SRAM words, same steps).
- **Alternatives:** Model the SPORT transmit DMA and the FX hand-over (rejected: not needed to read the lane); model audio input buffer ping-pong for ORG Core2 (rejected: sources unknown, slots are null); keep `--pre` (kept as is, still works).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-sharc` (`core/periph/{epdma,access_log}.rs`, `glue.rs`, `mod.rs`, `runner/periph_run.rs`, bins `sharc-sys`; `runner/system.rs` one `start()` arm), `tools/dsp_lane_vs_hw.py` (+ test), [sharc-core](specs/sharc-core.md) section 7, OQ34. Hardware numbers (lane vs `organ_b3` clip, 8 keys): RMS delta 0.0 dB, centroid 361 / 338 Hz, gap score 11.0 dB; the sub band is +27.5 dB. No audio or default change.

### D121. The synth filter chain links are measured: the type is an identity stage table, the resonance position is `128 knob / 127`, the stage runs 16 calls per pass; the fitted factors stay

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-16
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session, review notes applied; evidence [synth-filter-links](research/synth-filter-links.md))
- **Context:** D108 left three captures open before the EMPIRICAL per-type cutoff factors could be replaced by firmware words: the `kFltType` link, the resonance word `W28`, the stage call rate. The opt-in derived laws scored 3.88 and 3.20 against 2.18 for the fitted ones on 12 clips.
- **Decision:** (1) `kFltType`: strip word 1 is `STAGE_TABLE[file type]`, an identity table (18 of 18 layers of six factory programs on the real firmware), the selector is copied `+0x74 -> +0x7C` by a block copy; no per-type factor exists in any ARM word. Raw type 3 is the unmodelled stage `0x1257B2` (61 of 988 filter-on factory layers; `filter_model_of_type` plays it as LP12). (2) Resonance: the pass smooths `kFltRes` (`0.995 w10 + 0.005 w`) and the settled position is `128 knob / 127`, clipped at `127.999`; the 47.4 .. 80.6 range of D108 was the pass-59 transient; voice, envelope amount, LFO amount and strip words 10 / 20 add nothing. `fw_filter_law` now uses the settled position (supersedes the resonance part of D108). (3) Stage calls: 16 per pass for every stage, 4 samples each; the stage rate is `2 F` if a pass is two frames (the pass = 2 frames reading stays ASSUMED). (4) Opt-in `--synth-fw-filter` (= `--synth-filter-law 1.015` with the settled resonance). No default, golden or `render_hash` change.
- **Why:** The corrected chain scores 3.36 on the 12 clips against 2.18 for the fitted factors (the settled resonance alone: 2.17, neutral), so D22 is not met and the factors stay EMPIRICAL. The remaining gap sits in the row and type cutoff factors of the LP24 / SUPER / BP clips, which no ARM word explains.
- **Alternatives:** Flip the default to the measured chain (rejected: 2.18 to 3.36); drop the resonance opt-in (rejected: it now equals the default within one index and documents the link); model `0x1257B2` in this change (rejected: separate oracle work).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-core` (`synth/fw_filter_law.rs`, `synth/fw_filter.rs` clamp, `render_opts.rs`), `tools/arm_synth_filter_probe.py`, `tools/synth_filter_links.py`, `tools/synth_filter_type_link.py` (+ tests), `docs/research/synth-filter-links.md`, `docs/research/data/synth-{filter-type-link,res-position,stage-calls}.csv`, [synth-engine](specs/synth-engine.md) s4.6, `emu_vs_hw_segments.json` (variant `synth-fw-filter`).

### D122. The synth filter stages match the Rust port, the per-type factors are not a port error, the Ladder24 position in a sounding voice is open

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-16
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session, review notes applied; evidence [synth-filter-stage-vs-model](research/synth-filter-stage-vs-model.md))
- **Context:** D121 measured the filter links and left `--synth-fw-filter` at 3.36 against 2.18 mean gap score, so the remaining gap was thought to sit in the row and type cutoff factors of `p_table.rs` / `p_type.rs`, with the hypothesis that those factors compensate a port error in the Rust stage model (`fw_filter.rs`, `fw_ladder.rs`).
- **Decision:** (1) Drive each real stage (`0x1253BD`, `0x12566E`, `0x12549D`, `0x125913`, `0x125A50`) in the ORG core 2 oracle with the frame words a sounding voice gives (voice-law `R`, settled `W28`, 7 cutoffs x 9 resonances x 1024 samples) and diff it against `FwCoefs` / `FwFilter`: the worst difference is 3.3e-5 (LP12), 3.9e-5 (LP24), 3.6e-5 (BP), 2.1e-5 (HP) and 1.4e-4 (Ladder24) of the output peak, flat over the grid. The response in Hz at the displayed cutoff and the `2 F` unit agrees with the real stage to 2.3 % below 1.4 kHz (peak height 0.05 dB). **There is no port error to fix**; no engine path and no default changes. (2) The per-type factors are therefore not compensation for the stage shape; the stage already gives LP12 1.52, LP24 1.35, BP 1.03, HP 0.78 times the display Hz at resonance 64, so a 1.4 multiplier moves the chain away from the stage. The remaining gap lies outside the stages (terms added into `R` before the stage, input gain, pre-filter path): OQ22. (3) Ladder24: the stage port is right, but with a voice word the stage's coefficient words stay at table position 0.5458 for knobs 40 .. 100 while `R` follows the voice law, and the literal voice `R` as position would give a corner of 2 .. 500 Hz at display 139 .. 4400 Hz; the position word of a sounding Ladder24 is open (OQ22).
- **Why:** The oracle is the hardware reference; the port equals it to float32 accumulation over the whole range a program can produce, so a fit that multiplies the cutoff by 0.8 .. 4.4 cannot be repairing it.
- **Alternatives:** Change the stage model to chase the 1e-5 residual (rejected: it is rounding, and no result moves); re-score the 12 clips (rejected: the engine is bit-identical to the D121 build); drop the per-type factors (rejected: D22, 3.36 against 2.18).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-tools/src/bin/fw_filter_stage_dump.rs` (new), `crates/nord-core/src/synth/fw_filter.rs` (`FwCoefs::new_strip`, tool-facing, no render caller), `tools/synth_filter_stage_diff.py` (+ test), `docs/research/synth-filter-stage-vs-model.md`, `docs/research/data/synth-filter-stage-{diff,corner}.csv`, [synth-engine](specs/synth-engine.md) s4.6, OQ22, ROADMAP. `render_hash` unchanged.

### D125. The FX chip runs on live audio in the oracle: the chain transfer is exact, reverb and chorus stage installs run but do not match the ports yet

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17. **Superseded in part by D133:** the reverb type 0 and CHOR mismatches claimed here were the harness (35 stage calls per pass) plus a CHOR port bug, not an unexplained transfer problem.
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session, review notes applied; evidence [fx-oracle-live](research/fx-oracle-live.md))
- **Context:** The FX routines were checked one at a time with direct calls (`sharc-fx`), never as the chain on a live signal. The question was whether the FX chip pass can run on audio in the oracle with one real routine installed, so reverb, chorus and the other families can be compared with the native ports on the same input.
- **Decision:** (1) Add `sharc-fxlive` (`runner/fx_run.rs`): boot an FX core, deliver ARM messages, feed 4 frames per pass, collect the output and optional bus tap; `--regs-at PC` reports the stage entry registers. (2) Add `fx_live_native` and `tools/fx_oracle_live.py` as the native counterpart and the comparison (lag, fitted gain, RMS delta, centroid, tail, null depth). (3) Record the measured status: the chain with a pass-through stage equals `0.1866 x input` at lag 0 with a -157.6 dB null (VERIFIED); the stage installs of reverb type 0 and Mod 2 CHOR run but their oracle response differs from `FwReverb` / `FirmwareFx::mod2` (linear, starts after 38 passes instead of 1352 calls, unstable), so no family is reported as matching and no native model is changed. (4) The PNO lane is not yet fed into the FX input (WAV hand-over only). OQ35 tracks the stage calling convention.
- **Why:** The harness is the missing piece for any live FX check and its transfer is exact, so it is useful now. The reverb and chorus responses cannot be called hardware or port evidence while the stage convention is open: the golden direct-call vectors stay the proof of the ports (D90, D91).
- **Alternatives:** Report the oracle numbers as a port error (rejected: the routines are verified bit-exact by direct call, the difference is in the call environment); call the routines directly instead of through the dispatcher (rejected for this task: that is the existing `sharc-fx` golden path and says nothing about the live chain); wait until a family matches before landing the harness (rejected: the harness, the identity result and the blocker list are reusable).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-sharc` (`runner/fx_run.rs`, `bin/sharc_fxlive.rs`, `Cargo.toml`, `runner/system.rs` one visibility change), `crates/nord-tools` (`wav_io.rs`, `bin/fx_live_native.rs`), `tools/fx_oracle_live.py` (+ test), `docs/research/fx-oracle-live.md`, [sharc-core](specs/sharc-core.md) s8, OQ35, ROADMAP. No engine default or `render_hash` change (D22).

### D123. Piano extra voices are already played, the damper-down voice list equals the pedal-up list, pedal-down notes take it as an opt-in

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-16
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session, review notes applied; evidence [piano-extra-voices](research/piano-extra-voices.md))
- **Context:** audio-remaining item 21 said classes 1, 2, 3, 5, 6 start an extra voice the engine does not play (250 programs, 276 layers); item 12 listed the damper-down voice list as undecoded, with pedal-down notes bypassing the firmware list in the engine (25 to 77 % of the clip notes).
- **Decision:** (1) The extra voice has been played by default since D23; the coverage row is relabelled `piano.extra_voice=played` (verified). (2) The record byte 4 (`& 3`) is the stroke kind (0 normal, 1 damper-down, 2 key-up): the grand and upright extra voice is the damper-down stroke on every note-on. (3) With CC64 = 127 the firmware runs the same level calls as without it on 38 of 38 factory sounds; the pedal only defers the key-up record. (4) New opt-in `--firmware-piano-pedal-voices` (`PianoLayer::firmware_pedal_voices`) lets pedal-down notes start the firmware voices. Defaults and `render_hash` unchanged.
- **Why:** The ARM run is the firmware's own behaviour; the legacy bypass has no firmware counterpart. The clip scores are mixed (Velvet 1.37 to 2.74, Felt 1.37 to 1.33, Grand Pad 1.43 to 1.51 alone; the chain + slew path improves on two of three), so D22 is not met and nothing flips.
- **Alternatives:** Make pedal-down firmware voices the default (rejected: score worsens on two of three clips); model the firmware key-up record placement under the pedal (deferred: no single-note pedal clip).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-core` (`piano/pedal_voices.rs`, `piano/extra_voice.rs`, `piano.rs`, `render_opts.rs`), `tools/arm_piano_pedal.py`, `tools/program_sweep.py`, `docs/research/data/{program-sweep,emu_vs_hw_segments}.json`; [piano-extra-voices](research/piano-extra-voices.md), `docs/specs/piano-engine.md`, ROADMAP 7b.5, OQ20, `audio-remaining` rows 12 and 21.

### D128. Raw firmware artifacts are never tracked; derived data is classified A to D; B and C move to runtime loading

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session, review notes applied; evidence [firmware-derived-data-inventory](research/firmware-derived-data-inventory.md))
- **Context:** D20 allows derived values only, but the root of `main` tracked `helper.exe` (587,736 B, the USB driver installer from the Nord OS updater), `sram.bin` (64,640 B, size of the firmware SRAM segment) and `sram.asm` (about 420 KB), referenced by nothing. Beyond them, code images (`bass-fw-image.csv`, `farfisa-fw-image.csv`, `organ/*/prog_*.rs`) and large tables without a closed form (`synth-p-words.csv` 370 KB, `synth-super24-tables.txt`, `synth-fm-tables.csv`, `fx/fw_*_tables.rs`) are tracked. The WAVE tables already load at runtime from the user's image (D22, D127).
- **Decision:** (1) Class A, raw artifacts (installers, SRAM/CODE dumps, disassemblies), are never tracked; the three root files are removed and `.gitignore` blocks the names and `*.exe`, `*.dmg`, `*.ns4b`, `*_sram.bin`, `*_code.bin`. (2) Every tracked firmware-derived file is classified A (raw), B (code image), C (numeric table dump), D (own measurements, logs, goldens, fitted laws) in the inventory. (3) B and C move to runtime loading from the user's own OS update file (`FirmwareContainer::extract_segment`) in follow-up PRs, ordered in the inventory; tests switch to small synthetic fixtures. (4) D stays. (5) History is not rewritten here; whether to do so is the owner's call.
- **Why:** Keeps the repository to our own work and measurements, matches D20 and the WAVE precedent, and each migration is independently testable.
- **Alternatives:** Keep B and C tracked as "derived" (rejected: no closed form, and they are the bulk of the copied material); delete B and C now (rejected: breaks default renders and tests until loaders exist); rewrite history (owner's decision, out of scope).
- **Impact:** This PR: file removals, `.gitignore`, the inventory, this decision, bean. No crate code change. Follow-ups change `crates/nord-core` loaders, tests and `docs/specs/`; defaults without an image need an explicit fallback (D22).

### D124. The ARM sends no SY pitch entry `e` in any of the 384 recalls and 149 played sample voices; the writer is a DSP side transfer or not modelled

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session, review notes applied; evidence [arm-dsp-capture](research/arm-dsp-capture.md))
- **Context:** The writer of the SY sample pitch entry `e` at `DM(0x9354A + index)` resisted four hunts (D89, D105, D111, D112: no DSP store, no ARM message in the notes run, no ISR, no handler). The remaining ARM side hypothesis was that `e` arrives in a message of the real program recall or of a played voice.
