- **Decision:** (1) Capture the ARM to DSP stream of all 384 factory programs after the firmware's own recall (`tools/arm_dsp_capture.py`: 800 load ticks, 100 idle, keys 36 / 60 / 84 each from the same saved state, event queue drained, a stand-in library sample bound to the flagged synth layers with the real loader), summarise it by target (`tools/arm_dsp_summary.py`, `docs/research/data/arm-dsp-capture-*`). (2) Verdict: in the 119 programs with an active SAMPLE layer no ARM word of any chip lands in `DM(0x93500..0x935A0)` and no 16-bit half is within 2 of the modelled entry; the voice path ran for 60 programs (149 voices, 76 distinct SY words, all in `0x921A6..0x9252C`). The recall sends 0 words to SY in all 384 programs, the same 1,516 words to SMP in every program (nothing sample specific), the layer's 32-bit sample id hash to no DSP, and the sample loader plus bind send 0 messages; no sample address, length or flash page is in any SY or SMP word. (3) The writer of `e` is therefore a DSP side transfer (SPI flash or external-port DMA started by the SY image) or a path the harness does not run; the flash model of a later task cannot take its parameters from the ARM stream. (4) `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in; no engine or default change.
- **Why:** The capture closes the ARM side for what the harness can run, 119 of 119 sample programs, with a negative result that is reproducible (`arm_dsp_summary.py` prints `0 window hits, 0 e-shaped hits`). It also fixes what the later DMA model must not assume (an ARM descriptor).
- **Alternatives:** Treat the earlier single-sample note-on run as enough (rejected: it did not cover the recall path or the other 118 programs); flip `--sample-pitch fw` (rejected: the entry is still modelled, D65); wait for the program's own samples before reporting (rejected: the hash to file map is unknown and the bound sample only changes values, not addresses).
- **Impact:** `tools/arm_dsp_capture.py`, `tools/arm_dsp_summary.py` (+ tests), `docs/research/arm-dsp-capture.md`, `docs/research/data/arm-dsp-capture-{e-check,programs,targets}`; OQ22 / [audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md) row 2 updated; no engine change. Limits: the stand-in sample is `Carina Accordion 1` (the program's own file is unknown), 59 of 119 sample programs stop at the voice gate for an undiagnosed reason, the library and the DSP status bit are harness stand-ins.

### D141. The SY sample bytes come through AMI banks 2 and 3 by external-port DMA chains; there is no SPI flash and no chain TCB writes the pitch window

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [dsp-flash-dma-model](research/dsp-flash-dma-model.md))
- **Context:** D124 left the writer of the SY pitch entry `e` and of the sample stream as a DSP side SPI flash or external-port DMA (ASSUMED) after four failed hunts, and asked for the smallest model of that path.
- **Decision:** (1) Add the opt-in external-port DMA channel 0 chain model `core::periph::epchain` (`Core::enable_epchain`, `sharc-ref` command `epchain [path= base=]`, never on by default): `CPEP0` / `DMAC0` walk the TCB chain (HRM order CP, EM, EI, C, IM, II), instant completion, sparse external memory, a census and a counter of transfers into `DM(0x93500..0x935A0)`. (2) Record that the SY SPI is the ARM command link (opcode 9), so there is no SPI flash model to build; the sample bytes are read from AMI banks 2 and 3 (`0x0800_0000 + 2 off` write, `0x0C00_0000 + off` 8 word reads into the staging buffer). (3) `tools/ns4_arm_init_script.py --tcb-summary` censuses the static 68 slot x 3 TCB table of the ARM init script. (4) No engine change: `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in.
- **Why:** The chain code and the static table show the destinations: the tag array, one dummy word `DM(0x935D3)` and the 128-word staging buffer. 204 static TCBs and 3,888 run TCBs (81 chains) write 0 words into the window. The 59 programs that stop at the voice gate stop in ARM code before any DSP message, so no DSP model changes them.
- **Alternatives:** Model an SPI flash from the local image (rejected: the SPI block is the ARM link, nothing in the SY reads a flash through it); feed the staging buffer from the `.nsmp4` stream through the new chain (rejected for now: the device behind banks 2 and 3 and the offset table `DM(0xB2000..)` are unknown, so an external image would be invented); flip any default (rejected: nothing measured).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-sharc` (`core/periph/epchain.rs`, `glue.rs`, `mod.rs`, `bin/sharc_ref/epchain_cmd.rs`, `script.rs`, `exec.rs`), `tools/ns4_arm_init_script.py` (+ tests), `tools/oracle_scenarios/sy_isr_epchain.scn`, [dsp-flash-dma-model](research/dsp-flash-dma-model.md), OQ22 / [audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md) row 2, [emulation-coverage](research/emulation-coverage.md), ROADMAP 7b.6. Limits: no external image, no ARM fed run, offsets stay 0 so the chain only exercises the first voice slots, completion timing ASSUMED.
### D148. Not found in the paths run: the amp type 7 edit and a 6,000-tick recall (PNO core 1 words) do not install the B3 Leslie `0x1256AD`; the stage measured in the oracle port, left-lane sines, has its envelope on the horn word; no port

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (evidence [leslie-gate](research/leslie-gate.md))
- **Context:** D135 and D139 left the install path of `0x1256AD` open and named the amp type 7 "To Rotary" recall and a longer recall as untested, and the stage had never been measured with test signals.
- **Decision:** (1) Record the negative, limited to the paths run (PNO core 1 words, 120-tick edits and one 6,000-tick recall; FX cores and System routing ids not tried): the amp type edit (`0x1a7` = 7, 0, 7) sends the same 8 PNO core 1 words for 7 and 0 (the `0xB0476` Amp stage set, so those sets are the Amp module, not Leslie installs), no `0x1256AD`, no chain-head write, 0 reads of row 6 of table `0xC05450C8`; the long recall result is in the note. (2) Record that the Part A `organ_b3` clip has envelope lines at 0.773 and 7.000 Hz (horn slow and fast), so a Leslie runs on the hardware. (3) Add `leslie_probe` (`nord-tools`): the oracle exact port driven with sines and an impulse at slow, fast and mixed speed words; envelope lines follow the horn word at every tested frequency (fast: 6.90 Hz at 8 of 12); the rotor word showed no amplitude line in left-lane sines only (ASSUMED reading: a phase / Doppler effect is not measured yet), gain +6.4 dB at 2 kHz and -11.6 dB at 5 kHz. (4) Port and change nothing in `nord-core`.
- **Why:** The one hardware-facing difference, the fast horn line 7.00 Hz against 6.89 Hz of the D63 physical ratio, comes from one clip with an unknown speed history, and the crossover cannot be read from sines because of a +/-3 dB cabinet ripple; a change would be fitted to one number (D22).
- **Alternatives:** Set the physical ratio to 1.0 opt-in (rejected: one clip, 3 analysis bins); move the engine crossover from 800 Hz to about 450 Hz (rejected: ripple, no clean crossover, no clip evidence for the engine path).
- **Impact:** `docs/research/leslie-gate.md`, `crates/nord-tools/src/leslie_probe.rs`, `crates/nord-tools/src/bin/leslie_probe.rs` and tests, OQ21, ROADMAP 7b.3, [audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md) row 9. No `nord-core`, golden, `render_hash` or default change.

### D155. Not found: seven B3 / rotary programs, 20,762 edit phases and the live PNO / ORG chips do not name, load or execute the Leslie `0x1256AD`; the same pc on core 2 is the filter stage

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (evidence [leslie-paths](research/leslie-paths.md))
- **Context:** D148 left four paths untried: the FX cores and System routing ids, more than one program, run-time loaded images and the live chips. D148 also named `0x1257B2` as an open stage.
- **Decision:** (1) Record the negative, limited to the paths run: 7 programs (rotary on / off, slow / fast / stop, drive 0 to 4.25, one piano layer with rotary), a 6,000-tick recall, 2,966 edit phases each (160 Leslie / rotary ids on every owning store, 232 amp and FX type edits on four FX units, 2,574 `AudioRouting` dispatches on 19 stores): 0 messages carry `0x1256AD`, `0x1257B2` or `0x1237F8` (value or code record), 0 DSP L2 code stores, the DSP image download (1 call), the SPI block download (4) and the bulk sender (2) run only in the boot; the scan controls (`0x121E2B`, the Amp words) are found in every program. (2) Record that the 19 routing ids have no value cell in any of the 10 layer clients or 12 FX units and cause no message. (3) Record the static census: the Leslie words `DM(0xB2F21..0xB2F31)` are read by 9 instructions in the PNO core 1 image only (0 in FX 1 / 2, PNO 2, ORG 1 / 2; raw scan of the five image files: PNO 1, others 0). (4) Record the live link (PNO 1 / 2, ORG 1 / 2, 2,441 passes, two programs): PNO 1 executes `0x1256AD` in 0 passes while the B3 processor runs every pass; on PNO 2 and ORG 2 the pc `0x1256AD` executes every pass because there it is `F12 = F4 * F4` inside the filter stage `0x12566E`: probes of the Leslie must name the PNO core 1 image. (5) `0x1257B2` is the raw synth filter type 3 stage (D121), not a Leslie candidate. (6) Add `tools/arm_leslie_paths.py` with tests. Port and change nothing in `nord-core`.
- **Why:** The stage is measured (D148) but never linked; the open question is the install, and widening the ARM and live paths without a hit narrows the hypotheses to the part command consumer, UART / MIDI gestures (speed switch, organ on / off), a DSP side DMA, longer ARM time and the unparsed SY1SY2 / SMP images (section 5 of the note, with counts).
- **Alternatives:** Probe the Leslie on core 2 chips (rejected: wrong routine, correction 1); apply a native fix from the D148 stage numbers (rejected: no measured gap against a linked stage, one clip).
- **Impact:** `docs/research/leslie-paths.md`, `docs/research/data/leslie-paths.json`, `tools/arm_leslie_paths.py`, `tools/tests/test_arm_leslie_paths.py`, OQ21, ROADMAP 7b.3, [leslie-gate](research/leslie-gate.md) follow-ups 1 and 2 (partly answered). No `nord-core`, golden, `render_hash` or default change.

### D139. Not found: no PNO core 1 ARM message of two B3 recalls or three Leslie edit ids installs or names `0x1256AD`; nothing is ported

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (evidence [leslie-locate](research/leslie-locate.md))
- **Context:** D126 showed `0x1256AD` runs in 0 of 76 PNO core 1 passes with the org-system capture; D135 found no FX-chip Leslie. The open question was which ARM message, flag or DMA makes the chain loop call it, and whether the harness can open that gate.
- **Decision:** (1) Record the result: no instruction of the PNO core 1 image names `0x1256AD` (only the chain loop's `CALL (M8, I8)` can reach it through a chain element); the ARM names it only in code row 6 of table `0xC05450C8`, read once at boot by the channel resolver `0xC0013928` (caller `0xC0220DB0`, target 2) and never in 800 recall ticks or the Leslie enable / speed / drive edits; a full B3 recall (`B3 Chords n Solo`, `B3 Jazz Split`, 7,340 PNO core 1 messages) contains no `0x1256AD`, no chain-head write and only the stage-record words `DM(0xB0479..0xB0483)`; replayed into the lane, `sharc-sys --probe 0x1256AD` gives 0 of 316 passes. (2) Add `tools/arm_table_watch.py` (ARM read / write hook over a RAM range, call-site trace, edit replay on the organ FX unit) and `tools/arm_capture_script.py` (capture file to `sharc-sys` script). (3) Port nothing to `crates/nord-core`; the lane pair (sub +27.54, mid +1.04, brilliance +15.41, gap 11.02 dB) stays.
- **Why:** There is no Leslie output to compare, so any coefficient port would be ASSUMED. The remaining install path (the part command list `[part + 0xC8]` consumer, or a DSP read-back handshake) is not run by the harness.
- **Alternatives:** Hand-install a chain element with `0x1256AD` and tune it to the clip (rejected: fitted to one clip, D22, and arm-part-activation s4 already showed the organ pair is not in the chain buffer); declare the Leslie FX-chip only (rejected: D135 does not support it).
- **Impact:** `docs/research/leslie-locate.md`, `docs/research/audio-remaining.md` row 9, `docs/ROADMAP.md` 7b.3, `tools/arm_table_watch.py`, `tools/arm_capture_script.py` and tests. No `nord-core`, golden, `render_hash` or default change.

### D126. PNO core 1 Leslie ring-DMA restart model (`--ring-dma`, opt-in) and stop on native port

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (evidence [b3-leslie-pno-lane](research/b3-leslie-pno-lane.md))
- **Context:** Audio-remaining row 9 left the Leslie history ring DMA (`0xB0078` fill, 8-pass restart) open on the live PNO core 1 lane.
- **Decision:** (1) Add opt-in `core::periph::ringdma` (`sharc-sys --ring-dma`, off by default) modelling `DM(0x310C3000)` (`7` arm, `0x107` copy first `0x40`-word record of chains `0xE31E8` and `0xE35E0` in `0x0A00_0000` DMA view into `c[-8]` `0xE31A6`/`0xE359E`), plus `sharc-ring` (`runner/ring_trace.rs`) for per-pass window access traces. (2) Do **not** port anything to `crates/nord-core`: on the live PNO lane `0xB0078..` is never read/written and `0x1256AD`/`0x1237F8` run in 0 of 76 passes (`DM(0xC2F48..)` hold the null stub `0xC2E4A`), so `--ring-dma` produces bit-identical output (`peak 0.360115`, `rms 0.060318`, `organ_b3` sub `+27.54`, mid `+1.04`, brilliance `+15.41`, gap `11.02 dB`).
- **Why:** The ring-DMA copy works as specified by the chain heads, but the active chain in the captured PNO lane does not invoke the Leslie processor; porting an unverified ring change to `nord-core` would have no live oracle backing.
- **Alternatives:** Port a speculative ring model to `nord-core` anyway (rejected: violates Step 3 and D22); leave `DM(0x310C3000)` unmodelled (rejected: the census and trace show it is the only non-1:1 per-pass peripheral write).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-sharc` (`core/periph/ringdma.rs`, `runner/ring_trace.rs`, `bin/sharc_ring.rs`, `core/periph/{glue,mod}.rs`, `runner/{periph_run,mod}.rs`, `Cargo.toml`), `docs/research/b3-leslie-pno-lane.md`, `docs/specs/sharc-core.md` section 7, `docs/research/audio-remaining.md` row 9, `docs/ROADMAP.md` 7b.3. No `nord-core`, golden, `render_hash` or default change.

### D144. The live ARM link serves several chips on one clock; ORG core 2 and PNO core 2 run, the SY chip is blocked by its interrupt driven pass model

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** the session task (follow-up 1 of D138); evidence [arm-dsp-live-link-synth](research/arm-dsp-live-link-synth.md), [arm-dsp-live-link-synth.json](research/data/arm-dsp-live-link-synth.json)
- **Context:** D138 ran one chip (PNO core 1). The synth envelope words of D132 and the synth voice need the organ-program chips (ORG core 2, PNO core 2) and the SY chip live.
- **Decision:** (1) Chip start-up is data: `runner/live_presets.rs` holds `pno` and `org` (`sharc-sys` defaults: entry `0x125C88`, poll loop `0x125DDF`, mailbox `0xB00E2`); `sharc-live --org` serves ORG core 1 / 2 and PNO core 2, `--window ADDR:COUNT:STRIDE` reads the level from the voice record instead of a lane. (2) `tools/arm_dsp_live.py` takes repeatable `--chip CHIP:CORE=PRESET=IMAGE[=WINDOW[=PROBES[=WATCH]]]`, starts one `sharc-live` per chip, routes each ARM path 2 message by `(comm, core, path)` (`tools/arm_dsp_route.py`, `Router`), runs every chip the same number of passes after each 24 kHz tick, writes one `sharc-sys` script per chip and can replay them (`--old-replay RECALL,KEY` merges the D132 way). (3) The SY chip is **not** built: SY1SY2 has no mailbox pass (interrupt per frame, FIFO ring, boot kernel), so it needs its own chip model; its 290 path 1 words are counted.
- **Why:** The pass model of `Live` is a mailbox poll loop; the organ program fits it with one preset. Evidence for the SY blocker is in the research doc s4. Nothing flows back into ARM state, so the recall stream is the D132 stream.
- **Alternatives:** One process serving several cores (rejected: each core has its own SRAM image and pass state, a process per chip keeps the protocol unchanged); a stub SY chip that only accepts words (rejected: it would hide the interrupt driven behaviour); deliver the key to ORG core 2 as D132 did (rejected for the live run: the firmware sends it to PNO core 2).
- **Impact:** new `runner/live_presets.rs`, `tools/arm_dsp_route.py` with tests; `live.rs`, `sharc_live.rs`, `arm_dsp_live.py` extended; no engine, render or default changes. **Measured (Smooth Lead and 12dB Sweep, key 60 held 1,500 ticks, 2,700 ticks, 2,441 passes per chip, all idle, deterministic):** ORG core 2 and PNO core 2 each receive 12,601 boot + 912 load words, identical in order to the offline capture; the key phase sends 2 + 2 words to PNO core 2 and **0** to ORG core 2 (silent, voice peak 0.0); PNO core 2 voice peak 0.449895 / rms 0.130574, equal to a replay of its own stream and to the D132-style replay on the ORG core 2 image; no new envelope word appears; the envelope chain states differ per program (not the D132 "identical" row, cause open).

### D138. The ARM harness runs live against one oracle chip (PNO core 1) over a pipe; one direction only, the Leslie gate stays closed

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** the session task (owner goal: full ARM + SHARC emulation as an offline golden reference); evidence [arm-dsp-live-link](research/arm-dsp-live-link.md), [arm-dsp-live-link.json](research/data/arm-dsp-live-link.json)
- **Context:** The ARM harness (`tools/arm_*.py`, `unicorn`) and the SHARC oracle (`crates/nord-sharc`) never ran together: every DSP input was a captured or replayed ARM stream (D120, D124, D126, D130) and every DSP answer a stub (`DspAck`). Gaps stayed open that a live pairing could close: the Leslie processor `0x1256AD` never runs on the PNO lane (D126), the synth envelope words are missing from the recall stream (D132), PUMP counter writer (OQ36).
- **Decision:** (1) `sharc-live` (`runner/live.rs`) serves one oracle chip over stdin/stdout lines: `M W1 W2` store, `P N` passes with a summary line (idle, steps, lane count, sum of squares, peak, probe-pc hits), `T`, `D`, `Q`; no unsolicited output. (2) `tools/arm_dsp_link.py` couples the clocks: ARM timer 24 kHz, DSP pass `2 / F`, `F = 43,402.34375 Hz`, owed passes `floor(t * 1,388,875 / 1,536,000)` after `t` ticks (integer, deterministic); `PortGrouper` groups the port stores incrementally. (3) `tools/arm_dsp_live.py` boots the full machine with a factory program, feeds the chip as the firmware stores (boot included), starts the DSP clock at the main loop, and reports numbers. (4) The reply direction is telemetry only; nothing is written back into ARM state.
- **Why:** A pipe is the smallest transport that carries both directions, has no clock or thread in the chip and needs no change to the oracle core; pyo3 would couple the Windows build to Python, a Rust ARM core does not exist. PNO core 1 with a B3 program carries the most ARM-input gaps and its preset exists. The reply direction is not invented because no PNO-side writer of an ARM-visible state is known; a made-up reply would hide the gap.
- **Alternatives:** capture then replay (what exists); pyo3 in-process; embed the ARM output in a Rust CLI; deliver boot messages one pass at a time (left as follow-up, the DSP image is downloaded at boot on hardware); a stub reply for the handshake (rejected: no evidence).
- **Impact:** new `crates/nord-sharc/src/runner/live.rs`, `src/bin/sharc_live.rs`, `tools/arm_dsp_link.py`, `tools/arm_dsp_live.py` with tests; no engine, render or default changes. **Measured (B3 Chords n Solo, key 60 held 1,500 ticks, 2,700 ticks, 2,441 passes):** 30,067 words reach PNO core 1 (22,389 boot, 7,340 load, 338 key phases), all 8,306 distinct words of the old replay present (27 differ in value, 92 new), all passes idle, the live run equals a replay of its own stream (lane peak 0.074589, rms 0.017788), two live runs are byte-identical; `0x1256AD`, `0x1237F8`, `0x1236F8` run in **0 of 2,441** passes (B3 processor `0x121E2B` 2,143), no ARM word reaches `0xC2E00..0xC2FFF`, the Leslie descriptors `0xB0476` / `0xB047E` are written at pass 160 and read by no pass. The in-window ARM stream does not close the D126 gate (EMPIRICAL): the 5,910 out-of-window stores and the missing reply direction are not covered, and #493 (D136, open) reaches a similar negative from the recall stream.

### D137. The 3 to 5 dB treble excess is not a downstream stage; the firmware amp's frame-rate mismatch is fixed behind opt-in `--fx-rate-match`

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the owner (evidence [brightness-downstream-oracle](research/brightness-downstream-oracle.md))
- **Context:** The Part A clips read 3 to 5.5 dB brighter than the hardware above 1.5 to 3 kHz on clips of different engines, which pointed at a stage downstream of the engines (amp, EQ, compressor, master). The oracle FX chain `sharc-fxlive` (D125, D133) and the oracle-exact ports allow a stage-by-stage comparison with identical noise.
- **Decision:** (1) No default changes. (2) New opt-in `LayerFxChain::set_fx_rate_match(bool)` (`fx/fw_rate_match.rs`, `ns4 render-patch --fx-rate-match`, `FirmwareOptions::fx_rate_match`, default off) runs the firmware Amp drive and cabinet behind a windowed-sinc converter at the DSP frame rate 43,402.34 Hz instead of once per 48 kHz host frame; latency about 36 host frames plus the stage's 4. (3) New tools `fx_stage_response` and `band_response` (per-stage band gains from noise), `docs/research/data/emu_vs_hw_rate_match.json` and `emu_vs_hw_downstream_ablation.json` with their results. (4) Findings recorded: the oracle FX chain is flat on white noise (null -156.3 dB), so the master IIR (`master_iir.rs`) is not in that path and stays off; the layer compressor has no tonal effect; the Amp EQ differs from its oracle by at most 1.7 dB; `LP24` / `HP24` cannot run in the oracle.
- **Why:** The firmware amp words are per-frame and bit exact per frame against the oracle, and the frame rate is VERIFIED ([dsp-frame-rate](research/dsp-frame-rate.md)), so the oracle's response in Hz is the port at 43,402.34 Hz; at 48 kHz it is stretched by 1.106: +0.5 to +2.2 dB in 3..8 kHz, +0.9 to +3.5 dB in 8..16 kHz, 60 of 234 third-octave cells off by 3 dB or more. With the converter the response is within 0.13 dB (aggregate bands) and 1.09 dB (worst third octave) of the frame-rate response, and 0 cells differ by 3 dB. Clips (`emu_vs_hw.py --confirmed-only`): Tiny Transistor brilliance +13.1 to +8.7 dB, score 5.80 to 4.80; Vox J3 presence -1.3 to +1.2 dB, score 3.03 to 2.95; clips without a firmware amp are bit identical. The clip evidence does not meet the D22 bar for a flip (one performance per program, the Farfisa source itself is +10.3 dB bright with every stage off).
- **Alternatives:** Re-derive the biquad and cabinet coefficients at 48 kHz (rejected: the cabinet is a measured 400 tap response and the drive stage has table waveshaping; a converter keeps the verified words untouched); flip the rate match to default (rejected: latency, cost, and the clip gap is dominated by the source); enable the master IIR (rejected: not in the oracle chain); port a different `LP24` model (rejected: no oracle words).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-core/src/fx/{fw_rate_match.rs, tests_fw_rate_match_chain.rs, amp_eq.rs, mod.rs}`, `render_opts.rs`, new `render_opts_fx_rate.rs`, `crates/nord-tools/src/{band_response.rs, bin/fx_stage_response.rs}`, [effects-and-routing](specs/effects-and-routing.md) s4.0c, [audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md) row 23, ROADMAP 7b.4. Default render unchanged (the switch is off). Open: `LP24` / `HP24` words, the A:25 gap, owner decision on the default.

### D135. No evidence yet that the B3 Leslie runs on the FX chip: `0x1256AD` is not in the static FX images and the boot logs send no Leslie row to the FX cores, but run-time loaded code and the amp type 7 recall are untested

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [b3-leslie-fx-chip](research/b3-leslie-fx-chip.md))
- **Context:** D126 showed the Leslie processor `0x1256AD` runs in 0 of 76 passes on the live PNO core 1 lane. The ASSUMED explanation was that the Leslie runs on the FX chip, driven through the `sharc-fxlive` harness of D125.
- **Decision:** (1) Record the FX-chip hypothesis as **not supported by the evidence so far**, not as rejected. Tested: byte-identical code presence of `0x1256AD` and the B3 processor `0x121E2B` in the static FX, ORG and PNO core 2 images (0.00, `tools/fx_code_presence.py`, positive control 1.00 for the cabinet copy in both FX cores; a relinked copy would still score); the boot-time ARM logs (Leslie row words of D98, `0xB2F25..0xB2FA1`, only in `pno_core1.script`; one-off scan, EMPIRICAL); `--probe 0x1256AD` is 0 of 316 PNO passes. Not tested: code loaded into the FX cores after boot, an ARM recall of a program with amp type 7 (To Rotary), and any unmuted FX chain with a cabinet stage and its state block. The FX copies of `0x1237F8`, `0x1235AA`, `0x125371`, `0x1250CF` are the Amp cabinet module of [fx-type-map](research/fx-type-map.md) s10, so a cabinet-based rotary on the FX chip is not excluded. The `sharc-fxlive` run with the boot log (output 0 even for a pass-through stage; a Leslie or cabinet stage does not return without its state block) is vacuous for audio and shows only that no stage address of the booted chain is executed. (2) Add `tools/fx_code_presence.py` (+ `tools/tests/test_fx_code_presence.py`), a numbers-only check of whether a routine is code in another image. (3) Do not port anything to `crates/nord-core`; the D126 band numbers (sub +27.54 dB, mid +1.04, brilliance +15.41, gap 11.02 dB) stay the only measured pair, no FX-chip Leslie render exists.
- **Why:** The check needs no clip and no audio: code that is not in an image cannot run there, and the ARM never sends the row to the FX cores. The result lowers the weight of the FX-chip branch of audio-remaining row 9 without closing it; the leading open question stays the ARM install path on the PNO chip.
- **Alternatives:** Install the FX Amp cabinet stages by hand and call it the Leslie (rejected: no ARM message links them to the organ Leslie, the hardware path would be guessed); render a Leslie from the PNO copy `0x1237F8` with the booted context (rejected: [fx-leslie](research/fx-leslie.md) shows it outputs nothing without the gated drive state, D98 drive words are already opt-in).
- **Impact:** `tools/fx_code_presence.py`, `tools/tests/test_fx_code_presence.py`, `docs/research/b3-leslie-fx-chip.md`, `docs/research/audio-remaining.md` row 9. No engine default, golden or `render_hash` change.
### D131. The measured 24 kHz ARM tick is the default for the Vox key timeline and the synth gain de-zipper; `--legacy-1khz-tick` restores 1 ms; arp gate and PUMP stay as they are

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** the owner (explicit approval in the task from the parent session to flip the measured part of D130 to default; evidence [arm-tick-timers](research/arm-tick-timers.md) s5, [arm-tick-keydelay.json](research/data/arm-tick-keydelay.json))
- **Context:** D130 measured the ARM timer at 24 kHz (Timer0 `PRD34 = 1000` at 24 MHz) and found the Vox key-down delays timer driven (3 / 17 / 47 interrupts at four interrupt spacings), while `TICK_SECONDS` (`organ/vox_timeline.rs`) was 1 ms and `gain_dezip.rs` latched the gain target at 1 kHz. D130 changed no default (D22: the real-time effect was ASSUMED). The owner now approves flipping the measured part.
- **Decision:** (1) `TICK_SECONDS` = 1/24,000 s: key-down delays 3 / 17 / 47 ticks = 0.125 / 0.71 / 1.96 ms, key-up 1 / 7 / 37 = 0.04 / 0.29 / 1.54 ms (key-down counts: `arm-tick-keydelay.json`; key-up counts: `keydown-organ-keys.csv`, model 1, 24 notes); the weight refresh interval follows the tick (2 samples at 48 kHz). (2) `CONTROL_TICK_HZ` = 24,000: the gain target latches every `fs / 24000` frames (2 at 48 kHz). (3) Opt-out: `FirmwareOptions::measured_tick` (default `true`; `legacy_all()` false; `--legacy-1khz-tick`), applied by `render_opts_tick::apply_measured_tick` to both organ layers (`OrganLayer::set_measured_tick`) and the three synth layers (`SynthLayer::measured_tick`); `program_sweep --legacy-1khz-tick`; the GUI inherits it through `FirmwareOptions` (Organ and Synth preset groups: Classic preset = off, Firmware preset and Defaults = on) and has a Global "24 kHz ARM tick" checkbox in the firmware bar; `layer_init` (Shift+Section Edit) rebuilds layers with `::new` and now carries the old layer's tick setting over (test `layer_init_keeps_the_legacy_1khz_tick_opt_out`). (4) The arpeggiator gate (OQ32, 2/3 of a step) and PUMP (OQ36) are **not** changed and stay opt-in / open.
- **Why:** The 24 kHz timer and the timer driven tick counts are VERIFIED; keeping a 24 x slower tick that has no firmware counterpart is the less accurate choice, and the owner prefers parity. The delays are lower bounds in time: the real delay is the larger of the timer count and the ARM work time (12,500 cycles per interrupt), which is unmeasured, and the 0.125 ms group (3 ticks) is the least certain because the ARM is most likely to need longer than 3 interrupts. The owner approved the flip knowing this (approval recorded above). The key-down effect on the real attack is ASSUMED (no Vox clip), hence the opt-out.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the 1 ms tick until a Vox clip exists (rejected by the owner); make the delay `max(timer count, ARM work time)` (rejected: the work time is unmeasured, D130 follow-up 1); flip the arp gate and PUMP in the same change (rejected: OQ32 has no hardware recording, OQ36 has no counter writer).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-core/src/organ/vox_timeline.rs`, `synth/gain_dezip.rs`, `synth/mod.rs`, `organ/mod.rs`, `render_opts.rs`, new `render_opts_tick.rs`, `nord-gui/src/firmware_panel/presets.rs`, `nord-tools/src/bin/program_sweep.rs`, specs [synth-engine](specs/synth-engine.md) s3.2 and [organ-engine](specs/organ-engine.md), [audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md) rows 17 to 19, ROADMAP 7b.6. **Render effect (measured):** `render_hash` unchanged (whole `46f4dc64fc6de62eac40f1ee8549548d3965eea2d86aa5d59b828ed6566da506`, all five segments identical: no Vox source, constant voice gain); the factory program sweep (384 programs, default and firmware-all, 96,000 frames) is byte-identical before and after (`--legacy-1khz-tick` against the new default), so 0 programs change: the sweep never selects `--tone-source vox` and the gain de-zipper only moves the latch for a gain written mid-note. The effect sits in the opt-in Vox source (key-down footages inside 3 ms instead of 47 ms; test `vox_layer::tests::measured_tick_sends_the_footages_within_3_ms_and_the_legacy_tick_has_sent_none`) and in mid-note gain changes (latch error 42 us instead of up to 1 ms).

### D134. The firmware PUMP follows the Master Clock behind the existing opt-in: counter ticks and a division increment

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [fx-mod-types](research/fx-mod-types.md) s7.2). The number is D134 because D131 is claimed by three open PRs (#486, #487, #488) and D132 / D133 may follow; renumber on merge.
- **Context:** OQ36 / D130: `FirmwareFx::mod1` PUMP (`0x122D2C`) free-runs at 6.108 Hz (`kFx1RateClkDiv` 73) while the hardware pumps at 2.11 Hz = the 126 BPM beat on A:25; Master Clock sync (`mod1_sync_beats`) fell back to the engine model for every Mod 1 type. 9 of 384 factory programs use PUMP; no hardware clip of PUMP exists beyond the one A:25 figure.
- **Decision:** With `FirmwareFx::mod1` on and `mod1_sync_beats = Some(beats > 0)` at a valid BPM, PUMP (types 10, 11) stays on `FwMod1Rest` and (1) writes the counter word `c[-0x15]` with a new value once per division (`fx/fw_pump_clock.rs` `MasterClockTick`, period `fs * 60 / bpm * beats` engine frames, the first tick at once), which makes the routine reset the phase to `0x80000000`; (2) replaces the free-run increment `c[-32]` by `floor(2^33 / period)` so one phase cycle is one division. Free run (no sync) writes nothing new and is unchanged; A-PAN, TREM, RM, A-WAH, WAH in sync and PUMP with an invalid clock keep the engine path. No default flips: `FirmwareFx::DEFAULT` is unchanged and `render_hash` does not move (the new path is reached only with `mod1` on and Master Clock sync set).
- **Why:** The routine compares the counter with its copy and resets on any change, so the counter step carries no rate; only "one change per division" matters (VERIFIED in the oracle-verified port, tests `fw_pump_clock`, `fw_mod1_rest`, `tests_fw_pump_sync`). The reset alone cannot give 2.11 Hz: with the free-run increment the phase wraps every 0.164 s anyway (6.1 Hz); the increment under sync therefore has to span the division. The ARM writer of the counter was not found (OQ36 narrowed: it writes 0 at boot only, nothing periodic), so the increment law and the tick alignment to the beat grid are ASSUMED; the check is the single 2.11 Hz measurement.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the engine pump in sync (rejected: it has none of the firmware shape); tick only and keep the knob increment (rejected: still 6.1 Hz); wait for the ARM writer (rejected: the DSP-side behaviour is already determined, the ASSUMED part is small and tagged).
- **Update 1 (program load, review of #490):** the factory programs never set `mod1_sync_beats` (only the GUI edit and the plugin did), so a synced program free-ran. `apply_fx_chain` now sets it for PUMP programs with `mod1_enabled` and the stored MST CLK flag (`FxDecoded::mod1_clock_sync`): beats = `delay_sync_beats_from_index(rate)` (`engine/program_fx.rs`, test `engine/tests_pump_load.rs` through `apply_program`). **The rate to division law is ASSUMED and disagrees with the one clip:** A:25 Sidechain Whl II stores rate 73 with MST CLK on, which the delay law maps to 0.375 beats (5.6 Hz at 126 BPM), the clip shows 2.11 Hz (one beat). The sweep lists 9 programs with an enabled, synced PUMP (rates 39 to 76, most 72 to 75) and one free-running enabled PUMP (`Topliner`, type 11). Until the law is known the Master Clock drive stays under `FirmwareFx::mod1` (opt-in). Also: a Mod 1 type change or a pause of the firmware RM / A-WAH / WAH / PUMP stage re-initialises the existing allocation (`FwMod1Rest::retire`, `LayerFxChain::prepare_firmware_mod1` allocates at program load), no `Box::new` on the audio path. The engine PUMP of the default render follows the loaded clock too (`mod1_sync_beats` is also read by the engine model). Sweep (`tools/program_sweep.py --jsonl`, 384 of 384 render, panic 0, non-finite 0, silent 0; `program-sweep.json` and `program-coverage.md` regenerated, the 2026-11-14 baseline was stale): against the commit before this update 8 default renders change (RMS: Boxxx -0.06 dB, Shoreline Whl 0.00, Sidechain Whl II -0.14, Dance Lead -0.10, Calling Whl -0.01, Sidechain String +0.05, J3 Super Sawz -0.23, ScissorSplit Whl -0.33) and 8 `--firmware-all` renders (-0.44 to +0.46 dB); `render_hash` unchanged.
- **Update 2 (rate to division law, D134):** the delay law was wrong for PUMP. The ARM run (`tools/fx_clients.py`, `kFx1ClkEnable` 1, `kFx1RateClkDiv` 0..127) shows the sync handler writes a phase multiplier `N` to `c[-28]` in 15 steps (2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16, 24, 32, 48, 64, 96, 128, 192, 256; [fx-pump-sync-n.csv](research/data/fx-pump-sync-n.csv)). Program load now sets `mod1_sync_beats = 32 / N(v)`: A:25 (rate 73, N = 32) gets one beat = 2.10 Hz at 126 BPM, matching the clip (2.11 Hz). The 32 beat phase span is ASSUMED (it is what makes N = 32 one beat), `c[-32]` under sync was not seen to change in the harness. The A:25 blocker of the default flip is therefore lifted for the law; the flip stays a separate draft PR. The sweep numbers below are those of Update 1; the sweep was regenerated for this update.
- **Superseded in part (D149):** the counter model (counter ticks, `c[-32]` division increment, duck half a division after the start) is replaced by the clock word the ARM points `c[-29]` at; the rate law `N(v)` of update 2 stays.
- **Default flip deferred:** the repo owner approved making the firmware PUMP the default on 2026-11-17 (relayed by the coordinating session); it is split out of this PR (branch `pump-default-flip`) because the default must follow the Master Clock at the hardware rate and the division law of the stored rate does not reproduce the 2.11 Hz of A:25. D134 stays proposed until that is settled.
- **Impact:** `fx/fw_pump_clock.rs` (new, tests), `fx/fw_mod1_rest.rs` (`set_clock_period`), `fx/modulation.rs` (routing), `fx/tests_fw_pump_sync.rs`, [fx-mod-types](research/fx-mod-types.md) s7.1 / s7.2, [effects-and-routing](specs/effects-and-routing.md), OQ36 narrowed, ROADMAP 7b.6. Hosts see no new parameter: the existing `mod1_sync_beats` (17 divisions) now reaches the firmware stage.

### D130. The ARM timer is 24 kHz, the periodic tasks and the key-down delays are timer driven, the engine's 1 kHz ticks are documented not changed, PUMP figures are sourced, the arp gate is 2/3 in the replay

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session, review notes applied; evidence [arm-tick-timers](research/arm-tick-timers.md))
- **Context:** OQ22 and audio-remaining rows 17 to 19: the ARM tick was ASSUMED 1 kHz ("1 ms" = 200,000 harness instructions) while the arp clock and the timer set-up pointed to 24 kHz; the PUMP figures "2.11 Hz hardware, 6.1 Hz free run" were not recorded in a repo file; the arpeggiator gate (OQ32) was 2/3 in the firmware boot state but 1/2 in the engine.
- **Decision:** (1) Add `tools/arm_tick_probe.py` (`timers`, `wheel`, `run`, `keydelay`, `fxwatch`, `flagscan`, pure helpers tested without firmware) and record the measured table in [arm-tick-timers](research/arm-tick-timers.md): Timer0 TIM34 `PRD34 = 1000` at the firmware's 24 MHz = **24 kHz** (irq 22), TIM12 96 kHz unused, a 512 slot tick wheel with a 6 kHz periodic list (17 entries, 1 to 200 Hz, the Leslie rotor update `0xC02090F4` the only one that writes DSP words in an idle run) and a 3 kHz one-shot stack, the arp clock at 1,500 Hz. (2) The Vox key-down slot delays 3 / 17 / 47 are timer interrupts (identical at 200,000 / 100,000 / 50,000 / 25,000 instructions per interrupt), i.e. 0.125 / 0.71 / 1.96 ms. (3) Source the PUMP figures (clips note s3b), reproduce the free run (`pump_free_run_hz(73)` = 6.108 Hz) and record that the ARM sends no FX word while A:25 runs. (4) Answer OQ32 in the replay: `E` and the plain arpeggio are 2/3 of a step. (5) Correct the comments that call the tick 1 kHz / 1 ms (`arm_steady.py`, `arm-organ-messages.md`, `gain_dezip.rs`, `vox_timeline.rs`, `synth-engine.md`); no value, no default, no `render_hash` change.
- **Why:** The measurement is a register read and a run, so the 24 kHz is VERIFIED (the 24 MHz input clock is the firmware's constant, the board clock ASSUMED). The effect of the engine divergences (Vox attack, gain latch) is ASSUMED, not measured against hardware, and the ARM must also finish its work in 12,500 cycles per interrupt, so the engine constants stay as they are until a Vox clip or the ARM work time exists (D22).
- **Alternatives:** Flip `TICK_SECONDS` to 1/24,000 now (rejected: the real delay is the larger of the timer count and the ARM work time, unmeasured; default flips need a clip); change `INSNS_PER_TICK` to 12,500 (rejected: it alters every capture, listed as a follow-up); close OQ32 as 2/3 (rejected: no hardware recording and the flag writers are a candidate list, not traced).
- **Impact:** `tools/arm_tick_probe.py` (+ `tools/tests/test_arm_tick_probe.py`), `docs/research/arm-tick-timers.md`, `docs/research/data/arm-tick-{timers,keydelay,fxwatch,flagscan}.json`, OQ22, OQ32, new OQ36, [audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md) rows 17 to 19, ROADMAP 7b.6, comment-only edits in `arm_steady.py`, `arm-organ-messages.md`, `emulation-coverage.md`, `synth-engine.md`, `gain_dezip.rs`, `vox_timeline.rs`.

### D127. The firmware WAVE bank is the default wherever `NS4_WAVE_TABLES` names the user's OS image; the tables are never committed

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** the user asked for the real wavetable as the default without committing it (task from the coordinating session)
- **Context:** audio-remaining row 20: the engine plays a 64-table placeholder unless `--synth-wave-bank`; stage `0x1247B6` (320 of 320 oracle words) and the 46 tables are decoded, but the tables are firmware rodata and cannot be committed. 12 programs use WAVE.
- **Decision:** `FirmwareOptions::from_args` loads the tables from the file named by `NS4_WAVE_TABLES` (OS update container or raw `bank0_CODE`) and enables the bank, unless `--synth-wave-tables` is given (it wins) or `--no-synth-wave-bank` is given. Variable unset or empty: placeholder, as before. No path is auto-discovered.
- **Why:** The user's priority is the closest audio to the hardware (parity over purity); the image stays on the user's disk, so nothing firmware-derived enters git; reading only an explicit variable keeps rendering deterministic and CI unchanged. D22 is not met for audio (no hardware clip of a WAVE program exists): this default is the user's call, ASSUMED better than the placeholder, and the placeholder stays one flag away.
- **Alternatives:** Auto-discover `.reference/` or `target/fw_unpacked/` (rejected: makes renders depend on local files, breaks determinism); flip `FirmwareOptions::default()` (rejected: the tables are not available to tests and CI); embed the tables (rejected: firmware data, D22 and AGENTS.md rule 6).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-core/src/render_opts.rs`, new `render_opts_wave.rs` (+ tests), [synth-engine](specs/synth-engine.md) s4.4, audio-remaining row 20. No `render_hash` or golden change.

### D133. The live FX mismatch of reverb type 0 was the harness (35 stage calls per pass); the CHOR ports had a wrapped pre-modify store, fixed

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session, review notes applied; evidence [fx-reverb0-chor-oracle-diff](research/fx-reverb0-chor-oracle-diff.md))
- **Context:** D125 built `sharc-fxlive`; reverb type 0 and CHOR did not match `FwReverb` / `FirmwareFx::mod2` (reverb onset after 38 passes instead of 1352 calls, CHOR null depth -18 dB).
- **Decision:** (1) The dispatcher calls an installed head stage 35 times per pass (the 10 group headers and the head link all point at the head record); the install helper `null_chain()` points the head link and nine headers at a null stub record, giving one call per pass (`sharc-fxlive --calls-at PC` counts them). With it reverb type 0 equals `FwReverb` (null -137.0 dB impulse, -130.2 dB noise): no port change. (2) The CHOR ports `fw_chorus4.rs` / `fw_chorus5.rs` wrapped the pre-modify store `DM(M6, I6)` (`I6 + 0x202`); they use `Pe::plain` now (D114 did this for ENS, SPIN). The left wet tap was missing; CHOR 4 null -125.3 dB (noise; -119 to -156 dB over rate and amount 0..127), CHOR 5 -89.5 dB at rate 64 / amount 64 (the only context the oracle script loads for it), against -18 and -26 dB before. The CHOR 5 residual is below one 16-bit LSB (max error 1.7e-5 on a peak of 0.054) and uncorrelated with the input at lags 0..128; rounding is a hypothesis, not isolated. The Python twin `fx_model_chorus.py` got `Machine.plain`, `tests_chorus_golden.rs` is regenerated (it pins the port to the twin, not to hardware), the final-context tolerance is 2e-5, a regression test checks left and right wet taps and the guard copy. (3) `fx_run.rs` split (`fx_probe.rs`, `fx_wav.rs`), `fx_oracle_live.py` gets `chor5` and the null chain, `fx_live_native` gets `chor5`.
- **Why:** The oracle runs the real routine; from identical state the port differed in exactly one ring word, the mirror copy of a pre-modify store, the error D114 already removed elsewhere. Reverb needed no change: its port matched the golden vectors and now the live chain.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the old VERIFIED header (rejected: the direct-call check missed this bug); the header is now EMPIRICAL, backed by a local-only live check (`tools/fx_oracle_live.py`) because the repo has no committed oracle fixture; keep 35 calls per pass and fit a time-compressed native model (rejected: it is not the hardware chain).
- **Impact:** `fw_chorus4.rs`, `fw_chorus5.rs`, `fw_chorus.rs` (tests), `fw_mod2.rs` (test accessor), `tests_chorus_golden.rs`, `tools/fx_pe.py`, `tools/fx_model_chorus.py`, `tools/fx_oracle_live.py`, the `nord-sharc` runner split, `fx_live_native`; supersedes the "not reachable" status of D125; [fx-oracle-live](research/fx-oracle-live.md), [audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md) rows 6 and 7. The `mod2` CHOR output changes (opt-in, D22); `render_hash` unchanged (see the research doc).

### D132. The synth voice runs in the ORG core 2 oracle on the real ARM stream; the first end-to-end diff shows oscillator and filter both differ from native (neither dominates), the envelope is not comparable yet

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [synth-e2e-oracle-vs-native](research/synth-e2e-oracle-vs-native.md))
- **Context:** D122 showed the filter stages equal the Rust port on equal words, and D120 called ORG core 2 silent. The whole synth chain (oscillator, envelope, gain, filter) was never measured oracle against native on a played note.
- **Decision:** (1) Add `sharc-sys --tap ADDR:COUNT --tap-out FILE` (`runner/tap_run.rs`): `DM` windows after every pass, binary. (2) Add `synth_e2e_native` (one synth layer of a `.ns4p`, before layer level, pan, FX) and `tools/synth_e2e_oracle.py` / `synth_e2e_metrics.py`: recall stream and key words of `arm_dsp_capture.py` into ORG core 2, tap the channel 0 record (4 samples per pass) with the real filter and with the B stage nulled, compare levels, rise time, level-normalised third-octave shape, null depths. (3) Record the measured result on five factory programs: the voice sounds (the ORG pass has no output lane for it, the key word goes to PNO core 2 only); the stage rate 4 samples per pass at 86,804.69 Hz agrees with pass = 2 frames to under 1 cent on 2 of 5 programs and within 5 cent on 4 (EMPIRICAL; it stays ASSUMED elsewhere); the oscillator path level differs from native by `-1.9 .. +11.4` dB; the filter gain is `+4.9 .. +5.9` dB above native on four programs (Phat 3 `-2.2`; `20 log10 1.98 = 5.93`, ASSUMED link to the table A input gain); the shape departs `2.9 .. 29` dB before the filter and `21 .. 46` dB after. The oscillator term is at least the filter term on 2 of 5 programs, so **no stage is declared dominant**. (4) State the confounds: the envelope chain is identical in all five programs (the recall carries no envelope words), so the oracle's filter and amp modulation is not the program's; the oracle reads one channel record while native sums the layer's unison voices. (5) No engine, default or `render_hash` change.
- **Why:** It gives the first stage-by-stage numbers for D122's open "gap is upstream of the stage" and shows level and shape both differ at the oscillator and the filter output, with a repeatable filter gain offset. Reporting the envelope and voice count as confounds is honest: the identical traces prove the stream lacks those words, and the tap reads one channel.
- **Alternatives:** Compare the oracle only with a fitted native chain (rejected: hides the per-stage offsets); read an output lane (rejected: silent, section 4 of the note); wait for the edit-message envelope path before landing the harness (rejected: the harness, the rate check and the level numbers are reusable).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-sharc` (`runner/tap_run.rs`, `runner/mod.rs`, `bin/sharc_sys.rs`), `crates/nord-tools/src/bin/synth_e2e_native.rs`, `tools/synth_e2e_oracle.py`, `tools/synth_e2e_metrics.py` (+ tests), `docs/research/synth-e2e-oracle-vs-native.md`, `docs/research/data/synth-e2e-oracle-vs-native.csv`, audio-remaining row 3, OQ22.

### D129. The OS update file is the user's input: the app reads the WAVE tables from the official `.exe` / `.zip` / `.dmg` at run time and remembers only its path

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** the user asked for a layperson flow (task from the coordinating session): download the official Nord OS update, give that one file to the app
- **Context:** D127 made the firmware WAVE bank the default when `NS4_WAVE_TABLES` names an OS image, but the user had to unpack the container or `bank0_CODE` first. The tables are firmware rodata and are never committed (D22, AGENTS.md rule 6).
- **Decision:** (1) `os_update::extract_firmware_container` accepts the bare container, the Windows updater `.exe`, a `.zip` holding one, and the Mac `.dmg`; `synth::tables_from_file` calls it, so `--synth-wave-tables` and `NS4_WAVE_TABLES` take any of them (a raw `bank0_CODE` still works). (2) A per-user `ns4/config.json` keeps only the path; `ns4` and `nord-stage4` call `user_config::apply_saved_default` once at start, which fills `NS4_WAVE_TABLES` when it is unset. Precedence: flag, then variable, then saved path, then placeholder. Libraries and tests never read the file. (3) `ns4 firmware set|status|clear|info` and a GUI window (drop the file or paste its path, official links, status) set it. Errors say "this is not a Nord OS update", "unsupported Nord OS version", or name the damaged part.
- **Why:** One file from the vendor site is all a layperson can be asked for. The container is found by its own header and size sum instead of PE resource parsing, so no PE or filesystem library is needed and the USB driver resource (ID 105) is skipped by construction. The `.dmg` is a UDIF image with zlib chunks; decoding the chunks and scanning the flat image finds the container contiguous (HFS+ file extent), so no HFS reader is needed (VERIFIED on v1.66: [os-update-input](research/os-update-input.md)). Only a path is stored, so nothing firmware-derived is ever written (D22).
- **Alternatives:** Parse the PE resource directory (rejected: more code for the same bytes, breaks on a repacked updater); write an HFS+/APFS reader (not needed); ship `rfd` for a native file dialog (rejected for now: a large dependency tree; drag-and-drop plus a path field cover the one-time setup); cache the extracted tables on disk (rejected: firmware data on disk, D22).
- **Impact:** new `crates/nord-core/src/os_update/`, `user_config.rs`, `firmware_setting.rs`, `crates/nord-gui/src/os_update_panel.rs`; dependency `miniz_oxide` 0.8 (MIT / Zlib / Apache-2.0, already in the lock file through `image`); [synth-engine](specs/synth-engine.md) s4.4, [guide](guides/nord-os-update.md), ROADMAP. The container is read at every start. The WAVE tables install once per process, so a file chosen in the GUI applies after a restart. The v1.44 to v1.64 updaters unpack, but their registry address differs: "unsupported Nord OS version".

### D142. The synth velocity default flips to the firmware law on owner approval; the file bits are verified, four of 16 distinct synth clips are marginally worse

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-17 (default flip, owner approval relayed by the coordinating session; the D136 policy it overrides in one point is still proposed)
- **Decided by:** the owner, 2026-11-17 ("pode dar flip", relayed by the coordinating session): flip despite 4 of 16 distinct clips marginally worse at replay velocity 100; the performance velocity of the clips is unknown
- **Context:** The audit ranked `--firmware-synth-velocity` first for a default flip (D104: 768 of 768 oracle words within 1 ulp; 213 of 287 factory programs sensitive; "no clip varies the synth velocity, so none can worsen"). D104 left the file bits of `kAmpEnvVel`, `kFltEnvVel`, `kOscEnvVel` ASSUMED and the multiply of `V` and `F` in the voice ASSUMED.
- **Decision:** (1) `tools/arm_program_synth_velocity.py` recalls a factory program in the booted firmware (no poke) and reads the three edit values of every synth layer store: 27 of 27 equal the decoder values (Steam Piano, Sub Surface, Santoor Pluck; amp 0..3, flt and osc 0 / 1). The file bits are VERIFIED. (2) The multiply stays ASSUMED: `DM(0xC10B6)` is linear in `V` in the ORG core 2 oracle (ratio 6.81 on both), but the default image makes no sound, so no level ratio at the channel record exists; `kOscEnvVel` is not isolated. (3) The 17 Part A synth rows were scored with `tools/emu_vs_hw.py` at replay velocity 100: linear blend against the firmware law, mean 2.384 against 2.390; **worse**: `synth_horizon` (= `super_lp24_horizon`) 1.89 to 2.02, `super_lp24_oopad` 4.86 to 4.89, `synth_sineplk` 4.73 to 4.75, `synth_filterbank` 5.39 to 5.40; better: `synth_silkpad` 1.64 to 1.47, `organ_pipe` 1.74 to 1.70; 10 rows equal. The sign depends on the replay velocity (sweep v60 to v127 in [synth-velocity](research/synth-velocity.md) s9). (4) The D136 rule alone would keep the default off. The owner approved the flip on 2026-11-17, overriding the clip rule: owner approval 2026-11-17, 4 clips marginally worse at replay v100, performance velocity of clips unknown. `FirmwareOptions::synth_velocity_fw` is on by default (`SynthLayer::new`, `firmware_all`), `--legacy-synth-velocity` and `--legacy-all` turn it off, `--firmware-synth-velocity` stays accepted; the GUI bar gets the switch `Synth: Firmware velocity` (D104 follow-up 4).
- **Why:** The law is VERIFIED against the oracle (768 of 768 words) and the file bits against the recall; the hardware clips cannot judge it because their performance velocity is unknown (the sign of the change depends on it) and the four worse rows are within 0.01 to 0.13 score (mean 2.384 to 2.390). The owner chose parity with the firmware over the strict clip rule. The D104 / D136 premise that no clip can worsen was wrong: every clip replays at one velocity and the law moves the level at it (+3.3 dB for setting 3 at `g(100)`: `V` 1.123 against the linear blend 98 / 127 = 0.77, 20 log10 (1.123 / 0.772) = 3.26 dB, [synth-velocity](research/synth-velocity.md) s2). The clips do not give the performance velocity, so they cannot confirm the law either way.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the default off (the first version of this decision; overridden by the owner); flip only the filter-envelope factor `F` (rejected: not tested apart from `V`, and the horizon clip has no filter velocity layer, so the clip result would not change but the claim "no worse" would be untested); score at velocity 80 where the law wins on `synth_horizon` (rejected: the velocity is not known, v80 loses `synth_silkpad` and `synth_filterbank`).
- **Impact:** `render_opts.rs` (defaults, `--legacy-synth-velocity`, summary prints `legacy-synth-velocity` when off, test), `synth/mod.rs` (`SynthLayer::new` on, `legacy` off), `tools/arm_program_synth_velocity.py` (+ test), GUI `SYNTH_VELOCITY` switch (flag `--legacy-synth-velocity`), [synth-velocity](research/synth-velocity.md) s7 to s9, clip data, ROADMAP 7b.6, audio-remaining, emulation-coverage, synth-engine spec, firmware-flag-audit. `render_hash` `46f4dc64...06` to `eb13063d4fca837a613670168a005b564c7f235a1578136d03955fa968d2ca28`; goldens: segment `engine` peak 0.951197 to 1.000000, RMS 0.208288 to 0.215027; segment `synth` peak 0.236886 to 0.424921, RMS 0.081122 to 0.140556; `organ`, `piano`, `fx` unchanged. `strip_voice_level_follows_the_key_velocity_series` now pins the legacy blend. Every default render of a program with velocity sensitive synth layers changes (213 of 287 factory programs with a synth layer); the program sweep is not regenerated. Open: performance velocities of the clips, the multiply of `V` and `F` in the voice, `kOscEnvVel`; the engine segment peaks at 1.0 (the limiter), watch clipping at high velocity.

### D140. Synth oscillator stages and the voice constant 0.25 equal the oracle (P gain); idle rows Other, Fm and SHAPE square sit +8 dB high and get an opt-in oracle table; native analog unison level is 2 to 8 dB off and not fixed

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [synth-osc-gain-oracle](research/synth-osc-gain-oracle.md))
- **Context:** D132 found the oscillator-path level offset at least as large as the filter offset on two of five programs, with unison, layer count and envelope as confounds. The task: isolate the oscillator level per wave and the voice gain in the oracle against native, fix the voice-count confound, and decide per finding whether a native constant is wrong or the oracle input is incomplete.
- **Decision:** (1) Add `tools/synth_osc_gain.py` (one oracle oscillator-path run per program, five native variants `default`, `unity`, `verified`, `g1`, `g1u0`, `needed_row_gain`) and `synth_e2e_native --unity --unison N`; run 46 programs (layer A, 11 wave families, unison 0 baseline plus unison 1 to 3). (2) Record: native oscillator times the voice constant `0.25` equals the ORG core 2 stage (`P[16] = 0.25`) at row gain 1.0 within 0.1 dB (noise, SHAPE square, SHAPE triangle, sine, saw 2 of 3 programs, FM 2 of 3; outliers `J3 Open Sky` +3.4 dB and `Necklace Upright` -0.4 dB), pulse and MULTI / SUPER follow their words; layer count has no effect on channel 0; the native analog unison level is `+2.4 .. +8.3 dB` below the oracle lane on saw, SHAPE triangle and SHAPE saw (SHAPE square ignores unison, `-2.0 dB` against the oracle at unison 3). (3) Add the opt-in `--synth-p oracle`: the built-in table with the never-fitted rows SHAPE square, ANALOG `Other` and `Fm` at `GAIN_PER_P16 x 0.25 = 0.4`; `PWave::Fm` and `PWave::Sampled` split from `Other` (all three idle by default, no default or `render_hash` change). (4) Do **not** change the fitted rows (SHAPE triangle, MULTI, SUPER, SHAPE SINE) or the unison level: the verified rows worsen 5 of 8 clip RMS errors, they are coupled to the native filter gain (D132), and the one unison hold-out clip goes the other way. (5) State the open oracle inputs: no envelope words, no output stage (the absolute gain `D = 0.4` stays the EMPIRICAL saw anchor), SUPER 1 .. 4 and two other programs silent, WAVE not on the stream.
- **Why:** The measurement turns D132's open "oscillator or filter" into a row-gain structure: the stages and the constant are right, the idle rows are +8 dB, the fitted rows hide the filter gain. `--synth-p oracle` is the only change with a clip number (`synth_12db` RMS error `+6.65 -> +0.21 dB`, band score `1.39 -> 1.50`, ten other synth clips and `pianopad_grand` unchanged to 0.01 dB), so the owner can flip it under D136 without a regression elsewhere.
- **Alternatives:** Flip `--synth-p verified` (rejected: worsens `synth_lead`, `synth_80s`, `synth_megaline`, `synth_silkpad`, `synth_horizon`); scale the unison sum to the oracle level now (rejected: `synth_crunch` is already `+2.0 dB` over hardware, the oracle lane rotation is the right model); change `Other` for the sample modes too (rejected: not on the oracle stream, so `Sampled` stays idle).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-core/src/synth/p_table.rs` (rows `Fm`, `Sampled`), `p_wave_mode.rs` (new), `p_words.rs` (`PTableSpec::oracle`), `p_block.rs` (`PSpec::Oracle`, `--synth-p oracle`), `synth_params.rs`, `crates/nord-tools/src/bin/synth_e2e_native.rs`, `tools/synth_osc_gain.py` (+ tests), `docs/research/synth-osc-gain-oracle.md`, `docs/research/data/synth-osc-gain-oracle.csv`, [synth-engine](specs/synth-engine.md) s4.2, audio-remaining row 3, ROADMAP. Also removes the stray merge-conflict markers that #488 left in this file (both D129 and D132 kept).
### D136. Firmware-validated paths become the default unless a hardware clip gets worse; the owner's rule of 2026-11-17

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** the owner's rule of 2026-11-17, relayed by the coordinating session; the policy text below is a proposal until the owner confirms it
- **Context:** `FirmwareOptions::default()` keeps many oracle-validated paths off (piano Touch, synth velocity, FX defaults, ...). D22 (2026-11-13) requires an end-to-end verification and a hardware clip or the owner's explicit OK before any default flips. The audit [firmware-flag-audit](research/firmware-flag-audit.md) lists every opt-in flag with its oracle evidence, clip numbers and blocker.
- **Decision:** The owner's rule, recorded as the proposed policy: everything validated against the firmware is the default, as long as it does not make the hardware-clip comparison worse. In practice: (1) a flag whose algorithm and ARM/preset mapping are VERIFIED (D22 criteria 1 and 2) and for which no clip gets worse becomes the default; (2) where no clip exists the effect on parity cannot be measured and the owner's explicit OK is still required, as D22 says, and one PR per flip records it with the `render_hash` re-baseline (D22 criterion 3); (3) a flag with any clip that gets worse, or that the owner vetoes by ear (EMPIRICAL listening, not a clip), stays opt-in and names the missing piece in the audit; (4) EMPIRICAL and ASSUMED paths (for example `--measured-laws`, `--synth-super-lp24`) are not firmware-validated and stay opt-in; (5) flags that need the user's own firmware image (D127, D128, D129) are default only where that image is present. (6) "Firmware-verified words" is not "validated": the role of a verified word can be wrong or unproven. Example: `--firmware-fx-ports reverb-mix` (`kRvbAmt` words VERIFIED, role EMPIRICAL): the owner heard little difference with it on or off on one program (A:21 White Grand, Reverb Cathedral at knob 3; listening, EMPIRICAL, no clip). Its role stays unverified until a hardware RT60 clip exists, so it is not flipped. The `reverb` port (`--firmware-reverb`) was heard worse than the engine reverb by the owner (A:21 White Grand, Cathedral knob 3, 2026-11-17, by ear, EMPIRICAL, one program); this is an owner veto by ear under point (3), not a clip result; a hardware RT60 measurement is wanted.
- **Why:** The goal is audio 1:1 with the hardware. Keeping verified paths off costs parity on every default render; D22's original reason (a verified routine with a guessed mapping, the B3 mix-up) is kept by criteria 1 and 2.
- **Alternatives:** Keep D22 unchanged (rejected by the owner: leaves verified paths off); flip everything oracle-validated regardless of clips (rejected: `--synth-fw-resonance` 3.20 against 2.18 under D108, `--synth-filter-law 1.015` 3.88 against 2.18, piano pedal voices on Velvet 1.37 to 2.74 would have been flipped).
- **Update 2026-11-17 (D142):** the owner overrode the clip rule for one flag: `--firmware-synth-velocity` became the default although 4 of 16 clips are marginally worse.
- **Impact:** Documentation only in this change; no default, golden or `render_hash` changes. The audit's ranked list gives the order of flips.


### D156. SHARC census follow-ups: sites counted in real passes, MR2F read-back kept as a pinned simulator artefact, JIT and AOT share the five semantics, four files split

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** the user's task for this session (finish the census follow-ups of D151; no default flips)
- **Context:** D151 closed 5 of 6 census-diff shapes but the census sites were not instrumented, `MR1F = R3, R1 = DM(..)` (MR2F read-back) stayed open, JIT and AOT were not compared, and `moves.rs` (614 lines), `mod.rs` (588), `shifter.rs` (547), `float_ops.rs` (493) exceeded the 400-line rule.
- **Decision:** (1) Add an opt-in per-pc counter (`NS4_SITE_HITS`, `core/site_hits.rs`, interpreter only). Of the 15 sites 2 execute every PNO pass (MR2F, `0x1211C4/C7`) and 3 execute in the FX ENS routine (`0x123AD4/D6`, `0x123B12`); the other 10 pcs of the list are not reached by any run available (ORG and PNO `0x18xxxx` block, FX core 1 ENS, PNO `0x1247AF`, `0x120E01`). SY has no site. (2) Old against new model: PNO core 1 (WAV and 18 lanes, 3,000 passes, Vox and Pipe), ORG core 2 state and FX ENS (96 scenarios) are bit-identical. (3) MR2F: the simulator sign-extends from the previous MR2 on a register read after `MR1F = Rn` (28 of 120 states differ in that one word); the firmware sequence with the accumulate is equal in 120 of 120. The model keeps the sign-extending read (measured for multiplier results) and both facts are pinned by `census_regress.rs`. Conditions 5, 6, 21, 22 get a direct unit test (`condition.rs`). (4) The JIT replays 1,580 census cases equal to the interpreter; the AOT lowers none of the closed shapes natively and `Pe::shift` equals the interpreter shifter (`census_engines.rs`). (5) Split `moves` into `moves` + `moves_types`, `shifter` + `shifter_bits`, `float_ops` + `reg_moves`, `mod` into `mod` + `condition` + `types` + `addr_hash` + `new`, without behaviour change (all files under 400 lines, 495 + new tests, identical outputs).
- **Why:** The oracle is the reference for every audio conclusion; a model fix is only worth trusting if its effect on real passes is known. The MR2F difference needs the previous MR2 image, a state the model does not carry, and nothing in the firmware reads it.
- **Alternatives:** Model the simulator's MR2 register image (rejected: extra state in JIT/AOT for a read the firmware never makes, silicon behaviour unmeasured); count sites inside the JIT (rejected: interpreter counts are enough, the JIT falls back to it for these shapes); two PRs for refactor and semantics (rejected: the semantics part is tests and docs only, no model change).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-sharc/src/core/{site_hits,census_engines,condition,types,addr_hash,new,moves_types,shifter_bits,reg_moves}.rs`, `census.rs` (engine selector), `census_regress.rs`, fixtures `census_mr2*.txt`, [sharc-census-diff](research/sharc-census-diff.md), [emulation-coverage](research/emulation-coverage.md), ROADMAP 7b.1, OQ37. No engine, golden or default changes. Does not touch D118 or the scheduler PRs.

### D153. The SY chip runs live behind sharc-live (interrupt driven, opt-in); the stub never sent `go` because the ARM polls inside one tick; with the poll fed `go` is sent, Choir reaches playing, past-first-ring is not shown

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [sy-live-chip](research/sy-live-chip.md), data `docs/research/data/sy-live-*.json`)
- **Context:** D144 left the SY chip blocked (interrupt driven image, no poll loop); D147 left voice free and stealing open and attributed 40,682 status reads to the wait at `0xC01E0694`.
- **Decision:** (1) `sharc-live --sy` (`SY:0=sy=<image>` in `arm_dsp_live`) models the SY1SY2 image as interrupt driven: per pass the FIFO pairs are fed and vector 13 (FIFO reset, `0x1C03FD`) raised, the staging ring of prepared voices is refilled with the D111 synthetic header, then vector 17 (audio ISR `0x1C05B9`) is raised `--isrs` times (default 2 = one pass of the 24 kHz ARM tick). Opt-in; no default changes. (2) The live chip answers the return path before the message driven mask. (3) The 40,682 reads of D147 are a second wait (return `0xC01E26FC`, `DM(0x9219A)`, descriptor in `r4`) spinning inside one ARM tick before the harness delivers its messages: with the stub, with the live chip and without a fed poll it never passes. `--poll-advance N` (message feed plus one pass per `N` reads of the same word) makes it pass after 192 reads and the ARM sends `prepare` and `go` for 3 of 3 chord notes (stub: 1 and 0). (4) Choir voice 0 reaches the playing state and keeps non-silent output for the whole run; Guitar and Quintet voice 0 alternate waiting and release. Past the first 128 frame ring, voice free and stealing are **not shown** in the paths run (step word reads 0, no state store after note-off). Audio from the live chip is the synthetic stand-in and is not a parity claim.
- **Why:** The numbers are in the research note; the stub could not distinguish a missing chip from a message delivery lag, the live chip can.
- **Alternatives:** A stub that sets the release bit on `prepare` (rejected: hides the timing the chip gives); `--epchain` as default (rejected: no external sample image); running passes on a wall clock inside the instruction loop (follow-up, larger change).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-sharc/src/runner/live_sy.rs`, `live.rs`, `live_presets.rs`, `periph_run.rs`, `bin/sharc_live.rs`; `tools/arm_dsp_live.py`, `arm_dsp_route.py`, `arm_dsp_return.py`, `arm_dsp_link.py`, `arm_sy_timeline.py` (+ tests); research note, ROADMAP, emulation-coverage, audio-remaining, OQ22; no render change.

### D147. The DSP to ARM return path is SPI reads of DSP memory; sample ring refill is not an ARM signal; an opt-in return source answers them

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [dsp-to-arm-return](research/dsp-to-arm-return.md))
- **Context:** D138 left the DSP to ARM direction as telemetry; D143 left the SPI1 device behind `0xC00119A4` open.
- **Decision:** (1) The ARM reads DSP state only through SPI reads of DSP data memory (`0xC00119A4`, `0xC00117E8`, `0xC0066E64`); SPI1 is the DSP SPI slave. In a note it reads the SY idle voice mask `DM(0x92196)` and 3 peeks of `DM(0xC1DDC..F)` (PNO core 2, ORG cores 1, 2). (2) Add the opt-in `tools/arm_dsp_return.py` return source and `arm_dsp_live.py --return-path`; nothing changes unless used. (3) Sample ring refill is not an ARM signal. (4) For 2 SAMPLE programs run in three modes the SY words, voice updates and key-down delay equal the neutral stub; voice free timing needs a live SY chip.
- **Why:** The reads are the only DSP to ARM data path found; the answers from images and shadow reproduce the D143 neutral read.
- **Alternatives:** Invent a ring refill reply (rejected: no ARM reader exists); model the release transition in Python (rejected: it is a DSP sample position test).
- **Impact:** `tools/arm_dsp_return.py`, `tools/arm_dsp_return_run.py`, `tools/arm_dsp_live.py`, research note, ROADMAP, emulation-coverage, audio-remaining row 2, OQ22; no render change.

### D143. The 59 stopped SAMPLE programs of the D124 capture were a harness artefact (SPI1 stand-in RAM defers the voice event); the SPI read is neutralised, no flash or DMA model

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [sample-voice-gate](research/sample-voice-gate.md))
- **Context:** D124 left 59 of 119 SAMPLE programs at the synth voice gate, undiagnosed, with the open question whether it relates to the missing pitch entry `e`.
- **Decision:** (1) The gate is `0xC01DEAFC` returning 0: the slot counter `[0x90639A50 + 4]` (`|signed byte|` of the SPI refill table `0xC05A4AB0`) is above 1, filled from the SPI1 data register `0x01E12040`, which the harness serves as auto-mapped RAM with test-pattern leftovers (3 or 1). (2) `tools/arm_voice_gate.py` zeroes `r12` at the two SPI store pcs (`0xC0011AEC`, `0xC0011930`); `arm_dsp_capture.py` applies it by default (`--no-spi-neutral` restores the old stream). (3) Measured on key 60: gated voiced 32, stopped 87; neutral voiced 110, **78 of 87 fixed**, 0 regressions; 9 programs queue no voice (6 scene II only, 3 scene I only), undiagnosed. (4) No change to `e`; `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in.
- **Why:** The pc traces of a stopped and a voiced program are identical until the scheduler return value; one compare (`cmp r3, #1`) differs and its word comes from a register the harness does not model. Neutralising it makes the voice update run in the stopped program with no other change.
- **Alternatives:** Model SPI flash or DMA (rejected: the gate is not the sample data path); force `0xC01DEAFC` to return 1 (rejected: skips the deadline logic, the data fix is smaller); leave undiagnosed (rejected).
- **Impact:** `tools/arm_voice_gate.py` (+ test), `tools/arm_dsp_capture.py` (+ test), `docs/research/sample-voice-gate.md`, `docs/research/data/voice-gate-*.json`; [arm-dsp-capture](research/arm-dsp-capture.md) follow-up 2 answered; [audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md) row 2; OQ22 note; no engine change. Limit: the hardware value of the SPI read is ASSUMED neutral (0).


### D161. GUI audio glitches: the firmware FX racks run over budget; an opt-in idle skip stops silent racks, the E40 arithmetic gets a bit-identical fast path

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-17 (opt-in only; no default flips)
- **Decided by:** task from the parent session (evidence [gui-audio-glitches](research/gui-audio-glitches.md)); the default of `--fx-idle-skip` stays with the owner. D161 chosen: D157 is on `main`; D158 is #515, D159 is #516, D160 is #519.
- **Context:** The GUI on A:16 Horizon Whl with `tone=Firmware filter=Firmware fx` showed 5 percent of blocks late and a worst block of 75.8 ms against 10 ms. `ns4 bench --gui` with the program loaded, the arpeggiator on and each block's fastest of 3 runs shows no tail spike (max 9 to 17 ms against a mean of 6 to 15), so the spikes are host scheduling on top of a render that is itself at 1.2 to 1.5 times the budget: the firmware FX racks cost 12 to 15 ms per 480 frames on Horizon Whl (all seven racks run a firmware Cathedral reverb at about 1 ms per rack, Mod 2, EQ and compressor add 2 to 4 ms), against 0.6 ms for the engine FX.
- **Decision:** (1) `ns4 bench --gui` gains `--backup`, `--program`, `--arp`, `--repeat` and an `FX racks:` line. (2) `E40::add` takes an exact-f64 fast path and `E40::mul` a 64 bit pack; the output is bit-identical (oracle goldens, a 200 000 pair random test against the integer path, equal `render_hash`). (3) New opt-in `LayerFxChain::set_idle_skip` / `--fx-idle-skip` / GUI "Skip idle FX racks": a rack with input exactly 0 and output under 1e-7 for 4 s returns silence until input returns. Default off.
- **Why:** The racks of silent layers (the other scene, unused layers) are most of the FX cost in a Scene I program. Skipping them cut Horizon Whl Scene I from 12 to 15 ms to 2.7 to 3.8 ms per block with an identical output hash, while all sounding racks run unchanged. It is not bit-identical to the always-run path once a rack wakes (phases and read pointers stand still while it sleeps), so by the owner's rule it is a switch.
- **Alternatives:** Skip by layer mask (rejected: needs the scene semantics and misses silent layers inside a scene); skip with a shorter window (rejected: a 2 s delay echo has silent gaps); catch-up of the frozen state on wake (rejected: not exact for the register machine); flip the default now (rejected: owner decision); a soft limiter for the hard clamp at the master bus (found, not changed: [gui-audio-glitches](research/gui-audio-glitches.md) s2, follow-up).
- **Impact:** `fx/idle_skip.rs`, `fx/fw_ext40.rs`, `bench_gui*.rs`, `render_opts*.rs`, `firmware_panel`, docs. No default, golden or `render_hash` change. Open: Scene II programs that play all seven layers with firmware reverbs stay over budget (the reverb itself must get cheaper, TD-14), and the master bus clamps hard at 1.0.

### D158. FX types against the FX chip oracle: every wired port matches, the engine paths differ; 4-frame Mod 1 option opt-in

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** the parent session's task (compare every FX type of the engine with the FX chip; fixes only behind opt-in flags; no default flips)
- **Context:** Only reverb type 0 and chorus had been compared with the FX chip (D125, D145). The engine paths of the other types had no firmware reference, and the live chain pass (4 frames) differs from the 2-frame call of the Mod 1 rest port.
- **Decision:** (1) Export the port contexts (`fx/fw_live.rs`, `live_install`) so the SHARC oracle runs the same routine on the same stimulus; compare Mod 1, Mod 2, reverb types 0 and 2 to 11 (not 6) and the compressor with `tools/fx_vs_oracle.py`: all match (level delta 0.00 dB, null -72 dB or better). (2) Add `LayerFxChain::mod1_rest_frames` (default 2, opt-in 4) because RM / A-WAH / WAH / PUMP null only to -16 .. -26 dB at 2 frames and to -100 dB or better at 4. (3) Report engine against port per type in `fx_engine_vs_fw` and `docs/research/fx-vs-oracle.md`: the engine differs in level (up to 17 dB for reverb at amount 127, 5.9 dB for Mod 2) and response. (4) Record, not fix: amp drive 127 silent in the port, EQ and amp drive not run live, Leslie not installable, Stage reverb and delay feedback FX have no port. No default changes.
- **Why:** A port that nulls the oracle is firmware-accurate, so engine against port is engine against firmware. The 4-frame mode matches the live pass exactly; whether the instrument calls the routines with 2 or 4 frames is unknown, so the default stays.
- **Alternatives:** Flip defaults to the ports (rejected: needs the owner's OK, D22). Change the Mod 1 default to 4 frames (rejected: call count unverified). Fix the amp 127 silence (rejected: unchecked against the oracle).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-core/src/fx/{fw_live,fw_mod1_rest,mod}.rs` and the port stages (context export), `crates/nord-tools/src/{fx_cases,bin/fx_engine_vs_fw,bin/fx_live_install,bin/fx_live_native}.rs`, `tools/fx_vs_oracle.py`, `docs/research/fx-vs-oracle.md`, `docs/research/data/fx-*.csv`, OQ38, ROADMAP 7b.4.

### D168. The 2/3 arpeggiator gate is the default; the pattern flag is written only by the constructor; `--no-arp-gate` opts out

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-19 (follows up D166 and D130; does not rewrite them)
- **Decided by:** owner (ymatrix1), 2026-11-19: "aprova o flip, pode mergear o 531", relayed by the coordinating session. The owner's rule is to flip where the result already matches the firmware; the gate has no hardware clip, the owner accepted the ARM evidence.
- **Context:** D130 / D166 kept the 2/3 gate opt-in (`--arp-fw-gate`) because 37 static candidate stores to `[pattern obj + 0xC]` were not traced and the 30 A:16 captures replay one boot state. The owner hears A:16 Horizon Whl far from the hardware; the arpeggio is its most audible part.
- **Decision:** (1) Trace the flag dynamically: `tools/arm_arp_flag_trace.py` (write hook over the six pattern objects, code hooks on the 37 sites and the constructor, `--patch LAYER.field=value` on the original `.ns4p` bits, `--force-flag`) and `tools/arp_flag_census.py` fold 32 programs and 9 variants of A:16 into `docs/research/data/arp-flag-census*.json`. Result ([arp-gate-flag-trace](research/arp-gate-flag-trace.md)): 0 stores to `[obj + 0xC]` after boot, constructor 6 calls in boot and 0 after, flags 1 at every phase, 29 of 37 candidate stores executed (21,908 times) none on a pattern object, the other 8 store the constants 1 / 2 (non-zero keeps the grid) or belong to list records (disassembly). 53 ARM-played layers of 30 of the 32 arpeggiator programs, in Arp, Poly and Gate mode, free run and Master Clock, have gate = 2/3 of the grid step (fractions 0.222 / 0.333 / 0.444 are notes spaced 1.5, 2 or 3 steps by pattern rests). (2) `SynthLayer::arp_firmware_gate` and `FirmwareOptions::arp_firmware_gate` default **on** (also `--firmware-all`); `--no-arp-gate` turns only the gate off, `--legacy-arp` turns walk and gate off, `--arp-fw-gate` stays accepted, `program_sweep` and `arp_trace` take the same flags; the summary names `no-arp-gate`. The single-note retrigger was already the default since D166 (part of `arp_firmware`). (3) `ArpMode::Gate` keeps its 4/8 duty: the ARM retriggers the chord with a 2/3 gate (3 programs), recorded as a follow-up.
- **Why:** The ARM is the reference and parity beats purity (owner preference).
