The flag has no writer after boot in any run and no candidate store touches a pattern object, so the boot grid is the state the firmware plays in. On the 16 layers where native and ARM report the same step, the gate matches within 1 ms on 13 with the gate on (1 with it off).
- **Alternatives:** Keep it opt-in until a hardware clip exists (rejected: owner approval for flips where the firmware already matches; the opt-out is one flag); port the Gate-mode retrigger and the Poly / pattern step structure in the same change (rejected: separate differences visible in the census, not the flag).
- **Impact:** `render_opts.rs`, `render_opts_arp.rs`, `synth/{mod,arp_gate}.rs`, `nord-tools::sweep_flags`, tests, new tools and data above, spec synth-engine, audio-remaining row 19, firmware-flag-audit, OQ32, ROADMAP 7b.6, beans ns4-hwe8. **Not touched (task rule):** `nord-gui` (its ARP GATE switch still reads "opt-in" in `firmware_panel/info.rs`; the box now starts checked because it copies the default) and `nord-plugin` (plays the gate by default, no opt-out). Caveat: no hardware recording of an arp note against its step.
### D167. GUI audio glitches round 2: raise the callback thread (cpal fails to), keep program loads allocation free, count master clamp hits and callback gaps

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-19 (tooling, scheduling and allocation changes only; no sound change, no default flip of a sound path)
- **Decided by:** task from the parent session (evidence [gui-audio-glitches](research/gui-audio-glitches.md) section 6); the defaults of `--fx-idle-skip` and a soft limiter stay with the owner.
- **Context:** The owner still heard crackle in `nord-gui` after D161. A real cpal run (`nord-stage4 --audio-probe`, new) showed the callback thread at priority 0 without an MMCSS class: cpal 0.15.3 passes the thread id as the `HANDLE` of `SetThreadPriority`, so its boost fails silently. Under CPU contention (40 busy processes) the default start-up engine in Scene II lost 13 of 1204 callbacks, worst 20.9 ms, although its load is 26 percent. The defaults are not render bound on this host (Horizon Whl Scene I 25 to 30 percent load), `--firmware-fx` is (86 to 88 percent). Program load and Mod 2 type changes built stage boxes and freed program boxes and sound banks on the audio thread (TD-18, TD-12). The master bus hard clamps at plus or minus 1.0 and the default start-up engine in Scene II clamps 6 to 49 percent of the frames of a chord, invisible to the over-0-dBFS count.
- **Decision:** (1) The first callback sets `THREAD_PRIORITY_TIME_CRITICAL` on `GetCurrentThread()` and joins the MMCSS task `Pro Audio`; `--no-thread-boost` opts out. (2) `standalone_engine` reserves every firmware Mod 1 / Mod 2 stage box (`Stage4Engine::reserve_audio_stages`) and a type change re-initialises a box in place. (3) `LoadProgram`, `LoadPianoSound`, `LoadSynthSample` hand the spent box or replaced bank to a reaper thread through a bounded queue instead of freeing it in the callback. (4) The meter reports callback gaps, `clamped` frames (`MasterBus::clamped_frames`, a counter) and the thread class. (5) Tools: `nord-stage4 --audio-probe`, `ns4 bench --gui --reload`.
- **Why:** The probe table pairs (boost off and on, same render) show the loss disappear: 13 late blocks and 20.9 ms worst to 0 and 4.5 ms (row C), 68 to 24 ms worst under contention on the render-bound case. The output of every change is unchanged (`render_hash` equal; `fx::tests_reserve_stages` compares bit for bit), and a counting allocator proves 0 allocator calls on program loads (`no_alloc_fx_stages`).
- **Alternatives:** A render-ahead thread with a ring (rejected for now: adds latency or complexity, the boost fixed the measured losses, `--audio-buffer 960` exists); pre-building stages on the control thread and swapping them in (rejected: the stages live inside the chains, which `SwapState` does not carry, so reserving boxes at start is simpler and bit-identical); flip `--fx-idle-skip` or add a soft limiter now (rejected: not bit-identical or changes sound, owner decision, numbers in the research note); the `thread-priority` crate (rejected: one short FFI call, `windows-sys` is already in the lock file).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-gui/src/audio/{priority,garbage,probe,callback,meter,io,standalone}.rs`, `crates/nord-gui/Cargo.toml` (`windows-sys`, a plain dependency on Windows targets only, so the plugin build compiles `priority.rs` too), `crates/nord-core/src/fx/{master,modulation,mod}.rs`, `engine/program_fx.rs`, `piano.rs`, `synth/mod.rs` (`replace_sound`, `replace_sample`), `bench_gui*.rs`, `tests/no_alloc_fx_stages.rs`, docs (TD-12, TD-14, TD-18, gui-audio, research, ROADMAP). Open: the master clamp (soft limiter opt-in), the seven-reverb Scene II render cost, the idle skip default, the plugin path.

### D166. The firmware arpeggiator walk (with the single-note retrigger) and the firmware morph law become the defaults; the 2/3 arp gate stays opt-in; `--legacy-arp` and `--legacy-morph` opt out

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-18 for the walk, the retrigger and the morph law (default flips); the 2/3 gate is **not** flipped (opt-in `--arp-fw-gate`, see below)
- **Decided by:** the owner, 2026-11-18, in chat: "pode fazer o flip se ja bate com firmware" (flip the opt-in firmware paths to the default where they already match the firmware), relayed to this session by the coordinating session. The gate is split out on the review of PR #523: it does not "already match the firmware" in the sense of D130 / D22 / D136 (no hardware clip), so it stays opt-in until the owner accepts that caveat.
- **Context:** `--firmware-morph` (D159) and the firmware arpeggiator (D162, `arp_firmware_walk`; the 2/3 gate was only a recorded difference, OQ32, bean ns4-hwe8) were opt-in. The owner hears A:16 Horizon Whl far from the Part A video. 32 of 384 factory programs use the arpeggiator; 126 carry a morph slot.
- **Decision:** (1) **Morph:** re-run evidence of D159 (`tools/morph_law_report.py`: firmware residual 0.0001 steps against 35.99 for the native law on the delay rate; weight `v / 128`, clamp on the sum, cutoff sender step drop, five extra synth targets). `FirmwareOptions::firmware_morph` and `Stage4Engine::firmware_morph` default on; `--legacy-morph` opts out; `--firmware-morph` stays accepted. Because the five extra synth slots (Osc Ctrl, resonance, LFO rate, LFO amount, filter envelope amount) now play by default, they join Copy / Paste / Clear Morph (`engine/morph_clip.rs`), a live edit of one of those fields becomes its new base and cleared depths put the base back once (`SynthMorphSet::apply`); an edit of only the FM amount or the pulse width is not seen (Osc Ctrl is read through the supersaw detune). (2) **Arpeggiator walk and retrigger (default):** 30 ARM replays of A:16 with direction and RANGE patched in the file bits (`docs/research/data/arp-fw-range.json`, `arp-fw-vs-native.md` s7). Up, Down and Up/Down walk the octave pool (D162 had only Up/Down); the pool holds the chord notes plus `n + 12 k` whose offset from the lowest key is below a RANGE span; a single held key retriggers every step. `FirmwareOptions::arp_firmware` (default on) sets `SynthLayer::arp_firmware_walk`; `--legacy-arp` opts out (and turns the gate off), `--arp-fw-walk` and `--arp-firmware` stay accepted. The span is a piecewise-linear law (`synth/arp_pool.rs`, `SPAN_KNOTS`, EMPIRICAL): a straight `12 + raw / 3` failed raw 7, 14 and 57 and was dropped. Zig zag and Random keep the engine walk (no capture). (3) **Arpeggiator gate (opt-in):** `E` and the plain arpeggio last 2/3 of a step in all 30 captures, but the 30 captures replay one boot state of the pattern grid (flag `[obj + 0xC]`, writer not found, no hardware clip), so they are one observation, and D130 declined to close OQ32 as 2/3 for that reason. `SynthLayer::arp_firmware_gate` and `FirmwareOptions::arp_firmware_gate` default **off** (also under `--firmware-all`); `--arp-fw-gate` and the GUI switch "Arp gate 2/3 step" turn it on; `q` / `Q` keep the full step, in-between GUI eighths interpolate (ASSUMED). Flipping it later is one line (`FirmwareOptions::default` and `SynthLayer::new`) once the owner accepts the caveat or a clip / the flag writer confirms 2/3; bean ns4-hwe8 stays open for that. (4) `program_sweep` gets `--legacy-arp`, `--legacy-morph`, `--arp-fw-gate`, `--wheel`, `--aftertouch`, `--pedal` (`nord-tools::sweep_flags`); the GUI firmware panel gets ARP, ARP GATE and MORPH switches.
- **Measured effect:** the `render_hash` golden `eb13063d...ca28` is unchanged, but it **does not exercise** the change (no arpeggiator and no controller in its segment), so it proves only that nothing else moved. The 384-program sweep (`program_sweep`, derived rows in `docs/research/data/arp-morph-flip-sweep*.csv`, compared with `tools/sweep_flip_compare.py`; commands in `arp-fw-vs-native.md` s7) gives, default and firmware-all modes, one held note: `--legacy-arp --legacy-morph` is **identical to the build before this change on 384 of 384**; with the new defaults and the controllers at rest **5 of the 32 arpeggiator programs change and nothing else** (Horizon Whl, Superior Sequel, Dramatic Opening, Rudiments, ZigZag Arp J3). With Mod Wheel, Aftertouch and Pedal all at 64 the morph law changes 101 of the 126 programs that carry a morph slot (0 programs without one); at 127 it changes 31 (the five extra slots only: at full travel the weight is 1.0 in both laws and the sender step drop does not fire). The sweep plays a single key, so chord and range effects of the walk are measured by the ARM captures and the unit tests, not by the sweep. The sweep numbers of the first version of this PR (27 of 32 arpeggiator programs) included the gate and are superseded.
- **Why:** The ARM is the reference, parity beats purity (owner preference). The morph law is VERIFIED by running the firmware and nothing moves at rest. The walk and the retrigger match every ARM capture on two layers and do not depend on the unknown flag. The gate depends on it, so it follows the D130 rule.
- **Alternatives:** Flip the gate with the walk (rejected on review: one replayed state, D130 / D22 / D136); flip only the morph (rejected: the owner approved both where evidence exists); keep the five extra morph slots opt-in (rejected: Clear / Copy / Paste and live edits now handle them); keep the straight-line span (rejected: fails three of 14 raw values); delete the engine walk (rejected: `--legacy-arp` needs it and Zig zag uses it).
- **Impact:** `render_opts.rs`, `render_opts_arp.rs`, `synth/{mod,arp,arp_pool,arp_gate,perf}.rs`, `synth_params_arp.rs`, `synth_morph.rs`, `engine/{mod,layer_init,morph_clip,snapshot}.rs`, `nord-tools` (`sweep_flags`, `program_sweep`, `arp_trace`), `tools/sweep_flip_compare.py`, GUI `firmware_panel/{info,bar,presets}.rs`, `docs/gui-testing.md`, data `docs/research/data/arp-fw-range.json` and `arp-morph-flip-sweep*.csv`, `arp-fw-vs-native.md` s7, `morph-fw-vs-native.md`, ROADMAP 7b.6, OQ30 / OQ31 / OQ32 / OQ39, synth-engine spec, audio-remaining rows 19 and 22, firmware-flag-audit, beans ns4-hwe8 (reopened) and ns4-li8h. **`nord-plugin` has no opt-out:** it builds `Stage4Engine::new`, so the plugin now plays the firmware walk and the firmware morph law with no switch (the gate stays off). Supersedes the "opt-in" parts of D159 and D162 for the walk and the morph.

### D159. Morph: the ARM weights Wheel, Aftertouch and Pedal by `v / 128`, clamps the sum and the cutoff sender drops a step from frac 0.5; an opt-in `--firmware-morph` reproduces both and plays five more synth targets

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17; default flipped by D166 (2026-11-18)
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session, bean `ns4-kq1o`; evidence [morph-fw-vs-native](research/morph-fw-vs-native.md), data `docs/research/data/morph-fw-*.csv`). D159 chosen: highest on origin/main was D156 at the last check.
- **Context:** The owner hears A:16 `Horizon Whl` far from the hardware clip; no earlier check compared the morph system with the firmware. Native weight is `v / 127`; five synth targets (Osc Ctrl, resonance, LFO rate, LFO amount, filter env amount) had decoded deltas and no engine slot.
- **Decision:** (1) Firmware law, VERIFIED by ARM run on 245 series (three MIDI sources, 39 morph ids): cells `[base, wheel, aftertouch, pedal]` in edit steps, weight `v / 128` with 127 giving 1.0, sum clamped on the total to the id range, no slew. Fit residual 0.0001 steps; the native `v / 127` law leaves up to 0.78 steps (depth 99). (2) The cutoff sender `0xC01C56CC` sends `(x - 52) << 24` minus one whole step once frac(x) >= 0.5 (words measured on Horizon layers A and C): up to 0.5 dB of cutoff difference at those wheel positions. (3) Opt-in `--firmware-morph` (`RenderOpts.firmware_morph`, `Stage4Engine::firmware_morph`) applies `v / 128` to all targets, the sender step drop to cutoff, and plays the five synth slots (`synth_morph.rs`). Default unchanged. (4) Horizon Whl: at wheel 0 every morph target equals its base in both; with the wheel up the native default misses the synth B filter-env-amount morph (+23 steps) and differs by up to 0.5 dB of cutoff. Morph does not explain a gap at rest.
- **Why:** The numbers come from the firmware's own dispatch and DSP words. Per D136 a firmware-validated path becomes default only if no hardware clip gets worse; no clip with wheel, aftertouch or pedal has been compared yet, so it stays opt-in.
- **Alternatives:** Flip `v / 128` to default now (rejected: no clip evidence, owner decides); leave the five slots unplayed (rejected: cheap to play, measured law).
- **Impact:** `morph.rs` (`firmware_weighted`), `synth_morph.rs`, `synth_params.rs` (`set_osc_ctrl`), `engine/{mod,mix,preset_load,layer_init,sound_init,snapshot,handoff}.rs`, `render_opts.rs`, GUI preset copy, `tools/arm_program_morph.py`, `tools/morph_law_report.py`, `tools/morph_test_program.py`, data under `docs/research/data/`. Not covered: organ drawbar morph, delay rate, Leslie, arp morph, physical wheel and pedal inputs (ASSUMED the same), SHARC oracle rerun. OQ30 and OQ31 updated.

### D154. SAMPLE oracle gaps: the 24 dB level gap is a harness omission, the 4 MiB member cap hid the layers, the per-loop poke is replaced by `credit=on`

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [sample-gaps](research/sample-gaps.md)). D154 chosen: D150 is #506, D151 is #507, D152 is #508, D153 is #509.
- **Context:** D150 measured oracle SAMPLE audio 20 to 23 dB under native, lost layers in 74 programs, never voiced 18 and voiced 51 only at other keys, and used a per-loop poke, prepare first and a 96-frame refill-ahead.
- **Decision:** (1) Level: the voice stage gap is 24.0 to 24.3 dB on 3 programs against 2^-6 / 0.25 = 24.08 dB; the oracle runs without the SY gain target, the core 2 receiver, the layer level and the program level, so the gap is a harness omission, not a native error (`smp_stages`, [sample-gaps](research/sample-gaps.md) s2). (2) Replace the per-loop `poke 0x92410 0x200` by `sharc-ref pcm ... credit=on` (bit-identical); `ahead=N` is an option (24 to 120 bit-identical); prepare first is the ARM order. (3) `arm_dsp_capture` serves a member up to `--member-bytes` (16 MiB for `sample_gaps_cap`); voice at key 60 for 110 of 119 programs (50 before), every scene I layer for 86; 6 programs are scene II only. (4) The Finger Bass octave was a key reading error: the ARM picks the zone root 49 (effective key 48), f0 130.7 against 131.1 Hz.
- **Why:** The per-stage tap and the changed member size are the only variables between the numbers. Nothing measured shows the native render wrong by a margin.
- **Alternatives:** Scale the native render to the oracle (rejected: the oracle lacks stages); keep the 4 MiB cap (rejected: it hid 60 programs).
- **Impact:** `smp_stages`, `smp_stream zones`, `sharc-ref pcm ahead= credit=`, `tools/sample_gaps_*.py`, `sample_stream_*` changes, data `sample-gaps-*.json`, docs; no engine, default, golden or `render_hash` change. Open (not found in the paths run): the third bound layer gets no voice (2 voices for 3 layers on 21 programs), 3 single layer programs without a key 60 voice, the SY receiver and gain target.

### D150. SAMPLE audio comes out of the SY oracle through a decoded-PCM ring feed (`pcm`); the real packed stream still desyncs, pitch entry `e` stays modelled

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [sample-stream-audio](research/sample-stream-audio.md)). D150 chosen: D144 to D149 are on main or in open PRs (#500, #502, #504, #505, #503).
- **Context:** D143 showed 110 of 119 SAMPLE programs voice; nothing measured the audio. D112 left the real stream stalling after the first block.
- **Decision:** (1) Add `smp_stream` (resolve, extract packed stream and decoded PCM per program, modelled `e`), the `sharc-ref` `pcm` command (decoded PCM into the source ring ahead of the read position) and `tools/sample_stream_*.py`. (2) Report oracle audio for 10 programs: 5 valid (prepare in the k60 window) are non-silent, f0 within about 5 cents of native for 4, Finger Bass about +2290 c by its f0 pair (octave not in `e`), 20 to 23 dB under native. (3) Coverage of 119: prepare at k60 for 50, only at other keys 51, none 18.
- **Why:** The SY resampler, step word and ring read run as firmware; only the unpacker is bypassed, so level, spectrum and duration of the resampled voice can be compared to native.
- **Alternatives:** Wait for the unpacker desync fix (blocks all measurement); synthesise audio natively only (does not touch the oracle).
- **Impact:** New tools and tests, docs only otherwise. No default, golden or `render_hash` changes; `--sample-pitch fw` stays opt-in. Pitch agreement is by construction of the modelled `e`.


### D151. The SHARC oracle census-diff open list is closed except the MR2F read-back

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** the user's task for this session (close the known instruction-level differences of the oracle, no default flips)
- **Context:** [sharc-census-diff](research/sharc-census-diff.md) listed 18 cases in 6 shapes where `crates/nord-sharc` differed from the CCES ADSP-21489 simulator on a firmware instruction. The census was rerun (49,722 cases, same seed) and reproduced exactly those 18 (plus 4 simulator `HALT` cases that agree on a rerun).
- **Decision:** Fix 5 shapes in the model, each measured on the simulator with `census_probe.py`: (1) the shift-immediate count of `LSHIFT`/`ASHIFT`/`ROT` and OR forms is 8-bit signed; (2) conditions `MV` (5) and `MS` (6) read ASTAT bits 7 and 6 of the element; (3) `Rx <-> Sy` writes `Rx` under PEx's condition and `Sy` under PEy's, no PEy exchange; (4) a long-word load into ASTATx/y or STKYx/y loads the named register twice; (5) a conditional load into a non-data register in SIMD runs when either element's condition holds. Leave `MR1F = R3, R1 = DM(..)` (MR2F read-back, 2 cases, 2 firmware occurrences) and `SF` (unused in firmware) as open and documented. 461 new regression cases are committed.
- **Why:** Every oracle-based audio conclusion rests on the core model. The 5 shapes cover 54 of 65,343 firmware instruction occurrences (15 sites in ORG/PNO shared code, FX and PNO). Old and new oracle give identical output on PNO bank/mix/post, PNO and ORG passes, so no conclusion changes.
- **Alternatives:** Fix only the most frequent shape (rejected: each fix is a few lines and a measured case set); model MR2F as zero-extension (rejected: it depends on the previous MR2F, the cause is unmeasured); run the 21571 census first (rejected: 4 h of machine time, kept as follow-up).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-sharc/src/core/{shifter,mod,moves,float_ops}.rs`, `census_regress.rs`, `docs/research/sharc-seq/{census_probe,census_freq}.py`, `census_extra_*.txt`, [sharc-census-diff](research/sharc-census-diff.md), [emulation-coverage](research/emulation-coverage.md), ROADMAP 7b.1. No engine, golden or default changes. Does not touch D118 or the multi-core scheduler PRs.

### D149. PUMP under the Master Clock reads a clock word through `c[-29]` (ARM run): the counter and the own phase are not the mechanism, the duck is on the beat

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [fx-mod-types](research/fx-mod-types.md) s7.2, `tools/fx_pump_clock_probe.py`)
- **Context:** OQ36 / D134: the engine drove the PUMP counter `c[-0x15]` once per division and set the increment `c[-32]` so the own phase wrapped once per division. The hardware writer of the counter was never found, and `N(v)` (D134 update 2) had no role in the model beyond the division length.
- **Decision:** (1) The routine computes `g = f(imul([c[-29]], c[-28]) + c[-18])` and reads the position through the pointer `c[-29]`. Free run: `c[-29]` is the address of the own phase `c[-31]`, `c[-28] = 1`. With `kFx1ClkEnable` = 1 the ARM handler of `kFx1RateClkDiv` rewrites **both**: `c[-29]` = `0xC0C70` (a word the FX core 1 pass copies each pass from `DM(0xC0230)`, the head of the SMP serial frame buffer, `pc 0x121400`) and `c[-28]` = `N(v)`; `c[-18]` and `c[-22]` keep their free-run words. The own phase, `c[-32]` and the counter `c[-0x15]` no longer reach the output, so the counter was never the beat mechanism (no ARM message writes `DM(0xC0230)`, neither in the boot log nor in 30,000 ticks: a DSP-side word). (2) The engine now does the same: under `mod1_sync_beats` it points `c[-29]` at a spare context word `c[0x2A]` that holds a 32 bit saw (`ClockWord`, wrap = `N` divisions, 0 at the clock start) and writes `c[-28]` = `N = round(32 / beats)`; the counter and the increment write are removed. (3) The saw wraps once per 32 beats (ASSUMED, it makes `N` = 32 one duck per beat) and is 0 at the clock start, so the duck falls on the beat; before it fell half a division after the start. The rate law is unchanged (`sync_beats`).
- **Why:** The ARM run shows the pointer and multiplier write; the oracle verified port shows `c[-29]` is the only read of the position. The rate is `N` ducks per wrap of the clock word, so a wrap of 32 beats gives 2.10 Hz for A:25 at 126 BPM (clip 2.098 Hz, FFT of the 5 ms envelope; the render 2.097 Hz). Same number as D134, now from the hardware mechanism.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the counter model (rejected: wrong mechanism, wrong alignment under a transport); model `DM(0xC0230)` from the SMP stream (rejected: the SMP DSP side is not run, a plain phase serves the engine).
- **Impact:** `fx/fw_pump_clock.rs` (`ClockWord`, `multiplier`), `fx/fw_mod1_rest.rs` (`set_clock_period((period, beats))`, tests in `fw_mod1_rest/sync_tests.rs`), `fx/modulation.rs`; `tools/fx_pump_clock_probe.py` (output in [fx-pump-clock-probe.txt](research/data/fx-pump-clock-probe.txt)), `tools/pump_envelope_hz.py`; OQ36 narrowed; no default changes.
- **Hardware clip, A:25 Sidechain Whl II** (clip 28 s at 594 s of the Part A video, Transkun notes, program tempo 126 BPM from the `.ns4p`; `python tools/emu_vs_hw.py ... --events-from transkun`, `score` = the gap score of `emu_vs_hw.py`, **lower is better**; levels, six band shape, centroid, width, attack, T60; one lossy YouTube clip, shape evidence only):

| Render | score | strongest 1.5..8 Hz envelope line (`tools/pump_envelope_hz.py`) |
|---|---|---|
| hardware clip | | **2.098 Hz** (bin spacing 0.036 Hz; 126 BPM beat = 2.100 Hz; the 2.11 Hz of earlier notes was a 10 ms RMS dB measure) |
| flag off (engine pump, default) | 5.90 | 2.097 Hz (strength 0.38 of the hardware's 1.00) |
| `--firmware-fx-ports mod1`, main (counter model, D134) | 5.01 | 2.097 Hz (0.31) |
| `--firmware-fx-ports mod1`, this change (clock word) | 5.33 | 2.097 Hz (0.39) |

The rate matches in all three; the score of the clock word is better than flag off (5.90) but **0.32 worse than the counter model (5.01)**. The difference is the duck phase against the clip (the counter model ducks half a division after the start, the clock word on the beat), which depends on the unknown relation between the clip start and the hardware's clock word zero: ASSUMED zero point, not evidence for or against either. The flip (#491) is judged against flag off, where no clip gets worse.
### D145. The firmware multi-tap reverb equals the oracle over its whole tail and adds no gain; the by-ear gap is level, tail and colour, not a port bug; the +21 / +26 / +36 dB excess is consistent with the packed-word noise floor

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [reverb-fw-vs-hw](research/reverb-fw-vs-hw.md))
- **Context:** The owner hears `--firmware-reverb` (A:21 White Grand, Cathedral, knob 3) as worse than the engine reverb against the hardware video; a firmware Cathedral pad render read sub +21, brilliance +26 and air +36 dB over dry, which looked like a gain or feedback bug. Types 2..11 were only checked against the oracle over 3,600 calls, which ends before the Hall and Cathedral tails start (first output at call 3,025); D133 found a CHOR wrap bug that its short golden window had missed.
- **Decision:** (1) `FwReverbMulti` is checked against the oracle over 30,000 calls for all nine types (impulse and noise), word for word (`tests_fw_rvbm_long.rs`, `fx-rvbmulti-golden-long.csv`). (2) The routine is linear and has no gain above unity (wet-only noise gain -4.6 to -7.7 dB below 60 Hz, -34 to -38 dB at 8 to 16 kHz); the excess figures are consistent with the noise floor of the packed 16-bit delay words (a pure tone leaves -51 / -61 / -70 dB re signal in sub / brilliance / air at mix 1, Room -37 dB in sub) read against a spectrally empty dry pad; the pad render was not reproduced, so this is a hypothesis. The parity test covers the E40 port only, noise at amount 63 only, and the CSV is regenerated locally (firmware image needed). (3) The audible gap is measured per type with `fx_reverb_types`: at knob 3 the firmware wet is 15 dB below the engine wet, Hall and Cathedral share one 1.2 s tail with 180 to 252 ms first echo (engine 3.4 s and 7.9 s, 60 to 77 ms), the spectrum is dark. (4) No engine change, `FirmwareFx::reverb` stays opt-in (D22, D136).
- **Why:** The port is correct against the oracle and the oracle is the firmware routine, so the gap is in the data around it (type words of the synth unit instance, the reverb stage input gain, the second module routine `0x1211C1`) or in the engine's ASSUMED tails, not in a transliteration error.
- **Alternatives:** Scale the firmware wet or lengthen its tail to match the FDN (rejected: no hardware measurement, would hide the cause); flip the default (rejected: owner verdict against it); keep the 3,600 call window only (rejected: it cannot see tails).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-core/src/fx/tests_fw_rvbm_long.rs`, `tests_fw_rvbm_levels.rs`, `tests_fw_rvbm_long_data.rs`, `crates/nord-tools/src/reverb_measure.rs`, `bin/fx_reverb_types.rs`, `docs/research/reverb-fw-vs-hw.md`, `docs/research/data/fx-rvbmulti-golden-long.csv`; OQ21c, ROADMAP 7b.4, [firmware-flag-audit](research/firmware-flag-audit.md) updated; no audio change. Open: the stage gain word and the second routine of the reverb module in the real dispatcher, a hardware recording with a clean release.

### D157. The firmware reverb stage is a dry-wet cross-fade with no gain above unity, kRvbAmt writes only the send word, the core runs at 21,701 Hz, and the excess is a signal-relative floor

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [reverb-stage-gain](research/reverb-stage-gain.md))
- **Context:** D145 left four items open for the by-ear gap of `--firmware-reverb` (wet 15 dB low at knob 3, tail 1.2 s, dark): the stage gain word, the second routine `0x1211C1`, the 43,402 Hz time scale and whether the +21 / +26 / +36 dB excess is a gain.
- **Decision:** (1) The dispatcher stage law is recorded and verified in the oracle: `c = clip(L + s, lim)`, `g = c + lim`, stage input `g * bus`, new bus `g * y + (lim - c) * bus`; at the image limit `0.5` a stage never exceeds unity (`tools/fx_stage_law.py`, `data/fx-stage-law.csv`, max error 3e-5). (2) `kRvbAmt` writes exactly the send and dry words (`c[-31]`, `c[-30]`; Spring `c[18]`, `c[19]`); the module reads the send only (smoothed into `c[-29]`), no instruction reads `c[-30]`, the wrapper's own dry `c[-28]` is 0 and never written, `c[42]` / `c[43]` are boot-written 1.0 switches; no reverb edit and none of 6,264 boot / settle FX messages writes a stage record word or installs `0x120FDB` / `0x1211C1`. The routine output is wet only; the dry is the stage's `(lim - c) * bus` term. (3) `0x1211C1` is the 2-frame, filter-free variant of the same core reading `{-28, 42, 43}`; role and trigger open. (4) The core runs at half the frame rate, 21,701.17 Hz (fs/4 input low-pass, frames 0 and 2); the real tail is 1.32 s, not 1.19 s. (5) The excess is a signal-relative out-of-band floor (-55.75 dB re tone at two levels, 0.1 dB apart) read against an empty dry band, not a gain. (6) No engine change, no default change.
- **Why:** Every number is a run against the oracle or the ARM harness or a listing scan with a committed table and a test; the missing piece (who writes the stage level) cannot be invented from the harness, which never reaches the install path.
- **Alternatives:** Scale the firmware wet or set `g` from the knob (rejected: the real writer is unknown, would hide the cause); port `0x1211C1` (rejected: no install path to tell when it runs); apply the 43,402 Hz scale to the reverb now (rejected: the block timing depends on the same install path, listed as follow-up).
- **Impact:** `tools/fx_stage_law.py`, `tools/fx_rvbm_ctx_access.py`, `tools/fx_rvb_stage_capture.py` and their tests, `crates/nord-core/src/fx/tests_fw_rvbm_floor.rs`, `docs/research/reverb-stage-gain.md`, `docs/research/data/fx-stage-law.csv`, `fx-rvbm-ctx-access.csv`, `fx-rvb-stage-capture.csv`; OQ21c, ROADMAP 7b.4, [reverb-fw-vs-hw](research/reverb-fw-vs-hw.md) updated; no audio change.

### D152. PUMP clock word: `DM(0xC0230)` is not in the SMP frame, no ARM or SMP writer found in the paths run; span and zero point stay ASSUMED

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [pump-dsp-source](research/pump-dsp-source.md))
- **Context:** OQ36 / D149: the PUMP reads `DM(0xC0C70)`, a copy of `DM(0xC0230)`; D149 called it the head of the SMP serial frame and assumed a 32 beat span and a zero at the clock start.
- **Decision:** (1) Record that `0xC0230` lies 4 words before SMP frame A (`0xC0234`), inside the FX strip filled by three 2D receive DMA blocks. (2) Record the negatives: no SMP image store to ring words 4 to 9, no tempo word from the ARM to any DSP in a 30,000 tick A:25 run (not found in the paths run). (3) Keep span and zero point ASSUMED; add two engine tests pinning the native duck rate at 60 / 90 / 126 / 150 BPM and the duck on the clock start. No engine change, no default change, PR #491 untouched.
- **Why:** The writer was not found, so a firmware-derived span does not exist; claiming one would be a guess.
- **Alternatives:** Derive the span from 2.10 Hz (rejected: circular). Flip the default (rejected: open writer, one clip).
- **Impact:** [pump-dsp-source](research/pump-dsp-source.md), [fx-mod-types](research/fx-mod-types.md) s7.2, OQ36, ROADMAP 7b, `fx/tests_fw_pump_sync.rs`.

### D160. Program ranking: native default is ranked against the oracle-verified firmware paths, not against chain audio; FX groups move the worst 20 (Mod 2 first), the chain has no piano tap

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-17
- **Decided by:** pending the user (task from the parent session; evidence [program-ranking](research/program-ranking.md))
- **Context:** The native engine had been compared with the firmware chain on a handful of programs. The live ARM + SHARC link has one audio-like tap per engine (PNO core 1 lane for the organ, PNO core 2 voice window for the synth, SY ring for SAMPLE) and none for the piano, FX or master, so a per-program level and spectrum comparison of all 384 programs against the chain does not exist.
- **Decision:** (1) Rank the 384 programs by the distance of the native default render to the native render with every oracle-verified firmware option (`--firmware-all --firmware-routing`), key 60, velocity 100: `abs(level delta dB) + band share RMS dB + 6 x abs(centroid octaves)` (weights ASSUMED). (2) Record the chain census next to it as flags (13 synth and 5 organ programs the chain does not voice at key 60, 24 SAMPLE programs that lose a layer, 3 with no voice, 155 piano programs without a tap). (3) Attribute the worst 20 by single-group ablation: only FX groups reduce their score (best single carrier: Mod 2 12, Mod 1 3, delay 2, EQ, reverb, compressor 1 each); for 6 of the 20 the best single group leaves more than 5 (pairs not run). (4) Tools `program_rank` (Rust), `program_rank_chain.py`, `program_rank.py`; shared sweep loading moved to `nord_tools::sweep_load`. No default flips, no engine change.
- **Why:** A score against a tap that exists for one engine would rank engines by tap availability, not by error. The verified firmware options are the closest reference every program can be rendered against; the ablation shows where the default differs from it.
- **Alternatives:** Chain audio against native per program (rejected: no piano, FX or master tap, the SY ring peak is the stand-in sample, 0.05394 on every voiced run); rank by hardware clips (rejected: 22 segments only); score by the level alone (rejected: 18 of the worst 20 are band shape).
- **Impact:** [program-ranking](research/program-ranking.md), `docs/research/data/program-ranking.csv`, `program-ranking-ablation.csv`, ROADMAP 7b, OQ40, `crates/nord-tools/src/bin/program_rank.rs`, `rank_metrics.rs`, `sweep_load.rs`, `tools/program_rank*.py`.


### D162. A:16 arpeggiator: the ARM step time equals the native one, the gate (2/3), the single-note retrigger and the UP/DOWN octave walk differ; opt-in `arp_firmware_walk`

- **Status:** proposed; walk and retrigger became the default in D166 (2026-11-18), the gate stays opt-in
- **Decided by:** the task of the parent session (evidence [arp-fw-vs-native](research/arp-fw-vs-native.md)); owner to confirm.
- **Context:** The owner hears factory A:16 Horizon Whl far from the Part A video. Layers A and C run the arpeggiator (rate raw 90 / 96, file BPM 266 / 318, tempo 100, MST CLK on); D81 never compared the arp.
- **Decision:** (1) Capture the ARM note events of the layers with `tools/arm_arp_events.py` (listener `0xC0123A18`, voice lists, 24 kHz ticks) and compare with the new `arp_trace` bin (`synth/voice_edges.rs`) through `tools/arp_fw_compare.py`. (2) Step time: ARM, native and video agree at 150.0 ms (1/4 beat at 100 BPM; the file BPM and the dial table are unused under MST CLK). The ARM step is an input of the capture (the SPI Master Clock position is fed, the scale `2^19` per beat is ASSUMED). (3) Differences: gate 100 ms (2/3 step) against the full step, a single held note retriggers every step in the ARM and not in the native engine, UpDown over 2 octaves walks 7 pool notes (top root included) in the ARM and 60 64 67 76 72 76 in the native engine. (4) New opt-in `SynthLayer::arp_firmware_walk` (`arp_trace --arp-fw-walk`) reproduces the ARM UpDown cycle (EMPIRICAL, one layer). Gate and retrigger stay under OQ32; no default changes.
- **Why:** The video step equals the engine step, so the audible gap is the gate, the retrigger and the octave order, not the rate law. The walk is cheap to reproduce and testable; the gate needs the OQ32 decision.
- **Alternatives:** Flip the UpDown walk by default (rejected: one layer of evidence, owner decides); change the gate in the same PR (rejected: OQ32 open, the 1/2 gate of `E` steps interacts with the pattern player); wire the flag into `FirmwareOptions` now (rejected: 17 struct literals, left as a follow-up).
- **Impact:** `synth/arp.rs`, `synth/mod.rs` (field), `synth/voice_edges.rs`, `nord-tools` bin `arp_trace`, `tools/arm_arp_events.py`, `arp_fw_compare.py`, `arp_video_onsets.py`, data `docs/research/data/arp-*.json`, OQ32 and OQ39, ROADMAP 7b.6. No golden, `render_hash` or default changes.

### D163. FX parity: Mod 2 plus EQ explain the worst programs; the firmware Mod 2 becomes the default (`--legacy-mod2` opts out), `FirmwareFx::PARITY` opt-in takes the 384-program score from mean 5.35 to 1.66

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-18 for the Mod 2 default flip; proposed for the rest of `PARITY`
- **Decided by:** the owner for the Mod 2 flip (approval relayed by the coordinating session in chat 2026-11-18: flip where the native / firmware path already matches the oracle chain, null dB, before/after ranked distance and `render_hash` shown, opt-out flag kept; same relay pattern as D142); pending the owner for the other groups
- **Context:** D160 ranked the 384 programs by the distance of the engine default to the firmware-derived paths and found that only FX groups move the worst 20, but ran single-group reverts only (6 programs stayed above 5). D158 showed every FX port that runs on the chip nulls the oracle, and that the engine paths differ.
- **Decision:** (1) Ablate every combination of the six FX groups (63 renders per program, `program_rank --ablate-fx`) for the worst 20: Mod 2 is in the smallest explaining set (score left at most 2) of 14 programs, EQ 10, compressor 7, Mod 1 6, delay 2, reverb 2; 7 programs need one group, 7 a pair (partner of Mod 2: EQ, 8 programs), 6 three or more; reverting all six leaves 0.00 in all 20. (2) Add `FirmwareFx::PARITY` (every port but `reverb` and `reverb_mix`, with `amp`) and `--firmware-fx-ports parity`. **Opt-in, not the default.** (3) Measure the before and after of a default flip (`program_rank --flip`): PARITY takes the worst 20 from mean 18.14 to 2.19 and all 384 programs from mean 5.35 to 1.66 (median 3.92 to 1.04, p90 12.72 to 3.84, max 22.15 to 16.61; above 5: 162 to 22; above 10: 61 to 3; 251 programs improve by more than 0.5, 6 rise by more than 0.5, largest +1.87). (4) Test `nord_tools::fx_parity`: the PARITY chain nulls against the single-port firmware model for every Mod 1, Mod 2, EQ and compressor case (-314 dB or better, compressor -86 dB) while the engine path does not (Mod 1 -10.7 to +3.7 dB, Mod 2 -7.3 to +3.1 dB at amounts 64 and 127). (5) **Flip: `FirmwareFx::DEFAULT` = amp + Mod 2** (`FirmwareFx::LEGACY` = the old default, amp only). Mod 2 is the one family whose live FX-chip null covers all 12 types (level delta 0.00 dB, null -72 dB or better, [fx-vs-oracle](research/fx-vs-oracle.md) s3.1; laws on the dense ARM grid D90, D100). `--legacy-mod2` turns it off, `--legacy-all` and the GUI Classic preset restore the old FX default; a Mod 2 port runs only for programs that carry the raw `kFx2Type` / `kFx2Rate` words (decoded programs), so edits made by type in the GUI stay on the engine path. Test `nord_tools::fx_parity::the_default_mod2_nulls_against_the_firmware_port_and_legacy_does_not`: for all 36 Mod 2 cases the default chain nulls against the port (-120 dB or better) and the legacy chain does not (above -40 dB at amounts 64 and 127). Ranked distance against the all-firmware chain, flip of `fx_mod2` alone on the old default: 384 programs mean 5.35 to 3.78, median 3.92 to 2.76, p90 12.72 to 8.24, above 5 162 to 106, above 10 61 to 24; 111 programs improve by more than 0.5, 258 stay within 0.5, **15 get worse by more than 0.5** (Wah EP Ondre J 6.78 to 12.93, TapePiano AT 10.04 to 14.79, Stereo Wah Clav 10.37 to 15.07, Melody Pearls 5.40 to 9.43, Gravity Whl 2.92 to 5.79, Taped Wurly 0.81 to 3.54 and 9 more, listed in [program-ranking-fx](research/program-ranking-fx.md) s6: a firmware Mod 2 after an engine Mod 1, EQ or compressor sees a different signal than in the all-firmware chain, so they need the next flips); the worst 20 go from mean 18.14 to 9.67. `render_hash` is unchanged (`eb13063d...ca28`) but **not exercised by the hash scenario**: the golden FX sweep sets Mod 2 by type without the raw words, so it stays on the engine path and the hash cannot show the flip; the evidence is the ranking, the null test and the end-to-end test `engine::tests_mod2_default` (`apply_program` on the default engine: the chain equals the hand-wired firmware port and differs from `FirmwareFx::LEGACY`). Review fix: the firmware stage reads only the raw words, so a GUI edit of the Mod 2 type, rate or variation (`FxEdit::Mod2Type`, `FxFloat::Mod2Rate`, `FxFlag::Mod2Variation`, `AudioCommand::SetFxMod2`, the plugin host selectors) now drops them (`LayerFxChain::clear_mod2_firmware_words`) and the engine Mod 2 runs; the words travel with `FxParams` (undo, snapshots, Live slots, FX clipboard) and `copy_all_settings_from`; a Mod 2 rate morph moves the rate word. **The plugin runs the engine Mod 2**: `LayerFxSelectors::sync_to_engine` writes the host values every block and clears the firmware words each time, so a program loaded in the plugin plays its Mod 2 through the engine model, not the firmware stage. Cost (`ns4 bench --scenario worst-fw-chorus | worst-fw-spin`, release, this machine): worst case mean 1.20 ms with the engine Chorus, 1.58 ms with the firmware CHOR and 2.0 to 2.4 ms with SPIN on all 7 chains, about +0.05 and +0.11 to +0.17 ms per active chain and 128 frame block ([TECH_DEBT](TECH_DEBT.md) TD-14, TD-18). The 384-program sweep data (`docs/research/data/program-sweep.json`) is not regenerated. (6) **Not flipped, stays opt-in** (`--firmware-fx-ports`, `parity`): compressor (role EMPIRICAL, engine 1.7 to 10.9 dB lower), EQ (not run live in the oracle, joint mid words ASSUMED), Mod 1 (matches only at 4 frames per call, 2 by default, OQ38; PUMP clock OQ36), delay (`delay_laws` role EMPIRICAL, no oracle chain) and reverb (owner hears it worse by ear). The rest of `PARITY` takes the 384 mean to 1.66 and stays a proposal for the owner.
- **Why:** The score is the distance to the oracle-verified model, which is the evidence available without hardware, and the ranking shows that one switch applies it to 251 of 384 programs. The Mod 2 flip moves audio for the 158 Mod 2 programs without a hardware clip to confirm it (the chorus clip reads the delay 1.7 times shorter, scope calibration ASSUMED): the owner's rule for this PR is the oracle null and the ranked distance, and parity over purity is the standing preference. The groups without a full oracle null stay opt-in.
- **Alternatives:** Flip all of `PARITY` now (rejected: EQ and the Mod 1 rest types have no full oracle null). Recalibrate the engine Mod 2 and EQ models to the ports instead (rejected: a second approximation of a routine that exists word for word, with no oracle for the fit). Include the reverb ports (rejected: owner veto by ear, D136 point 3).
- **Impact:** [program-ranking-fx](research/program-ranking-fx.md), `docs/research/data/program-ranking-fx-combos.csv`, `program-ranking-fx-flips-worst20.csv`, `program-ranking-fx-flips-384.csv`, `crates/nord-core/src/{render_opts,render_opts_ports_tests}.rs`, `fx/{firmware_fx,firmware_fx_ports}.rs` (DEFAULT, LEGACY, PARITY, `parity` keyword), `crates/nord-tools/src/{rank_groups,fx_parity}.rs`, `bin/{program_rank,fx_parity_null}.rs`, `tools/program_rank_combo.py`, [effects-and-routing](specs/effects-and-routing.md) s4.0c, ROADMAP 7b.4, OQ38, bean `ns4-agyp`. Default changed: Mod 2 (`FirmwareFx::DEFAULT`, `LEGACY`, `--legacy-mod2`, `apply_to`, GUI info text and Classic preset, edit paths, `FxParams`, tests); `render_hash` unchanged but not exercised by the hash scenario (it sets Mod 2 by type, without the raw words); bench scenarios `worst-fw-chorus`, `worst-fw-spin`; TD-14 and TD-18.

### D164. Silent chain programs (OQ40): 17 of 18 are harness artefacts or legitimate silence; the chain census compares a silent tap with the firmware expectation, the Router forwards L2 wavetable stores; native unchanged

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-18
- **Decided by:** the task of the parent session (evidence [silent-programs](research/silent-programs.md), [silent-programs.csv](research/data/silent-programs.csv)); owner to confirm.
- **Context:** [D160](DECISIONS.md) flagged 13 synth and 5 organ programs whose chain tap stays 0 at key 60 (1,500 ticks held), while native plays them. The open question was whether native is also wrong.
- **Decision:** (1) The 5 organ programs are legitimate silences: zone ends below key 60 (Wormhole Organ, Nox Split) or stored level 0 without morph (Ballad Layer Whl, J3 Grand Layers, and 24 Frames J3, which has the zone and the level 0; corrected in D170). (2) 10 synth programs (7 WAVETABLE, 3 SUPER 2..4) were silent because the ARM writes the per-program wavetable to the L2 byte window and `Message.core` files it under core 1 with a negative word, which `arm_dsp_route.Router` dropped; it now forwards those stores to PNO core 2 (`Router.forward_l2`, `Link.poke`). (3) Arp programs are held 15,000 ticks (`program_rank_chain.py`, `ARP_KEY_TICKS`); the first arp step reaches the DSP about 2,900 ticks after the key. (4) `tools/program_expect.py` computes the firmware expectation (scene mask with `default_layer_a`, zone gain, level, arp) and `program_rank.py --expect` flags `*_expected_silent` instead of `*_chain_silent`. (5) Synth Engine (arp mode 1 zigzag, custom 14 step pattern) stays silent in the chain at holds up to 60,000 ticks: open. No native code, default or option changed.
- **Why:** The census had no notion of what the firmware itself sounds, and its harness lost the wavetable of every program that uploads one. With both fixed the chain voices 17 of 18 (5 as expected silences), so the native engine is the one that agrees with the firmware at key 60 and the ranking in D160 stands.
- **Alternatives:** Treat the 18 as native bugs (rejected: native plays only what the zone and level allow, and the chain voices once the harness is fixed); poke the 985-word boot table (rejected: did not voice SUPER or WAVETABLE programs); lengthen the hold for every program (rejected: arp programs only, the census costs CPU per tick).
- **Impact:** [silent-programs](research/silent-programs.md), `tools/arm_dsp_route.py`, `tools/arm_dsp_link.py`, `tools/arm_dsp_live.py`, `tools/program_expect.py`, `tools/program_rank.py`, `tools/program_rank_chain.py`, OQ40 (narrowed to Synth Engine and the piano tap), ROADMAP 7b. The 384-program census must be rerun before the chain columns of `program-ranking.csv` are read for wavetable programs.

### D165. The program Touch bits equal the firmware recall for all 384 factory programs; the piano Touch curves are the default, `--legacy-piano-touch` opts out

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-18
- **Decided by:** the owner, approval in chat 2026-11-18 (relayed by the coordinating session): flip if the recall shows N of N, keep `--legacy-piano-touch`, record `render_hash` and program-sweep evidence; standing preference parity over purity.
- **Context:** D88 and D99 kept `--firmware-piano-touch` off because the per-program Touch bits were verified only by single-bit flips of one program and D117 added two recalled programs (Heavy, Light). The listening test of 2026-11-15 showed that key force changes the piano sound a lot; 176 factory programs use a Touch (bean `ns4-yp85`, audio-remaining row 4).
- **Decision:** (1) `tools/arm_program_touch_bits.py` recalls every factory `.ns4p` through the firmware (Store record of the EEPROM image, full boot, no poke), reads `Edt_Pno_kKbdTouch` (`0x159`) of both piano layer stores and the `curveId` the handler `0xC011AA38` stores, and compares them with the decoder values of `piano_touch_census --csv`: **384 of 384 programs equal** (768 of 768 layer values: 332 Heavy, 414 Medium, 22 Light; every (A, B) combination of the factory set seen; `curveId` 10 / 11 / 13 as expected). Data: `docs/research/data/piano-touch-program.csv`, `piano-touch-recall.json`; the unit test `test_committed_recall_equals_the_decoder_census_for_every_factory_program` pins the N of N in CI. (2) `PianoLayer::firmware_touch` and `FirmwareOptions::piano_touch` are on by default (`PianoLayer::new`, `default()`, `firmware_all()`); `--legacy-piano-touch` and `--legacy-all` turn them off (Medium for every `.npno` note, ASSUMED powers for the other sounds, the pre-D165 behaviour bit for bit); `--firmware-piano-touch` stays accepted; the summary prints `legacy-piano-touch` when off; the GUI Firmware bar switch `Touch curves` is on. (3) `program_sweep` gains `--piano-touch` / `--legacy-piano-touch` to print the sweep before and after the flip with one build.
- **Why:** The chain program file bits, decoder, Touch handler, `curveId`, `T`, stroke, `X`, Q23 level is verified for every factory program (bits, 384 of 384) and for a Heavy and a Light program end to end (D117, 18 of 18 rows, max error 0). The sweep of the 82 factory programs with an active non-Medium Touch (velocity 100, notes C3 G3 E4 C5, [piano-touch-sweep.csv](research/data/piano-touch-sweep.csv)) renders without NaN, Inf or silence; RMS moves -1.59 / -0.46 (median) / +1.23 dB, Heavy quieter and Light louder as predicted; default-mode renders with clipped samples fall from 8 to 3, but the total clipped samples rise from 224 to 534 (Bubbles Mk I 184 to 518, DistantGrand 2 to 14, both Light at +0.70 dB; six Heavy programs drop to 0 or 2). Every active Heavy or Light layer of those programs resolves to a loaded `.npno` sound; whether each such sound carries a firmware level model is checked for layer A only (D117 census, 83 of 83), so the ASSUMED `touch_fallback_amp` is not excluded for a layer B sound. D22 asks for verified mappings; the owner accepts the stated gaps.
- **Alternatives:** Keep it opt-in until a Heavy and a Light hardware clip exists (the rule of D136 and D117; rejected by the owner, parity over purity). Flip only for `.npno` sounds (rejected in D117: mixes paths; moot here because no factory program with a non-Medium Touch uses another sound). Flip and re-baseline `render_hash` (not needed: the hash segments are Medium). Wire the program Touch into the plugin in the same PR (rejected: out of scope, see Impact).
- **Impact:** `piano.rs` (default), `piano/touch_layer.rs`, `render_opts.rs` (default, `--legacy-piano-touch`, summary, tests `piano_touch_is_the_default_and_legacy_opts_out`), GUI `info.rs` (switch now `--legacy-piano-touch`) and `docs/gui-testing.md`, `program_sweep.rs` flags, `piano_touch_census --csv`, `tools/arm_program_touch_bits.py` (+ test), research [piano-touch-curves](research/piano-touch-curves.md) s4b, [audio-remaining](research/audio-remaining.md) row 4, [firmware-flag-audit](research/firmware-flag-audit.md), [emulation-coverage](research/emulation-coverage.md), [piano-engine](specs/piano-engine.md), ROADMAP 7b.5, OQ20, bean `ns4-yp85`. `render_hash` `eb13063d4fca837a613670168a005b564c7f235a1578136d03955fa968d2ca28` before and after (unchanged), goldens unchanged. Every default render of a program with a Heavy or Light piano layer changes (about 82 of 384 factory programs are active, 176 carry a stored Touch on at least one layer). The plugin is not affected: `nord-plugin` `Params::sync_to_engine` overwrites both layers with the global `piano_touch` parameter (default Medium), so a program's own Touch does not reach it. Still open: no Heavy or Light hardware clip (the flip cannot be measured against hardware), the System-menu MIDI velocity curves (`Edt_Sys_kMIDIVelCurve`, not modelled), the ASSUMED fallback gain for sounds without a level model, a level model check of layer B sounds.

### D169. Plugin: a loaded program's piano Touch and firmware Mod 2 survive the block sync until the host moves the matching parameter; the plugin gets `fwx_*` switches for the firmware-law defaults

- **Status:** accepted 2026-11-19
- **Decided by:** the coordinating session, 2026-11-19, as the fix for the review findings of PRs #520, #521 and #523 (owner standing preference: parity over purity, no regression of old sessions). Open for the owner to revise.
- **Context:** `Stage4Params::sync_to_engine` runs every `process()` block and wrote the single global `piano_touch` (default Medium) over both piano layers and cleared the firmware Mod 2 raw words (`mod2_type_raw`, `mod2_rate_raw`) of every layer through `fx_selectors::apply_to`. D163 (firmware Mod 2 default) and D165 (per-layer program Touch) therefore never reached the plugin for a program applied to its engine, and the host's untouched defaults erased what the program set. D166 (arp walk, morph law) reached the plugin silently as engine defaults, without a host switch to opt out.
- **Decision:** (1) `ProgramOverrides` (`program_overrides.rs`, lock-free atomics in `Stage4Params`) tracks four control groups: `piano_touch`, and per layer `fxsel_mod2_type_n` + `fxsel_mod2_rate_n` + `fxy_mod2_var_n` + `fxx_mod2_amount_n`. A group overrides the program only after the host changed one of its values from the value seen at the last program load (before any load: from the parameter default); until then `sync_to_engine` leaves the engine value, so the per-layer program Touch and the firmware Mod 2 words play. Once armed a group stays armed (an undo back to the default is still the host's choice) until the next program load, which re-baselines all groups. (2) `NordStage4Plugin::load_program` / `load_program_bytes` apply a program, re-baseline, and keep it so `initialize` and `reset` re-apply it on the rebuilt engine. (3) New host parameters `fwx_arp_walk` (default on), `fwx_arp_gate` (on since D168; was off, opt-in per D166), `fwx_morph_law` (on), `fwx_touch_curves` (on) write the engine flags every block; defaults equal the engine defaults. (4) No parameter id was renamed or removed; four ids were added (`param-ids.snapshot`).
- **Why:** The loaded program must reach the sound, and a parameter the user or automation really moves must still win. Comparing against the value at load time needs no extra persisted state: a restored session sets its parameters before `initialize`, so every non-default restored value counts as a host change and renders as before; the old plugin had no program load path, so no old session holds a program to preserve. An untouched host without a program leaves the engine at its defaults, which equal the parameter defaults (tests `defaults_match_engine_defaults`, `an_untouched_host_without_a_program_equals_the_engine_defaults`), so it renders bit-identically. Atomics keep `sync_to_engine(&self)` and the audio thread free of locks and allocation.
- **Alternatives:** Per-parameter `is_modified` / smoothing state (rejected: nih-plug has none that distinguishes "moved since the load" and a restored default is indistinguishable from untouched). Always host-wins and ask users to avoid programs (rejected: that is the bug). Program-wins always (rejected: automation would stop working). Writing the program values into the host parameters (rejected: needs a host `ParamSetter` and changes automation lanes behind the user; `piano_touch` is also one parameter for two layers). Tracking all 800+ parameters (rejected: only these three have a loaded-program counterpart that the defaults erased; the rest stay host-wins, TD-21).
- **Impact:** `crates/nord-plugin`: `program_overrides.rs`, `firmware_params.rs`, `params.rs`, `fx_selectors.rs` (`apply_to` takes a flag), `fx_sharing_params.rs`, `lib.rs`; tests `crates/nord-plugin/tests/program_vs_host.rs` (fixture `tests/fixtures/old_state_v1.json`; 5 of 7 fail under host-always-wins), unit tests per module; [daw-plugin](specs/daw-plugin.md), [HOST_COMPATIBILITY](HOST_COMPATIBILITY.md), TD-21. Hosts that saved automation of `piano_touch` or the Mod 2 parameters see no change.

### D170. Chain census rerun: 12 synth programs voiced, 5 organ expected silent, Synth Engine is an ARM bank bit, no piano tap yet

- **Status:** proposed 2026-11-19
- **Decided by:** the task of the parent session (evidence [silent-programs](research/silent-programs.md) s6 and s7, [piano-chain-tap](research/piano-chain-tap.md)); owner to confirm. D170 chosen: D167 is #525, D168 is #531, D169 is #529.
- **Context:** [D164](DECISIONS.md) left follow-ups: rerun the 384-program census with the L2 forwarding, review items (provenance, controls), Synth Engine (OQ40), a piano tap.
- **Decision:** (1) Census rerun on all 384 programs: 12 synth programs flagged `synth_chain_silent` now voice, 5 organ programs read `organ_expected_silent`, 13 more window peaks changed (7 above 1 dB, largest +19.7 dB Night Gaunts Whl), other flags unchanged; the CSV scoring columns are byte-identical to `main`, only chain columns changed. (2) `program_rank_chain.py` retries failed census entries and missing return-path runs, reads `--audio-for` with `splitlines()`, has `--entries`; `program_rank.py` `by_engine` uses `statistics.median` (it returned the upper median); `arm_dsp_live.py` is split (`arm_dsp_live_parts.py`) and reports per-phase ARM message counts. (3) Review fixes in silent-programs: tags (VERIFIED only for the committed `program_expect.py` expectation, chain runs EMPIRICAL), 24 Frames J3 is silent by zone and by stored level 0, only Wormhole Organ and Nox Split have a key 40 positive control, Reproduce block fixed. (4) Synth Engine: the firmware player plays note 60 every 194.7 ms like native (the 2/3 gate stays opt-in), but the ARM key word carries voice bank bit `0x01` only; with both bits the DSP voice sounds (peak 0.194). Native unchanged; why the ARM omits the bit and whether hardware sounds it stay open (OQ40, `tools/arm_key_words.py`). (5) Piano tap: not feasible with the harness: a piano key sends no SMP message (12 of 12 sampled programs, Astoria Grand in detail), no sound object is bound, `sharc-live` has no SMP preset, page DMA and the FX hand-over are not modelled; recorded as OQ41 and bean ns4-pnoc. No default flipped.
- **Why:** A flag that the harness raises must be re-checked after the harness is fixed, and a piano column built without a voice would rank tap availability, not error.
- **Alternatives:** Poke the second bank bit in the census (rejected: hides the ARM behaviour); a placeholder piano tap on PNO core 1 (rejected: the lane is the organ lane); run all 155 piano programs (rejected: the sample already shows the cause).
- **Impact:** [silent-programs](research/silent-programs.md), [program-ranking](research/program-ranking.md), `docs/research/data/program-ranking.csv`, `silent-programs.csv`, `tools/program_rank*.py`, `tools/arm_dsp_live*.py`, `tools/arm_key_words.py`, OQ40, OQ41, ROADMAP 7b, beans ns4-pnoc and ns4-syng.
